- **Not verified on hardware**: the in-stream photo's latency, whether a press of "Add to my list" now shows a picture at once, and which of the paths above, if any, was the one that lost listening on 2026-10-25.

## Every add is a sighting (2026-10-10, user's choice after the third field run)

- Spec user story 33 (one sighting per species and run, a repeat add updating it) is dropped: the birder wants to add the same bird as often as they like. `RunSightings` writes a new sighting on every add, from the lens or the phone, each with its own time, place, confidence and photo; undo deletes the sighting that add wrote and nothing else. The ledger keeps one entry per sighting, so "N added" on the Listen tab counts sightings, and `count(of:)` gives a species' sightings this run. The update path (`SightingRecorder.update`/`restore`, the replaced frames kept for the undo window) is gone.
- **Lens**: the state machine counts saves per stack index (`savedCounts`). A saved species' details page shows "Added to my list ✓" (or "Added N times ✓" from the second) and an **Add another** button, which opens the viewfinder like the first add. _Since #51 the line names the card the add earned instead: "Your first House Finch" or "New card" (see "Every sighting is a card")._
- **Phone**: the review sheet of an added species reads "Added to your journal · N sightings" and offers **Add another sighting**; the acknowledgment reads "Added to your journal · N sightings this run" from the second. _Since #51 an add is acknowledged as a card: the Legendary reveal for a first, else "A new card for your collection"._

## Every sighting is a card (issue #51) — built 2026-10-14, hardware check pending
The first slice of "Collection and circles": local cards with no backend.
- **Album schema 3** (`AlbumSchemaV3`) adds every field the collection needs, so later tickets fill them in without another migration:
  - a stable `identifier` (UUID)
  - the `method` (Heard, the default; Seen comes later, on the phone)
  - the `rarity` snapshot (occurrence, label, calibration version, week)
  - the `caption` (text and generator version)
  - the art state
  - `attachedPhotoPaths`
  
  The art state is stored as JSON (`artState`, read through `art: CardArt`) so the art pipeline can grow without a schema version. The stage from schema 2 is a custom one: a column default would give every migrated sighting the same identifier, so `didMigrate` assigns each its own and sets Heard. Tested both ways (a schema 2 store and the first schema's store, through both stages); identifiers survive a reopen. A removal undone in the journal comes back with the same identifier and card fields, and the files a sighting refers to (frame, attached photos, painted art; `Sighting.filePaths`) go with it.
- **The collection** (`CardCollection`, Album) is pure over value snapshots (identifier, species, confirmation time):
  - Legendary is the earliest surviving sighting of a species, ties broken by the identifier's string.
  - Deleting it passes Legendary on, and deleting the last card removes the species, because nothing is stored.
  - Counts and the Cards summary ("5 cards · 4 species").
  
  Sets, collector numbers and editions wait for pack schema 4.
- **Recording an add**: `SightingRecorder.record` writes the identifier and Heard and reports whether the add is Legendary: no other surviving sighting of the species in the album at that moment (`CardCollection.addMakesLegendary`). `RunSightings.Addition` carries both to the lens and the phone. The rarity snapshot, captions, art jobs and finds come with their tickets.
- **Phone**:
  - **Reveal**: a first add shows the Legendary reveal (`ListeningCoordinator.reveal`), an overlay over the tabs and their bars, and over the run review sheet when the add happened there. It shows the Legendary card, "Your first <species>", **Back to my collection** (opens the Journal tab on Cards, closing the run review first when the add happened there), **Share this discovery** (the existing postcard share) and **Undo** while the add's six-second window is open.
  - **Acknowledgment**: the toast reads "Your first <species>" for a first and "A new card for your collection" for a repeat, both with Undo.
  - **Lifetime**: the reveal belongs to the add, not the card, so closing it keeps the sighting, reopening the card never shows it again, and an undo removes the card and the reveal. Adds from the lens show no phone reveal: the lens has its own line.
- **Journal tab**: Cards / Sightings / Species, Cards by default (the mode lives in `RootView` so the reveal can open it).
  - Cards is newest first in two columns, one at accessibility text sizes. A card is the evergreen face with ivory text, framed in gold foil and labelled "Legendary · Your first" with sparkles when Legendary, in sage otherwise.
  - Its art is the glasses photo, else the pack's reference photo, labelled either way. Search filters cards but never changes which is Legendary.
  - New tokens: `CollectibleGoldInk`, `CollectibleGoldWash` and `CollectibleSage` in the asset catalog (dark and Increase Contrast variants), plus the fixed card face, ivory and foil in `Design/Collectible.swift`. Gold ink is 4.9:1 on paper; the card's gold label is 10:1 and its ivory 13:1 on the face.
- **The one-time intro** (`CollectionIntro`, a `collectionIntroShown` default): "Your collection so far", an inline card at the top of the Journal the first time the collection has a card. It is inline, not modal, so it never covers anything, and it is marked shown when it appears. A brand-new birder sees it after their first add too, as the spec's rule says ("first computes with at least one sighting"); it explains Legendary.
- **Lens**: after the sighting is written, the run tells the lens what the add earned (`LensStateMachine.earned(_:for:)`, `LensEarnedCard`). The details page's saved line reads "Your first House Finch" with a star (`.star`; the Display DSL has no sparkle) or "New card" with a check, and "Added to my list" until the write lands (an "Add another" clears the previous add's line until its own write lands). "Added N times ✓" is gone. No page or gesture is added. The line counts against the details page's budget, and the field marks give way to it.
- **Debug flags**: `-autoScreen cards|sightings|reveal` and `-collectionIntroShown NO|YES`. The seed adds an older House Finch, so the Cards view shows a repeat card beside its Legendary first. `scripts/screenshots.sh` captures the cards, the intro, the sightings and the reveal.
- **Tests**:
  - Album: migration, collection, intro.
  - LensSession: the saved line and its budget, earned cards per species.
  - App: recording an add, the reveal and undo in the coordinator, the edits' undo keeping the card, and the Mock Device Kit glasses run naming "Your first House Finch" then "New card".
  - UI (`CardsUITests`): a real add shows the reveal, Back to my collection lands on its Legendary card, a repeat add is the quiet acknowledgment, and reopening the card replays nothing.
- **Not verified on hardware**: the saved line on the glasses (DAT-SETUP-CHECKLIST.md step 13).

## The card: front and back, the flip, Heard or Seen, and rarity (issue #52) — built 2026-10-14
- **Rarity** (ADR-0006):
  - `RarityReader` (Identification) reads the geomodel's occurrence for the species at the sighting's coordinate and BirdNET week and labels it under `RarityCalibration`. No location, or a species the geomodel doesn't know (three pack species: Tyto furcata, Dryobates villosus, Dryobates borealis), gives no reading. Below the 0.03 floor is Exceptional and keeps its occurrence.
  - **Calibration 1** (`Sources/Identification/RarityCalibration.json`, written by `packbuilder calibrate-rarity --version 1`; never edited by hand): the geomodel over every species of the five pack definitions, on a 6 × 6 grid across each pack's bounding box, all 48 weeks, readings at or above the floor only (1.29 million). The bounds are the 50th, 20th and 5th percentiles: **Common from 0.763, Uncommon from 0.386, Rare from 0.101, Exceptional below**. So about half of what the packs expect is Common and one reading in twenty Exceptional. The pack species are mostly expected where their pack is (the median reading is 0.76), which is why the bands sit high. A new calibration takes the next version; saved cards keep theirs.
  - **At the add**: `SightingRecorder` saves the snapshot (occurrence, label, calibration version, week) with the sighting. The engine and the reader share one geomodel load (`BundledIdentification.geoModel`). A failed read records the sighting without rarity.
  - **Backfill**: sightings saved before rarity get one from their saved coordinate and date at the first launch, once per install (`rarityBackfilled` default; skipped for the debug in-memory album). The geomodel is read off the main actor; if a read fails, nothing is marked done and the next launch tries again. A saved snapshot is never read again, and a sighting without a location keeps none.
- **The card page** (`CardDetailView`, `Route.card`): the Cards view opens a card here; Sightings and Species still open the sighting's details, and the card links to them ("Sighting details": note editing, the map, removal). The labelled Flip control sits above the card, because a card is taller than the screen and the control must stay in reach. Share is in the toolbar as on the sighting.
  - **Front**: the Legendary label (editions wait for pack schema 4, so a repeat card has none yet), the art (a tap flips), the place, the species and scientific name, the rarity with its season ("Common here in mid October"), and the date and method. Captions, the type line and stats come with their tickets.
  - **Back** ("Encounter story", on the raised surface in the same frame): the date and local time, the place, Heard or Seen as a segmented control (saved at once, `JournalEdits.setMethod`), the note (edited in the sighting's details), the evidence ("Photo attached" with **View original photo** only when an original exists: an attached photo first, else the glasses photo), what the art is, and where the rarity came from.
  - **Original photo**: the glasses photo from the add counts, as CONTEXT.md defines it, so a glasses card reads "Photo attached" though the birder attached nothing by hand.
  - **Local time**: the sighting stores no time zone, so "local time" is the phone's current zone; a card read while travelling shows the time in that zone.
  - **Fallbacks**: "Place not recorded"; "Rarity unavailable for this place and season", with the reason on the back (no location saved, or no geomodel reading); missing art is a typographic face with the bird's name, labelled "No art yet"; the reference photo keeps its "Reference photo" label and on the back reads "not your bird" with its credit.
- **The flip** (`CardFlip`): both faces stay mounted in one stack, so the card keeps the taller face's footprint. An animated angle turns them about the vertical axis in 480 ms ease-in-out with slight perspective. Each face shows only on its half of the turn and is counter-rotated, so text is never mirrored. Only the face that is up is in the accessibility tree and takes touches. That follows the state rather than the turn, so mid-turn VoiceOver already has the new face. After a flip VoiceOver's focus moves to the Flip control, since the art that may have been activated is now on the hidden face. The control's label changes ("Flip to encounter story" / "Flip to artwork"), its value reads Front or Back, and a flip announces "Back, encounter story" or "Front, collectible art", with a light haptic. Which face is up comes from the state, not the animation, so rapid taps retarget the turn and settle on one face. Reduce Motion switches faces with no turn and no animation. Child controls on the back act alone: View original photo opens a sheet and the card stays on its back.
- **Debug flags**: `-autoScreen card|card-back` (the newest card) and `-reduceMotion YES` (the flip as with the system setting, which a UI test can't turn on). The seeds carry their real geomodel rarity, and a sixth seed (a Common Blackbird with no place, no photo and no pack) shows every fallback. `scripts/screenshots.sh` captures both faces.
- **Tests**: Identification `RarityTests` (the real geomodel at fixed Los Angeles weeks, one species per band, below the floor, no location, unknown species); pack builder `test_rarity.py`; app `SightingRecorderTests` (snapshot at the add, none without a location, backfill once, never recomputed), a coordinator test with a scripted location, `CardTextTests`, `CardFlipTests` and the method in `JournalEditsTests`; `CardFlipAccessibilityTests` hosts the card in a window and walks the UIAccessibility tree VoiceOver reads (one face, at rest and mid-turn); UI `CardFlipUITests` (art and Flip control with the Front/Back value, rapid taps (XCUITest waits for the app to settle between taps, so a tap landing mid-turn is covered by the design, the face following the state, rather than by the test), Reduce Motion, View original photo not flipping, Seen persisting, the fallbacks and the reference-photo label).
- **XCUITest and hidden SwiftUI views**: XCUITest's element tree lists views SwiftUI hides from VoiceOver (`accessibilityHidden`, even collapsed with `children: .ignore`), so `exists` can't check what VoiceOver reads. The accessibility test turns app accessibility on in its process (`_AXSApplicationAccessibilitySetEnabled` from libAccessibility, test target only): without an assistive client SwiftUI builds no accessibility tree at all.

## Species gallery (issue #53) — built 2026-10-15
- **The gallery** (`SpeciesGallery`, Album) is pure over value snapshots (identifier, confirmation time, place name):
  - The header: "House Finch · 15 cards · 2 places" (no place segment when no card has one) and the first discovery.
  - The place filter: All places, a recorded place, or Place not recorded. The picker lists each place alphabetically with its count, then Place not recorded when a card has no location.
  - The sort: Newest first (the default), Oldest first, or By place (alphabetically, newest first within a place, no place last). Ties break by identifier, as the collection's do.
  - The shown and total counts ("6 of 15 cards" under a filter, "15 cards" without), and a card's position in the shown order ("3 of 15") with its previous and next, none at the ends or outside the filter.
- **A card's place** is the name the phone has for it (`PlaceNames`, reverse geocoded to a city, cached per run), else its coordinate to a tenth of a degree (about 11 km, so a few fixes at one spot stay one place offline) until the name arrives; the gallery resolves its cards' places when it opens. Place names are not stored, so a place picked before its name arrived matches nothing once it does; the no-matches state with Clear filters covers it.
- **Phone** (`SpeciesGalleryView`, `Route.gallery`):
  - The Journal's Species view opens the gallery instead of the old list of sightings, and its rows count cards. The Field Guide profile links to it ("Your cards · 15", or "none yet") outside a run review. A card opened from the Cards view links to it with All encounters, which marks that card in the gallery.
  - A lazy grid of tiles, two columns and one at accessibility text sizes. A tile shows the Legendary label on the birder's first, the art, the place, the date, and the time with Heard or Seen. Set editions, named on the other cards, wait for pack schema 4.
  - A place menu and a sort menu (outlined buttons, stacked at accessibility sizes) above the counts.
  - Empty states: a species with no cards shows its guide photo and names with Start listening (`openListen`, the Listen tab) and Open in Field Guide; a filter with no matches shows "No encounters here yet" with Clear filters.
- **Browsing** (`GalleryBrowse`): the gallery's filter, sort and last-opened card live in an observable object the gallery owns while it is on the navigation stack, passed by identity in `Route.card(_:browse:)`.
  - The card page computes the same order from it, so "3 of 15", Previous and Next follow the gallery's filter and sort. Each opens the other card in place, front up. At either end the unavailable direction is disabled (outlined buttons now fade when disabled), and at accessibility sizes the buttons keep only their chevrons.
  - All encounters goes back to the gallery.
  - Back finds the filter and sort untouched (the gallery's state was never torn down), the card last shown ringed in moss and marked selected for VoiceOver, and the grid scrolled to it when Next took it off screen.
  - A card opened from the Cards view steps through all the species' cards, newest first.
- **Debug flags**: `-seedGallery YES` seeds fifteen House Finch cards over three weeks, nine in Los Angeles and six in San Diego, some Seen, some with a glasses photo. `-autoScreen gallery` (or `species`) opens the gallery of the newest card's species. `scripts/screenshots.sh` captures the gallery, light, dark and at accessibility size.
- **Tests**: Album `SpeciesGalleryTests` (header, place filter, picker places, no matches, the sorts and ties, position and neighbours within a filter); UI `GalleryUITests` (filter by the place with six cards, open a card, Next and Previous with the ends disabled, a different card opening front up, Back restoring the filter and marking the third card, All encounters; one column at an accessibility size).

## Open-license pack photos (issue #50) — built 2026-10-16
- **Photos are CC0, CC BY and CC BY-SA only** (ADR-0001: the app takes tips, a gray area under NonCommercial terms). The license set is configuration per asset kind in `packbuilder/licenses.py`: photos take `PHOTO_LICENSES` (`--photo-licenses cc0,cc-by,cc-by-sa` overrides it for one build); sounds take `SOUND_LICENSE_TIERS`, the same rules as before (open ahead of NonCommercial, no-derivatives refused). The iNaturalist query asks only for the set's licenses, so the newest 200 photos reach as far back as open photos need, and the API cache file names carry the set (`…-licenses-cc0-cc-by-cc-by-sa-page-1.json`), so another set is another query. A CC BY-SA crop is shared under CC BY-SA, which the pack LICENSE says when the pack carries one.
- **At most two photos per observation in the shortlist.** Only one photo of an observation is ever chosen, and with open licenses a few prolific observers post bursts of near-identical frames: 48 of the first 60 open Red-tailed Hawk photos were 12 observations by one person, and the species came out with one photo. The cap gave it five.
- **Los Angeles v4**: 160 species, 785 photos (546 CC BY, 231 CC0, 8 CC BY-SA; v3 had 775 CC BY-NC of 794). 152 species have five photos; White-throated Swift, Lesser Goldfinch and American Barn Owl four; Greater Roadrunner, Bald Eagle, Common Poorwill and Bell's Vireo three; **Western Screech Owl one**, the pack's only photo gap (eight open candidates in the county, one usable night photo). The sounds are unchanged: the same lock and the same 266 clips, byte for byte. Released as `pack-us-ca-la-v4`, pinned in `packs/manifest.json` and listed in the published index.
- **Review** on contact sheets of the phone crops, as for #36–#39: a first pass by three reviewer agents and one by hand over every species, then two rounds of replacements by hand until a round came back clean; 83 excludes and 16 includes with a reason each (`_why` in `overrides.json`). What it found: domestic ducks crowd the open Mallard pool (white Pekins, buff and black-bibbed breeds), so two wild Mallards are forced in; LA's Muscovies are all feral stock, so only the mostly white birds and close-ups were excluded; banding-station birds in hand, splayed or stranded birds, silhouettes, a screenshot and a photo with an arrow drawn on it. The detector misses owls, nightjars and swifts against the sky, so Barn Owl, Poorwill, Swift and Bell's Vireo photos are forced in. The Bald Eagle's old includes were NonCommercial photos; two open adults with the white head replace them, ahead of a dark bird in flight.
- **Phone**: the share policy shares CC BY-SA photos with their credit, and the credits link the CC BY-SA 4.0 deed. CC BY-NC stays shareable until the downloadable packs are rebuilt (#58).

## Art model bake-off (issue #49) — run 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-19; FLUX.3 Image Klein picked (ADR-0007)
Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 on comfybox painted ten hard species: Black Phoebe, Anna's Hummingbird, Red-tailed Hawk, Cooper's Hawk, House Finch, California Towhee, Western Bluebird, Great Egret, Great Horned Owl and Townsend's Warbler. Each was painted as pack art in the Los Angeles and San Diego editions, as scene art in three glasses photos plus a Legendary one, and as photo art from two bird photos plus a Legendary one. The rounds covered six prompt versions at two qualities.

Committed: `art/` (prompts, workflows, comfybox setup, the plan and the tuning workflows) and `packbuilder.art`, `packbuilder.comfy` and `python -m packbuilder.bakeoff`. The paintings, composites and contact sheets stay in `build/art-bakeoff/`, because the scenes are the maintainer's glasses photos.
- **The box**:
  - ComfyUI listens on loopback and the tailnet address only, through a systemd drop-in (`art/comfybox/tailnet-only.conf`). Checked: two sockets, 127.0.0.1 and the tailnet address, and the box's LAN address refused.
  - The drop-in also dropped two flags. `--highvram` forbade partly offloading a model, so the 20.5 GB model ran out of the 24 GB card. `--use-sage-attention` made every Qwen image black.
  - The models are installed and SHA-256-checked by `art/comfybox/download-models.sh`: Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 fp8mixed, its text encoder and VAE, and the 4-step Lightning LoRA (all Apache 2.0), plus BiRefNet-general for the cut-out. The comfyui-rmbg node fetches BiRefNet from a re-host that declares no license (upstream is MIT); confirm it before the art worker ships (#68).
- **Time per image** (rubric):
  - The 4-step Lightning LoRA takes about 12 s per 864 × 1152 painting, 20 s for a two-image graph.
  - Full quality (40 steps, cfg 4) takes 207 s, so 90 paintings take about 5 hours.
  - The same inputs and seed give the same image.
- **References**: open-license (CC0, CC BY) photos among the Los Angeles and San Diego packs' cached candidates, picked by hand (`art/bakeoff/plan.json`, with credits).
  - The pack scorer favours dark backgrounds for the lens, so it ranks dim photos high. From a dim or dull reference the model paints its stereotype: the House Finch became cardinal-like, and the female bluebird an Eastern-like male. **Art references must be picked for art: a well-lit, typical adult.**
  - A scene reference must also be unobstructed, because a twig across the bird cuts a hole in the cut-out.
- **What the rounds showed**:
  - Named in a prompt, a species pulls in the model's stereotype of it. Scene and photo art no longer name it.
  - Asked to add the bird from a second image, Qwen drew its own bird, at the wrong scale and with the reference's perch. **Scene art is now a composite**: the glasses photo zoomed 2× to the viewfinder's crop, the reference bird cut out (BiRefNet) and pasted near the middle at 38 % of the height (`packbuilder.art.paste_bird`), then painted.
  - The 4-step LoRA keeps the input faithfully but paints flat, almost photographic, pictures. Wording that forces a painted look makes it reinvent the bird: a hunched hawk stood up, and a female bluebird turned male.
  - Full quality paints richly and keeps sharp inputs: pack and photo art kept the pose and the markings. But it invents a new picture when the input is blurry, which a 360 × 640 stream frame is once zoomed, or when the wording asks for a "wilderness quest". Scene art therefore needs the full-resolution glasses photo, and wording that keeps the photographed moment (prompts v6).
  - "Adventure-cover painting" makes the model letter a title onto the picture. The negative prompt removes it, but only at cfg above 1.
- **Verdict against the rubric** (Claude's reading of the sheets; the maintainer's eye decides):
  - **Anatomy and field marks**: hold whenever the bird's pixels come from a good photo: pack art from a typical adult reference, photo art, and scene art from a composite. They fail where the model is left to imagine the bird.
  - **Scene plausibility**: the composite fixes the place and the scale. Every bird is pasted at the same height for now, so a hummingbird is as tall as an egret; #68 needs a per-species size.
  - **Style match**: the 4-step look is too tame. Full quality is painterly and close to the style guide's gouache, and the maintainer is tuning it by hand.
  - **Time**: as above.
  - **The model stays Qwen-Image-Edit-2511 for now**, pending the maintainer's comparison. No non-commercial model was tried (ADR-0001).
    - Being compared, installed by `art/comfybox/download-alternatives.sh` with adapted ComfyUI templates (`art/README.md`, "Alternative models"): FLUX.2 [klein] 4B, base and distilled, and FireRed Image Edit 1.1, all Apache 2.0, plus consistency LoRAs for Klein 4B and Qwen 2511.
    - Ruled out: FLUX.2 [klein] 9B and [dev] (non-commercial weights), Qwen-Image 2.1 (its own commercial license) and OmniGen2 (its Qwen2.5-VL-3B encoder is research-only).
    - Still unchecked as a whole stack: Step1X-Edit, HiDream-E1 and Qwen-Image-Edit-2509.
  - **A style LoRA** is not needed yet. Revisit it if hand-tuned prompts can't hold one style across species, and train it on public-domain plates only.
- **Tuning by hand** (the maintainer's choice, 2026-10-14):
  - Three editor workflows sit on comfybox under `BirdJournal/`, with copies in `art/comfyui/`. Each holds sample inputs for every species, the prompt template with its placeholders, and a fast-preview switch (`art/README.md`, "Tuning by hand").
  - The tuned templates and graphs go back into `art/prompts.json` and `art/workflows/`, and the batch repaints.
- **Telephoto scenes** (the maintainer's idea, auditioned 2026-10-14 with FLUX.2 [klein] 4B distilled and the House Finch, 69 paintings, sheets in `build/art-bakeoff/telephoto/`):
  - The glasses' wide lens makes a bird the size of a leaf, so a bird at card size looks huge in the whole picture or the 2× zoom. A 12× crop of the full-resolution photo around a perch keeps the perspective a long lens would have from the same spot. Blurred like a 300 mm lens wide open (Gaussian, sigma 10 on the 864-wide canvas) and given to Klein as image 2, with the bird cut out onto white as image 1, it gives a believable scale, a sharp bird on a sharp perch and the photo's colours. It takes 7 s a painting. The workflow is `art/comfyui/alternatives/klein-4b-distilled-telephoto.json`.
  - Pasting the bird onto the crop first failed, sharp or blurred: Klein kept the paste, and the bird floated with nothing under its feet. An unblurred crop as image 2 kept the scale but also the background's focus, so there was no bokeh.
  - One seed for every scene drew the same diagonal log under the bird. A seed per painting and "perched on something that belongs to the place in image 2 … never a bare log" gave twigs and stems of the scene's own plants.
  - The dim House Finch reference, brightened (autocontrast, gamma 0.75) and cleaned of twig scraps, gave a true male House Finch, red only on the head and breast with streaked brown flanks, where the dim one gave an all-red bird. That supports picking art references for art (above).
  - Open:
    - The crop spot was picked by hand. The art worker could crop around the photo's centre, where the wearer aimed the viewfinder.
    - A 15× crop paints the bird the same size and only shows less of the place.
    - Some paintings have an overlong tail or a yellow forehead.
    - The look is photographic. The art style would have to come on top, from the prompt or a second pass.
- **Style pass** (the maintainer's request, auditioned 2026-10-14). The nine telephoto House Finch paintings were repainted in six styles: fantasy line art, illustrative pastoral, a Renaissance oil study, a Greek-myth history painting, magic and mystery, and an epic Renaissance myth imbued with magic. The prompts follow prompts v6's structure: the style, then keep the bird, then no signature, text or frame. 169 paintings, sheets in `build/art-bakeoff/styles/`.
  - **Klein 4B distilled** (one image, 5 s) kept the bird best: red head and breast and streaked flanks in nearly every style. Pastoral turned it robin-like (orange breast, white belly). Its Greek-myth paintings added classical valleys and ruins. The texture is closer to digital painting than to oil.
  - **Qwen-Image-Edit-2511, 4-step Lightning** (one image, 12 s) painted the strongest styles but drifted to stereotypes. One finch became a Northern Cardinal (crest, black mask) in five styles of six, and another a robin in pastoral. **With the reference bird added as image 2** ("match its head pattern, bill shape and colours to image 2"), the cardinal was gone in three styles of four (pastoral kept a mask) and the robin was a finch again, at the cost of louder reds and some halftone-like specks in line art. For Klein the second image changed nothing.
  - **Qwen at full quality** (40 steps, cfg 4, 200 s) painted the most convincing old masters, but all nine birds lost their streaks and two turned yellow. Not a style pass for field marks as it stands.
  - **Signatures**: old-master styles make the model sign the painting, more often on some seeds than others. "Unsigned" in the prompt doesn't stop it at cfg 1. At full quality the negative prompt ("signature, initials") mostly does.
  - Workflows on comfybox: `BirdJournal/style pass` (Qwen, bird as image 2, Fast preview switch) and `BirdJournal/alternatives/Klein 4B distilled - style pass`, with copies in `art/comfyui/`.
- **Repeatability** (2026-10-15). The maintainer's pick, Klein 4B distilled with the Renaissance oil study, was tried on six more species in four spots across the three glasses photos: Black Phoebe, Anna's Hummingbird, California Towhee, Western Bluebird, Townsend's Warbler and Cooper's Hawk. That made 144 paintings, with sheets in `build/art-bakeoff/repeat/`.
  - **The zoom scales with the bird's length.** The finch (14.5 cm) gets 12×, so a 42 cm Cooper's Hawk gets 4× and a 10 cm hummingbird 17×. Every bird stays at card size, and at its true size against the place.
  - **The telephoto pass repeats.** Five species of six kept their field marks in all four spots.
    - The hummingbird failed in all four. Its reference is a back view in shade with no gorget, and from it Klein painted a songbird's body with a long bill.
    - A hole in a cut-out comes out as a white patch. On the bluebird, a leaf had covered its back.
  - **The two-pass Renaissance restyle does not repeat.** Strongly marked birds (bluebird, hawk) held, but plain ones gained head marks they don't have. The phoebe and towhee got a rufous cap, a pale collar or a black mask in most spots, and the warbler's yellow turned orange.
  - **Three fixes failed:**
    - the reference bird as image 2;
    - a neutral underpainting instead of the warm umber and varnish;
    - restyling from the telephoto painting's own latent, partway through an 8-step schedule (starting at step 2 to 5). These partial repaints stayed photographs, and the phoebe's cap was already appearing at step 2.
  - **One pass** paints the style straight from the reference bird, in 7 s. It uses the telephoto prompt with the Renaissance look in place of the 300 mm photograph, plus "do not add a cap, mask or eye stripe it does not have".
    - It kept plain birds plain and marked birds marked.
    - Its background is less repainted and keeps more of the photo's colours.
    - The phoebe came out grey-brown rather than sooty, and one warbler of four drifted.
- **Portrait with a backdrop** (the maintainer's design, 2026-10-15). The bird's markings may not change at all. So the bird is cut out, framed as a three-quarter portrait (cut at the belly, so there are no legs and no perch) and painted. The glasses crop behind it is painted on its own, and the two are composited. Six species (the House Finch plus the five above) on the four spots; sheets in `build/art-bakeoff/portrait/`.
  - **Measuring it.** Each painted bird's colours are compared with the photo's, inside the bird, after a 6 px blur: the mean CIE76 ΔE, where about 2.3 is just noticeable.
  - **Repainting the bird with Klein fails.** The colour error was ΔE 18–41: the finch lost its red, the warbler's yellow turned cream, and the towhee gained a black throat. That rules out a generative repaint of the bird.
  - **"The background of a Renaissance portrait" painted people** into the backdrops. "An empty background of foliage, walls, ground, sky and light only" gave classical landscapes.
  - **Colour-locked bird.** It takes the painting's luminance detail above a 4 px blur (the brushwork and the eye's highlight) and the photo's blurred luminance and all of its colour. Its error is ΔE 0.8–1.4, every marking unchanged by construction. A Kuwahara brush filter also keeps the colours, but it reads as a mosaic.
  - **Still open: one light and one surface.** The locked bird reads as a detailed photo set into a painted landscape. Shifting the backdrop back to the photo's daylight, or putting one warm glaze over everything (ΔE 2.3–4.2 on the bird), only narrows the gap.
  - **Cut-out holes show through.** On the bluebird and the hawk, the backdrop shows where leaves covered the bird in its photo. A reference check should reject such cut-outs.
- **FLUX.3 Image Klein** (Black Forest Labs, released 2026-10-15; the maintainer's pick, 2026-10-19: "this is really good"). The distilled 5.6B checkpoint in int8 on the 3090 Ti, 16 steps without guidance. It needs ComfyUI 0.41, so it runs on its own ComfyUI beside the shared 0.34 (port 8189, `art/comfybox/install-flux3.sh`, `comfyui-flux3.service`). Its license, the FLUX Kommunity License, lets a Qualifying User (under US$5M revenue) use the outputs commercially, on conditions: ADR-0007, accepted 2026-10-19. Committed: prompts v7, the FLUX.3 workflows and `bird-isolate`, `packbuilder.fidelity`, and the bake-off runner; sheets in `build/art-bakeoff/v7/sheets/`.
  - **One pass over a composite** (2026-10-15): the bird pasted at its true size into the telephoto crop, painted as gouache or as a telephoto photograph, its colours then locked to the photo's. FLUX.3 kept every bird's pattern, but the maintainer found none of it good, and a photograph-like card goes against the style guide (art must not pass for a camera photo).
  - **Two references** (the pick): the bird on white as `<ref_image_0>`, the telephoto crop as `<ref_image_1>`, a natural-history watercolour with touches of gouache on paper. FLUX.3 reads a caption of the finished picture, so v7's templates describe the result; the edition's colours are accents on the place's own colours, because v6's palette painted every wall terracotta.
  - **The references.** The cut-outs' flaws were mostly real tail cut off by a branch or running into shadow, and FLUX.3 painted them as scraps. Repairing the cut-outs with FLUX.3's isolated bird, keeping the photo's pixels where the two agree, failed: the isolated bird is not always exactly where the photo's is, so real streaks counted as obstructions and were replaced. The photo crop itself as the reference failed too: FLUX.3 carried its perch, leaves and framing into the painting. **Isolating the bird with FLUX.3** works: on white, at the crop's own size, with what the perch hid completed and "the same light, exposure and colour" (with "soft even light" a dim finch came out 63 L lighter). Its colours are then fitted back to the photo's: ΔE 5.4 before the fit, 3.3 after (medians, 13 references). Photo art paints the photo itself, whose setting is the point.
  - **Fidelity** (`packbuilder.fidelity`): both birds cut out, the photo's registered onto the painted one (scale and shift maximising the outlines' IoU), CIE76 ΔE inside both after a 6 px blur. FLUX.3 shifts colour and tone, most for a dim photo; photo art lightens a dim photo by 9 L (median). Before the fit, the median ΔE was 9.6 (pack), 12.1 (scene) and 13.9 (photo); after it, 5.4, 4.9 and 4.2.
  - **The fit matches each Lab channel's mean and spread** over each bird's own interior. A line fitted pixel by pixel scored about 0.3 lower but needs the outlines to line up: where they didn't (a Great Egret), it flattened the bird into a blue-grey silhouette.
  - **The fitted error is a gate.** FLUX.3 once painted the Great Egret on the lawn as a streaked brown bird; no fit repairs that. A painting is faithful when, after the fit, its ΔE is at most 10 and the outlines overlap by at least 0.65. An unfaithful painting is painted again with the next seed, three times at most, and if none passes the closest is kept and marked for review. In the plan, 88 paintings of 90 passed: the egret passed on its second seed, and two were marked for review (a towhee photo seen from behind, ΔE 10.8, and the egret's San Diego pack art, IoU 0.64). 109 paintings were made for 103.
  - **Time**: 14 s a painting (median; 18 s for scene art, 13 s for an isolation, BiRefNet 1.3 s), so the whole plan takes about 40 minutes on the box, against 5 hours for Qwen at full quality. Run it on the box: the Mac's tailnet link ran at 420 KB/s.
  - **A style LoRA** is still not needed: prompts v7 alone hold one watercolour look across the ten species and the three kinds of art.
  - **Well-lit references** (the maintainer's call, 2026-10-19). The colour is fitted to the reference, so a dim reference gives a dim bird: the dim House Finch (696977499) and the backlit Anna's Hummingbird (693694920) came out near-black, and the dull Western Bluebird (738434109) looks female beside the pack art's bright male. The bake-off keeps its references. **Pack art (#57) picks each species' reference among the pack's photos for a well-lit bird**, not by the lens score, which favours a dark background. Isolation removes the reason for `scene_reference`.
  - **Open**:
    - The perch: an egret was painted on a lamp post. The scene's perch spot could depend on the bird's size or habits.
    - The art worker (#68) needs a content filter on every scene and photo painting, and the model in the card's art provenance (ADR-0007).

## Sets and collector numbers (issue #54) — built 2026-10-19
- **Pack schema 4** lands whole: `set_definition` (one row, the pack's `set.json`, the same JSON the index carries) and the tables `stats`, `fact`, `lore`, `pack_art`, `threatened` (a row for every species once filled, so an empty table still means "unknown") and `family`. Only the set is filled here; the reader treats the other empty tables as "not available yet", so their tickets need no schema change. `SpeciesPack` opens schemas 2 to 4 (a schema-3 pack has no set). App builds older than schema 4 list the rebuilt Los Angeles pack without offering it, as #41 set out (`PackDescriptor.isSupported`), and ignore the index's new `set` key.
- **The set definition** is built from the `set` section of `pack.json` by `packbuilder set` and pinned as `set.json` beside it (committed, like the locks), so the index is written without network and every pack, built or not, has its set:
  - **Boundary**: named parts, each a US Census county (TIGERweb, public domain), an NPS park (public domain) or a hand-drawn GeoJSON polygon committed with the definition. The Coachella Valley and Anza-Borrego Desert State Park are hand-drawn, a few kilometres accurate, since neither is a county nor an NPS unit. Douglas–Peucker at 0.5 km, islets and holes under 1 km² dropped: the five sets are about 90 KB, and the compact index about 84 KB.
  - **Taxonomic order**: the IOC World Bird List 15.1 (CC BY 3.0, credited in the pack LICENSE), pinned by SHA-256. BirdNET's eBird/Clements names match IOC for all but two species of the packs (Red-cockaded and Hairy Woodpeckers, `Leuconotopicus` in IOC), mapped in `TAXONOMY_FIXES`; an unmapped species fails the build. eBird's own taxonomy was not used (eBird data is excluded, ADR-0001).
  - **Editions** (my choice, open to change): Los Angeles "Citrus edition", San Diego "Coastal edition", SF Bay Area "Fog edition", California Deserts "Desert edition", Orlando & Space Coast "Wetland edition", each with an art theme for pack art.
  - `build` and `index` refuse a `set.json` that no longer matches its definition (species, names, edition or boundary parts).
- **Smallest region** means the smallest containing **part**, not the whole set: the Deserts set as a whole (Imperial County, Joshua Tree, Coachella Valley, Anza-Borrego, about 20,000 km²) is larger than San Diego County (11,700 km²), yet the spec's example gives an Anza-Borrego point to the Deserts set. So a set's parts stay separate, and a sighting goes to the set whose containing part has the least area among the sets listing the species (ties to the lower id); otherwise Uncharted, as for a sighting with no location.
- **The collection** (`CardSet`, `CardPlacement`, `SetProgress` in Album) places every card on each computation, so a newly known set pulls older sightings in. Collector numbers pad to three digits ("021/160"), or to the set's size when larger. A card that is not Legendary shows its set's edition, or "Uncharted"; a Legendary card keeps "Legendary · Your first" and its number (or "Uncharted"). Progress counts the distinct species of the cards placed in the set, so a House Finch added in San Diego does not count toward Los Angeles.
- **Known sets** are each readable pack's own set, then the index's for packs not on the phone. The index is now fetched at launch (not only on the packs screen) and the last good copy is kept in `Application Support/Packs/index.json`, so cards stay in index-only sets after an offline relaunch.
- **Phone**: card tiles, gallery tiles, the card front and the Legendary reveal show the heading with the collector number. The Journal's Species view lists set progress ("Los Angeles · 87 of 160" over a bar, then the Uncharted species count), each opening the set's page. The Field Guide gains a Sets section (every known set with its edition and progress); a set's page lists its species in collector order, the collected ones with their photo and the rest as silhouettes, each opening the profile, and works before its pack is downloaded. The phone's add acknowledgment is unchanged.
- **Lens**: the details page's saved line reads "New card · 021/160" or "New card · Uncharted"; a Legendary add still reads "Your first House Finch".
- **Los Angeles v5**: rebuilt at schema 4 from the cache, the same 160 species, 785 photos and 266 sounds as v4 plus its set; released as `pack-us-ca-la-v5`, pinned and published in the index with every pack's set. The other packs stay schema 3 until #58 rebuilds them; their index entries carry their sets already.
- **Debug flags**: `-autoScreen species-list` (the Species view with set progress) and `-autoScreen set` (the bundled pack's set page; `-autoSet <id>` for another).
- **Tests**: pytest `test_sets.py` (simplification, area, IOC order and fixes, the set document, stale sets refused, every committed set current, Anza-Borrego inside San Diego County) and `test_index.py` (every entry carries its set, the bundled pack at schema 4, a stale set never published), and the schema-4 writer; Album `CardSetTests`; `PackSetTests` (schemas 2 to 4, the schema-4 set, every Los Angeles species placed, the index's sets with a set this build can't read costing only the set, the library's known sets, the kept index); the lens state machine's numbered saved line; app `SightingRecorderSetTests`.

## Card stats and the type line (issue #55) — built 2026-10-26
- **The datasets**, all CC BY 4.0 (checked on their Figshare records; one search summary called BIRDBASE CC BY-NC-ND, which is wrong), pinned by SHA-256 in `packbuilder/stats.py` and fetched into `cache/datasets/`:
  - **AVONET** (Tobias et al. 2022, Supplementary dataset 1, Figshare v7): mass, habitat, migration, trophic niche (the diet icon) and primary lifestyle, from its eBird/Clements sheet.
  - **BIRDBASE** v2025.1 (Şekercioğlu et al. 2025): clutch range, nest type, flightlessness, the 2024 IUCN category, and the order and family behind the type line.
  - **The avian wingspan database** (Fox, Brunton and Shephard 2026): the measured wingspan, else the order model's predicted midpoint (the global model's where the order has none), flagged so the card prints "≈".
- **The join** (`stats.join`, run before any download so an unmapped species fails at once; a known gap keeps what the datasets have, such as AVONET's stats when BIRDBASE lacks the species):
  - The BirdNET name is looked up in AVONET's eBird/Clements sheet, which gives the Avibase ID.
  - The wingspan database is keyed by BirdLife's Avibase IDs: the eBird ID is used when it is there, else AVONET's BirdLife–eBird crosswalk leads to it. Where one eBird species is several BirdLife species, the namesake is taken (19 species of the packs, such as Eurasian Teal, Great Horned Owl and Northern Flicker); a `wingspan` fix can pick another, and several with no namesake fail.
  - **BIRDBASE has no Avibase ID**, so it is matched by name: its eBird/Clements v2024b column (BirdNET's own taxonomy), then its main name. That departs from the issue's "through Avibase IDs" for this one dataset. It matched all 321 species of the five packs.
- **Fixes** (`packbuilder/stats/fixes.json`, committed, each with its reason): eight AVONET names for species renamed or split since Clements 2021 (Snowy Plover, Western Cattle Egret, Cooper's Hawk, Least Bittern; American Herring Gull, Hudsonian Whimbrel, American Yellow Warbler and American Barn Owl take the row of the species they were split from). No known gaps: every species of the five packs has stats.
- **Wingspan left off**: the database has no flightless species and no Galliformes at all, so California Quail and Indian Peafowl (and the quail and turkey of the other packs) print no wingspan. A flightless species (BIRDBASE) never gets one, even where the database has a row. Los Angeles: 158 of 160 with a wingspan, 62 measured and 96 predicted; 144 with a nest type (BIRDBASE leaves the Red-tailed Hawk's and some others' blank), every one with a clutch.
- **Threatened**: IUCN Vulnerable, Endangered or Critically Endangered in BIRDBASE, or on the hand list of US Endangered Species Act listings the IUCN rates lower (Western Snowy Plover, California Gnatcatcher, Least Bell's Vireo, Ridgway's Rail, Red-cockaded Woodpecker, Wood Stork, Everglade Snail Kite). Los Angeles has seven: those three, plus Marbled Godwit and Grey Plover (uplisted to Vulnerable in 2024) and the Red-crowned and Yellow-headed Amazons (Endangered in their native range; LA's are feral flocks). The flag is in the pack now for the circles' "a secret spot" rule; nothing on the phone uses it yet.
- **Type line** (`packbuilder/stats/type-lines.json`, my choice, open to change): a group word per order (Songbird, Raptor for hawks, falcons and vultures, Owl as the spec names it ("Owl — Barn Owl", "Owl — Screech Owl"), Waterfowl, Shorebird, Seabird for gulls, terns, auks, cormorants and boobies, Waterbird, Gamebird, Nightbird for nightjars, Landbird for doves, cuckoos, swifts, hummingbirds, kingfishers, woodpeckers and parrots) and a family word per family, with a genus's own word where the family's would be wrong ("Raptor — Eagle", "Waterfowl — Goose", "Seabird — Tern"). A family it lacks fails the build.
- **Pack**: the schema-4 tables `stats`, `threatened` (a row for every species) and `family` are filled; `report.json` lists `gaps.stats`; the LICENSE credits the three datasets with their DOIs. No schema change.
- **Phone**:
  - **Front**: the type line under the names, then wingspan ("≈ 23 cm" when predicted), weight and diet with its icon, side by side or stacked at accessibility sizes. Each reads as one VoiceOver element with its units spelled out ("Wingspan about 9 inches, estimated from wing length", "Weight 0.75 ounces", "Diet: mostly seeds").
  - **Back**: a "Natural history" section with habitat, migration, nest, clutch and lifestyle in plain words and the datasets the species' stats came from; "No natural-history stats for this bird in your packs" without them.
  - **Units** follow the phone's measurement system: inches, feet, ounces and pounds under the US system, metric otherwise (the UK system too: British birders measure birds in metric).
  - **Credits** list the three datasets with citation, DOI and license when a pack carries stats.
  - Lifespan and body length are not shown (the spec: no open global source).
- **Los Angeles v6**: rebuilt from the cache with stats; released as `pack-us-ca-la-v6`, pinned and published in the index.
- **Tests**: pytest `test_stats.py` (a manual fix, the failing unmapped species, a known gap, measured versus predicted wingspan, flightless species and one the database lacks, the crosswalk's namesake and a wingspan fix, threatened from the IUCN field and the hand list, the type line's genus word and an unmapped family, the committed fixes naming pack species, and every committed pack joining when the datasets are cached) and the writer; `PackStatsTests` (the fixture pack's stats, gap, type line and flag; a schema-3 pack without them; every Los Angeles species with stats or a listed gap and a type line); app `CardStatsTests` (units in both systems, "≈", the diet icons and their VoiceOver names, the front and back leaving off what is missing, the sources line).

## Pack art (issue #57) — built 2026-10-20 to 2026-11-01; Los Angeles v7 reviewed and published
- **The step** (`packbuilder/src/packbuilder/pack_art.py`, run by `packbuilder build` after the photos are chosen): one painting per species per pack on comfybox's FLUX.3 ComfyUI over the tailnet (the link ran at about 2.5 MB/s, against 420 KB/s during #49, so the build paints from the Mac), with the committed `pack-art` workflow in the set's edition theme (`set.edition.theme`, prompts v8 and on). The bird is isolated on white and every painting goes through the #49 fidelity gate (`painting.py`, shared with the bake-off). A species with no open-license photo is painted from text (`pack-art-text`, the only template that names the species); it must be reviewed by hand. A Bushtit from text came out a generic warbler, so expect to reject such paintings or list them as gaps.
- **The reference** is the species' best-lit photo among the pack's chosen photos (`lighting`: the 75th percentile of L* over the middle of the bird's box, less for a bird darker than the frame around it and for blown highlights), or the one `overrides.json` pins as `art.reference`. The lens score's first photo was often a flight shot or a dim bird. The lighting score helps, but it can't tell a flight shot, a flock or a head-only close-up from a perched bird, and it favours a slim bird against bright sky. In the Los Angeles first pass, 37 species of 160 needed a pin (flight or hovering 8, several birds 3, head-only close-ups 2, dim, backlit or distant 8, half-hidden or odd poses 10, and a dull female where the male is the bird birders picture 6), each with its reason in `overrides.json`. Follow-up for the other packs: score only the bird's own pixels (a variant measured against the background caught backlit birds but tied most others at full marks), and reject a reference whose cut-out holds more than one bird.
- **The crop** keeps the whole bird: a 3:4 box inside a landscape photo cut a House Finch's head off and FLUX.3 invented one, so the reference crop now reaches past the photo's edge, filled with its own colours blurred, which the isolation paints white.
- **Prompts v9**: v8's "it perches naturally" put the coot, the gull and the Mallard on citrus branches; v9 says it "perches, stands or swims ... where a bird like it lives: on a branch, a rock, the ground, the shore or the water". That in turn stands most birds on a rock by a stream. Two other wordings were tried on six species and were worse: "as it would be in life ... on something that suits it" stood every bird on a bare mound (the Mallard on a dry hill), and naming kinds of bird ("a songbird on a twig, a waterbird at the water's edge") painted extra birds into the scene. The look across the set is the maintainer's call on the sheets.
- **Prompts v10** (the maintainer's review of the v9 sheets, 2026-11-01): most paintings had the wrong scale or an odd composite. A House Finch on a twig in its reference stood on a rock as big as itself, and a Mourning Dove whose wire the isolation removed floated by a stray twig. So the setting comes from the reference, not the model: each species has `art.setting` in `overrides.json`, one of perch, trunk, flight, ground, rock, shore, wade or water, classified by hand from its reference on the sheets (Los Angeles: 80 perch, 28 ground, 18 shore, 15 water, 7 rock, 7 wade, 3 trunk, 2 flight). Any perch off the ground (a branch, wire, fence, post or rail) is painted as a branch, the maintainer's call. A woodpecker clinging to a trunk keeps a trunk, and the two flight shots (Osprey, White-throated Swift) fly above a branch. The bird stays about half the card's height, close up, with its setting at its own scale and the edition theme far behind. Its length (the middle of the description's range, or `art.length_cm` for the 13 without one) sizes the branch (pencil-thin under 20 cm, finger-thick to 35 cm, wrist-thick above) and how long a leaf is beside it. Without a size the 52 cm Red-tailed Hawk perched on a flimsy flowering twig. Trials on 23 species at v9's seeds are in `build/art-trial/` (not committed). Padding the isolated bird onto a larger canvas to make it smaller changed nothing: FLUX.3 sizes the bird from the prompt, and the Mourning Dove, whose reference crop fills its frame, still paints large. The setting is in the lock, so changing one species' setting repaints only that species.
- **The lock** (`packbuilder/packs/<id>/art.lock.json`, committed) pins per species the source photo, workflow, isolation and prompts versions, the theme, the seeds, the painting's SHA-256 (its name in `cache/art/paintings/`), its fidelity and the review. A warm lock paints nothing; changed inputs repaint and go back to review; a painting gone from the cache is repainted from its locked seeds. Isolated birds are cached by photo, seed, workflow and the isolation prompt's own hash, so a pack-art prompt bump reuses them.
- **The review**: contact sheets in `build/pack-art/<id>/` (each reference beside its painting with its state and fidelity, and an overview) and `packbuilder art-review` (approve, reject with a reason, gap). Only approved paintings ship. `--approve-all` skips paintings from text and the ones the gate flagged, which need an approve by name. A rejected painting is repainted by the next build with the next round of seeds; a gap gets none and is listed under `gaps.art` in `report.json`, and its cards show another pack's painting of the species, else the reference photo. Paintings not yet approved are listed under `unreviewed.art`, and a pack with any is not ready to publish (`PackArtTests` fails on one).
- **The format**: 864 × 1152 AVIF at quality 60 (`writer.py`), about 120 KB, half a JPEG's size at the same look. The v5 pack was 174 MB on disk against the 200 MB budget, and 160 JPEG paintings at quality 85 (about 250 KB each) would have passed it. iOS decodes AVIF natively. `pack_art` rows carry the file, the source photo ids (JSON) and "pack-art@3 bird-isolate@1 prompts@9". The LICENSE lists each painting with the photo it was painted from and its photographer's credit, and states that paintings from CC BY-SA photos are shared under CC BY-SA.
- **Phone**: `SpeciesPack` reads `pack_art`; `PackLibrary.packArt` takes the art of the pack whose set the card is in, then any other pack's. The art order (`CardText.Art`) is approved photo art, ready scene art unless "Use pack art", pack art, the reference photo, then the typographic face, and the glasses photo is evidence, not art. Pack and scene art are labelled "AI illustration", photo art "AI-embellished from your photo", and the back names FLUX.3 Image Klein (ADR-0007). Edition labels on non-Legendary cards came with #54. Debug flag: `-autoCardSpecies <scientific name>` with `-autoScreen card`.
- **Running it**: the Mac must stay awake. Asleep overnight, it stalled the paint for 14 hours and three requests timed out (those species stay unpainted until the next build), so run builds under `caffeinate -i`. At about 75 s a species from the Mac, the Los Angeles pack takes about 3.3 hours to paint from scratch.
- **The Los Angeles review** (2026-11-01). The maintainer turned down the v9 sheets for scale and odd composites, which led to prompts v10, and all 160 were repainted. 140 were approved at once. The 20 the gate flagged mostly floated: their isolated reference birds had lost their feet or were cut off by the photo's frame, and a new seed doesn't fix that. 18 got a new reference (a single, whole, well-lit bird with its feet showing) and 2 got new seeds. Two species had no usable photo in the pack, so their reference is forced in from the wider open-license pool with `include`: the House Finch (677925555, a red male in sun) and the Mitred Parakeet (339582922). All 160 are approved, with no gaps. Lesson for the other packs: look at the isolated bird, not just the photo, when picking a reference.
- **Los Angeles v7**: the v6 pack plus the 160 paintings, 192.7 MB on disk (192,679,091 bytes) against the 200 MB budget, 18.7 MB of it pack art (about 117 KB a painting), and a 191.3 MB zip. Released as `pack-us-ca-la-v7`, pinned and published in the index. That leaves 7.3 MB of headroom for the lore and fact sheets (#56, v8).
- **Tests**: pytest `test_pack_art.py` against a stub ComfyUI that paints (`tests/comfy_stub.py`: the references, the theme, the lock, a warm rebuild painting nothing, the review), the AVIF writer and the whole-bird crop; `PackArtTests` (the fixture's art and gap, older schemas, the library's set-first lookup, every Los Angeles species with art or a listed gap, and the size against the budget); `CardTextTests` (the art order and labels); the card UI tests.

## Tip jar: Support Bird Journal (issue #59) — built 2026-11-01, App Store Connect setup pending

- **The screen**: Settings → About → "Support Bird Journal" (`SupportView`, one row in `SettingsView`, `Route.support`). What a tip pays for (the server and the machine at home that paints the cards; circles are not named until they ship), "Tips are one-time and unlock nothing. Every feature of Bird Journal stays free for everyone, whether you tip or not.", the three tips with the App Store's names and prices, and a thank-you in place of the tips once one goes through ("Back to the tips" brings them back). The footer says no account is needed and nothing is kept or sent.
- **Products**: three StoreKit 2 consumables, cheapest first in `TipJar.productIDs`:
  - `com.matthewcelia.mybirdjournal.tip.seed` — "Scoop of seed", $1.99
  - `com.matthewcelia.mybirdjournal.tip.suet` — "Suet cake", $4.99
  - `com.matthewcelia.mybirdjournal.tip.feeder` — "Bird feeder", $9.99
  The prices are App Store Connect's to set; the screen shows `displayPrice`, so changing a tier needs no app change. Creating the products in App Store Connect is a maintainer step (the checklist is in the #59 PR).
- **Unlocks nothing, by construction**: `TipJar` finishes every verified tip at once and never looks one up again: no `currentEntitlements`, no `Transaction.all`, no receipt, no `AppStore.sync`, nothing in `UserDefaults`, nothing sent to the server, no account. `TipJar.swift` is the only file that imports StoreKit; `NoPurchaseGateTests` reads the app and package sources and fails if another file imports it, if any file asks StoreKit what was bought, or if the tip jar touches the network or `UserDefaults`.
- **Transaction updates** (Ask to Buy approvals, interrupted purchases) are listened to while the screen is open, not from launch: StoreKit re-delivers an unfinished transaction to the next `Transaction.updates` listener, and a tip unlocks nothing, so finishing it the next time the screen opens loses nothing. An unverified transaction is finished too and shows "The App Store couldn't confirm the tip." The thank-you (or a failure) is an outcome beside the loading state, not one of its cases, so a tip finished while the tips load neither hides them nor is lost.
- **Local testing**: `BirdJournal/BirdJournalTests/BirdJournal.storekit` holds the three products. It lives in the test target so `SKTestSession` finds it in the test bundle, and the scheme's Run action points at it (`StoreKitConfigurationFileReference`), so running from Xcode sells test tips. It is not in the app bundle.
- **Debug flags**: `-autoScreen support` opens the screen; `-autoTipThanks YES` shows the thank-you.

## Accounts: the backend stack and optional sign-in (issue #60) — built 2026-11-01, deploy and device check pending
- **Layout**: `infra/` is the CDK (Python) app (uv project, `birdjournal_infra/` constructs, `tests/` template tests, `scripts/` wizard, deploy, app-config writer and smoke test; the CDK CLI pinned in `package.json`); `backend/` is the FastAPI service (uv project, `src/birdjournal_api/`, `tests/` the in-process harness). Ported from ivan-parallax (its Auth construct, config, template tests, Google wizard, smoke script and access-token verifier), copied, not imported.
- **AWS account**: the shared Lightsail VR account (profile `lsvr-admin`), us-west-2, one stack `BirdJournal`. `Tags.of(stack)` puts `project=birdjournal` on every resource; the template test fails on any resource that lacks it unless CloudFormation's type takes no tags (an explicit list). The wizard tags the two secrets it creates the same way. The CDK bootstrap stack is shared with ivan-parallax and not tagged. Neither the wizard nor `deploy.sh` bootstraps over it: `infra/scripts/check-bootstrap.sh` reads its `BootstrapVersion` and stops if it is older than the synthesized stack requires (version 32 in us-west-2 on 2026-11-01, against 6 needed), leaving any upgrade to the account's owner.
- **Cost tracking**: the budget (`birdjournal-monthly`, $10 a month, `monthlyBudgetUsd` in `infra/cdk.json`) filters on `user:project$birdjournal`, so it counts BirdJournal alone in the shared account, and emails at 80 % actual and 100 % forecast. The address is kept out of the public repo (`BUDGET_ALERT_EMAIL` in `~/keys/birdjournal.env`). The filter and Cost Explorer only see the tag once `project` is an **active cost allocation tag**: a Billing setting that belongs to the payer (management) account in an AWS Organization, and a key appears for activation up to 24 hours after a tagged resource exists. The wizard's last stage checks and activates it (`aws ce update-cost-allocation-tags-status --region us-east-1 --cost-allocation-tags-status TagKey=project,Status=Active`), or says to run it from the payer account. ivan-parallax already uses the `project` key, so it may be active.
- **Cognito**: Sign in with Apple and Google (App Review 4.8), the hosted domain `birdjournal-lightsailvr`, one public client (authorization code + PKCE, no secret) with callbacks `birdjournal://auth/callback` (the app) and `http://localhost:48765/callback` (the smoke script). Password sign-up is closed. Access and ID tokens last an hour, the refresh token **a year** (spec story 68: stay signed in; ivan-parallax used 30 days). Token revocation on. The app asks for `identity_provider=SignInWithApple` or `Google`, so the hosted UI's chooser never shows. Google's and Apple's credentials live in Secrets Manager (`birdjournal/google-oauth`, `birdjournal/apple-sign-in`), created by the wizard outside the stack and resolved by CloudFormation at deploy.
- **Accounts outlive the stack**: the user pool and the table are Retain with deletion protection (the table also has point-in-time recovery). ivan-parallax's slice destroyed everything; BirdJournal's accounts are real birders'.
- **API**: an HTTP API with one `$default` route proxying to FastAPI on Lambda (Mangum, Python 3.14, ARM64, 512 MB, 10 s), stage throttled at 25 requests/s with bursts of 50 so a runaway client costs 429s, not Lambda time. **No API Gateway JWT authorizer**: the app verifies the access token itself (signature against the pool's JWKS, issuer, expiry, `token_use = access`, `client_id`), which is the path the harness tests; a second check at the gateway would be untested config. Logs kept a month.
- **The Lambda package** is built on the developer's machine by `uv` (the locked dependencies as `aarch64-manylinux2014` wheels for Python 3.14, plus the package), with no Docker; the AWS SDK is left to the runtime (25 MB instead of 53). The template tests skip bundling; `test_bundling.py` builds the real package.
- **DynamoDB**: one on-demand table, string `pk` and `sk`, single-table. An account is `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `PROFILE` with `display_name` (absent until set) and `created_at`. The first authenticated call creates it with a conditional put; a `PATCH` before any read creates it too. Later tickets add their items and indexes beside it.
- **Display name**: 1 to 40 characters after trimming, no control characters, else 422. On the first sign-in the app offers the ID token's `name` (Google's full name, Apple's name when Apple sends it, only on the very first authorization) to an account without one; the birder can change it in Settings → Account.
- **The harness** (`backend/tests/conftest.py`): FastAPI in process, DynamoDB Local in a testcontainers container (or `DYNAMODB_ENDPOINT`), a fresh table per test, tokens signed by a test RSA key whose JWKS the app is handed, and a settable clock. Every later backend ticket tests through it.
- **The app**: `AccountSession` (signed out, signing in, signed in, or unavailable when the build has no server) over `CognitoAuthClient` (ASWebAuthenticationSession, ephemeral so the hosted UI keeps no cookie and the next sign-in can pick another account; the token endpoint for the code exchange and renewal; `/oauth2/revoke` at sign-out) and `HTTPAccountAPI`. No Amplify. Tokens are one Keychain item (`AfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`); a reinstall starts signed out (Keychain items outlive the app, so the first launch clears them). A token within a minute of expiry is renewed before a call, a 401 renews once and retries, and an `invalid_grant` signs out with a message. Signing out forgets the tokens and touches nothing else: the journal and the cards stay.
- **Server settings in the app**: `BirdJournal/BirdJournal/Account/AccountServer.json` (API URL, Cognito domain, client ID; all public) is written by `infra/scripts/write-app-config.sh` after a deploy and committed. Empty, as now, Settings → Account reads "Accounts aren't available in this build yet."
- **Settings**: one Account section with one row ("Account · Not signed in" or the name) opening the Account screen (Debug only for now: off in Release, see the switch below). Nothing else in the app reads the account.
- **Debug flag**: `-fakeAccount signedOut|signedIn` swaps in a fake auth client and API with tokens in memory (the UI tests use it).
- **Tests**: backend pytest (the account created on the first call and returned, read back unchanged, one per subject, renamed, trimmed, bad names refused and the old one kept, a PATCH before any read; expired, other-client, other-pool, ID, forged and malformed tokens refused and no account written; an HTTP API event through Mangum; settings from the environment); infra pytest (tags on every resource, the budget's tag filter and alarms, both providers from the wizard's secrets, the public PKCE client and its callbacks, token lifetimes, Retain and deletion protection, the table, the HTTP API and throttling, the function and its environment, IAM limited to the table, log retention, outputs; the real bundle's handler, ARM wheels and no AWS SDK); app `CognitoAuthClientTests` (RFC 7636's S256 example, the authorize URL, the code exchange's form, callbacks with the wrong state, an error or another path refused, refresh keeping the refresh token, `invalid_grant`, revoke, the empty server settings, the account JSON) and `AccountSessionTests` (sign-in keeps tokens and names the account, a chosen name kept, tokens across a relaunch, renewal before a call, retry on 401, sign-out on a revoked refresh token, sign-out revoking and forgetting, cancel is no error, unavailable without a server); UI `AccountUITests` (the signed-out Account screen offers Apple and Google and says it's optional; signing in shows the name and email, signing out brings the buttons back).
- **Off in Release until in-app deletion ships (issue #88)**: App Review 5.1.1(v) wants account deletion in the app wherever sign-in is, and that comes with #71. The switch is the Swift compilation condition `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED`, set once in the project-level Debug configuration (`SWIFT_ACTIVE_COMPILATION_CONDITIONS`), so every target sees it. Off, Settings has no Account section and `AccountSession.forApp()` returns an unavailable session over an in-memory store before anything else: no Keychain read, no reinstall clear, no server. `AccountSession.isEnabledInThisBuild` is the one place the app reads it. A compilation condition, not an Info.plist key or a user default, so nothing can flip it at run time. **To turn it on** (with #71): add `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` to the Release configuration's conditions too. Tests: `AccountSwitchUITests` expects the row in Debug and none in Release (keyed on `DEBUG`, not the switch, so turning it on in Release fails the test until #71 updates it); `scripts/build-and-test.sh --release` runs it in the `BirdJournal-Release` scheme (Release configuration, that one test only, since the unit tests use DEBUG app code and cannot build in Release), under the same watchdog, with its own DerivedData. The default runs do not include it (a second full Release build), so run `--release` when touching the account or the build settings. `AccountSessionTests` checks `forApp(accountsEnabled: false)` is unavailable, with no server or Keychain behind it. `AccountUITests` compiles only with the switch on in Debug.
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy (it waits on the maintainer's go-ahead and the wizard's console steps), and on a device sign-in with Apple and with Google, tokens across a relaunch, and the journal and cards after sign-out.

## The Mock Device Kit pipeline: the hang, timeouts and the Chrome lens (issue #82) — built 2026-11-01
- **Root cause (toolkit bug, worked around).** The mock suites hung (#51, #54, #55, twice on 2026-11-01) because the toolkit's mock link wedges: when one mock device is torn down and the next paired while the old link's stream I/O is still closing, one `MWDATCore` run-loop thread (a single one for the whole process) spins at 100% CPU inside one block calling `CFReadStreamRead` on a CFStream bound pair (`sample` of the host, reproduced on BJ-82). From then on nothing in that test host gets an answer over the mock link. So every following lens test fails with "No connected display glasses." (the device selector never sees the new device), a permission check awaits forever, and the host does not exit after the run. The DAT reference says it: the async `disable()` returns while "the underlying stream I/O may still be closing on its background thread". The CoreBluetooth "XPC connection invalid" lines are noise: passing runs log them on every `enable()`. Load and parallel xcodebuilds make it likelier but are not the cause: run side by side under the same load, on two simulators, the old teardown wedged and the new one did not.
- **Repro**: `MockDeviceKitStressProbe` cycles pair, a started session with a camera stream, and teardown 40 times in one host (`TEST_RUNNER_BIRDJOURNAL_MDK_STRESS=40`, `TEST_RUNNER_BIRDJOURNAL_MDK_TEARDOWN=bare|unpair|settle|settled`; the command, with the longer allowance it needs, is in the probe's header). The old teardown (`enable` … `disable`) wedged at cycle 7; an awaited `unpairDevice` alone failed at cycle 2; the settle alone and the full teardown each passed 40 of 40. Not reported upstream yet: a repro outside this app would be the next step.
- **Workaround** (`MockDeviceKitTests.withMockDisplay`): wait until the paired device itself (`glasses.deviceIdentifier`) is connected, not any device (a stale one satisfied the old wait); tear down with an awaited `unpairDevice`, an awaited `disable()`, then a wait for `Wearables.devices` to empty and 300 ms more (DEBUG `GlassesConnection.tearDownMockDeviceKit`, which the DEBUG `unpairMockGlasses()` uses too). The interruption tests stop their run before the teardown (`withRun`), not in `defer { Task { … } }`, which ran the stop after `disable()` had begun. Test code and DEBUG code only; the glasses path in production is unchanged.
- **Timeouts** (a hang is a failure, never a stall):
  - The root `MockDeviceKitTests` suite has `.timeLimit(.minutes(2))`, which applies to every nested test (the longest takes about 25 s). Checked with `--hang-probe`: a test awaiting a continuation nobody resumes, or one spinning a thread, records "Time limit was exceeded" at the limit. Since it never returns, xcodebuild then ends the host ("Restarting after unexpected exit, crash, or test timeout"), the run fails (exit 65) and the script exits 1.
  - xcodebuild's `-test-timeouts-enabled YES` with a default allowance of 180 s and a maximum of 300 s (`BIRDJOURNAL_TEST_ALLOWANCE`, `BIRDJOURNAL_TEST_MAX_ALLOWANCE`) for the tests without a Swift Testing limit. It reaches Swift Testing tests too: the `silent` probe (no trait) failed with "Time limit was exceeded: 180.000 seconds" and the same restart.
  - The watchdog (`scripts/lib/watchdog.sh`, bash: macOS has no `timeout`) kills a run whose log has not grown for `BIRDJOURNAL_QUIET_MINUTES` (default 6). It `sample`s the test host into the logs, kills xcodebuild and the run's app processes on that simulator only, `simctl shutdown`s that simulator (the recovery that worked in #54 and #55), and fails the run with exit 124 and a clear message.
  - `-collect-test-diagnostics never`: after a failure xcodebuild ran `simctl diagnose`, which gathered every crash report on the Mac and wrote nothing for over 3 minutes, long enough to look like the hang.
- **Split runs by default**: build once, then the app's tests without `MockDeviceKitTests`, then `MockDeviceKitTests` alone, then the package; each runs even when an earlier one failed, so a mock failure cannot hide the rest. The onnxruntime CodeSign `rm -rf` is in the script.
- **Per-agent simulators**: `BIRDJOURNAL_SIMULATOR=BJ-<n>` (created as an iPhone 17 Pro on the newest iOS when missing) or `BIRDJOURNAL_DESTINATION`; CLAUDE.md says so.
- **The Chrome lens**: `scripts/mock-lens-in-chrome.sh` builds, installs and launches the app with `-autoMockLens YES -autoMockLensInputs "navLeft navDown"` on one simulator and waits until `http://127.0.0.1:9000/` answers. The simulators share the Mac's loopback, so it runs on one simulator at a time and refuses when another process holds the port; `-mockPreviewPort` (DEBUG, default 9000) and `--port` pick another.
- **Test runs do not start the preview server by default** (my choice, open to change): parallel agents would fight over :9000, and every server start and stop is one more teardown on the link that wedges. `TEST_RUNNER_BIRDJOURNAL_MOCK_PREVIEW_PORT=9000` starts it in every mock test for watching a run live in Chrome, on one simulator.

## Circles: create, invite by link or QR, join, leave (issue #61) — built 2026-11-01, deploy, DNS and device check pending
- **Rules** (ADR-0005, spec v2 stories 71–74, 87–89): a birder creates and names a circle (1 to 40 characters after trimming, no control characters, the account's own rules); any member invites; an invite link works for anyone who opens it until it expires **7 days** after it was made (one link can bring several friends); at most **20** current members, the 21st refused with 409 `circle_full`; an account can be in any number of circles; members leave; only the creator removes members (403 `creator_only` for anyone else; 404 for non-members, so a circle's existence never leaks). A circle carries the creator's device's IANA time zone from creation, for the monthly goals of a later ticket (default `UTC`).
- **Choices the issue left open** (mine, open to change): a creator who **leaves** hands the circle to the longest-standing remaining member, as the spec says for account deletion; the last member leaving leaves an empty circle item behind (its finds history arrives with #62). Concurrent leaves and handoffs are conditioned on the creator read and the successor still being a member, and re-read on a conflict, so `created_by` always names a current member. A **removed** member can't come back through an invite made before the removal (403 `invite_revoked`); an invite made after it lets them back, and anyone else can still use the old link. A member who left can rejoin with any live invite. Accepting an invite to a circle the account is already in changes nothing and returns it.
- **Items** in the single table, beside `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `PROFILE` (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/circles.py` documents them): `CIRCLE#<id>` / `META` (name, creator, time zone, `created_at`, `member_count`); `CIRCLE#<id>` / `MEMBER#<sub>` (`joined_at`, `left_at` once gone, `removed_at` when the creator removed them: members by circle with joined and left times); `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `CIRCLE#<id>` (circles by member, current only); `INVITE#<token>` / `INVITE` (circle, inviter, `created_at`, `expires_at`, and `ttl` a month after expiry). **No index**: the account's circles are a second item written in the same transaction as the membership, so they never disagree. The table gains DynamoDB time-to-live on `ttl`; expired invites still answer 410 for that month, then 404. Circles and memberships live in the Retain table with the accounts.
- **The cap under concurrency**: a join is one `TransactWriteItems`: increment `member_count` on `member_count < 20`, put the membership on "no current membership", put the account's circle item. The cancellation reason says which (full, or already a member by a racing accept). `TransactionConflict` retries up to four times. A test races ten friends for the last five places.
- **Tokens**: `secrets.token_urlsafe(16)` (128 bits; the token is all that stands between a stranger and the circle). Every route needs the access token, the invite preview included: the join screen signs in first.
- **API** (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/circles_api.py`): `GET/POST /v1/circles`, `GET /v1/circles/{id}`, `POST /v1/circles/{id}/invites` (returns `token`, `url`, `expires_at`; the QR code's payload is `url` itself), `GET /v1/invites/{token}` (the circle, who invited, expiry, `already_member`), `POST /v1/invites/{token}/accept`, `DELETE /v1/circles/{id}/members/me` (leave), `DELETE /v1/circles/{id}/members/{sub}` (creator removes; 422 for themselves). Invite URLs are `INVITE_BASE_URL/i/<token>`, the Lambda's environment set from `inviteDomain`.
- **The invite domain** `birdquest.matthewcelia.com` (`inviteDomain` in `infra/cdk.json`): CloudFront (PriceClass 100, TLS 1.2 2021, HTTPS redirect, GET/HEAD) over a private S3 bucket with origin access control, in the **us-west-2 stack**.
