It serves `infra/site/` (a home page and `invite.html`, the fallback page explaining how to get the app; a CloudFront function rewrites `/i/*` to it) and `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` as `application/json`, naming `iosAppIds` (`647FZYVR5Q.com.matthewcelia.mybirdjournal`) for `/i/*` only. Two `BucketDeployment`s: the pages prune but exclude the association file. The bucket is Retain. The temporary site pages (home, privacy, terms) stay on GitHub Pages (`lightsailvr.github.io/BirdJournal`) and the fallback page links there; they can move to this domain later.
- **The certificate is outside the stack** (not a second stack, not a cross-region construct): `infra/scripts/circles-wizard.sh` requests it in us-east-1 (tagged `project=birdjournal`) and saves its ARN as `INVITE_CERTIFICATE_ARN` in `~/keys/birdjournal.env`, which `deploy.sh` reads like the budget address (the ARN names the account, so not in the public repo). CloudFront takes it by ARN from the us-west-2 stack; CDK refuses an ARN outside us-east-1 (tested). Why: an ACM certificate in a CloudFormation stack with DNS at SiteGround holds the stack in CREATE_IN_PROGRESS until someone adds the record, and a us-east-1 stack would need a CDK bootstrap there in the shared account, which the #60 rule forbids doing from here. This way the only bootstrap is the existing us-west-2 one. ACM renews the certificate while the validation CNAME stays at SiteGround.
- **`INVITE_CERTIFICATE_ARN` is required for every deploy**, like the budget address: a deploy without it would delete the distribution and break every invite link, so the stack refuses to synthesize without one rather than leaving the invite site out. The accounts stack can't be redeployed until the circles wizard has requested the certificate.
- **The circles wizard** (7 stages, `WIZARD_FROM` resumes, the accounts wizard's library): request or reuse the certificate; the validation CNAME at SiteGround (Site Tools → Domain → DNS Zone Editor; SiteGround appends the zone, so the wizard prints the short name); wait for ISSUED (polls 30 minutes); deploy; the `birdquest` CNAME to the `InviteDistributionDomain` output; check the association file's content type and the fallback page; Associated Domains on the App ID.
- **The app**: a fourth tab, **Circles** (`Circles/`), shown only when `AccountSession.isEnabledInThisBuild` (#88's switch: Debug yes, Release no until in-app deletion, #71). `CircleClient` is the protocol, `HTTPCircleClient` the real one (the account's base URL, so `AccountServer.json` keeps its shape), `FakeCircleClient` the in-memory one (DEBUG). `CircleStore` holds the list and the pages and calls through `AccountSession.withAccessToken`, so circle calls renew tokens and retry a 401 exactly like the account's. The tab: signed out, an invitation with the Apple and Google buttons (`SignInButtons`, shared with Settings → Account); signed in, the list (name, member count, "You started it") with **New circle**; no circles, a "Start a circle" prompt. A circle's page is the shell later tickets fill: **Invite friends** (a sheet with the QR code, the link, its expiry and the share sheet), the members with initials avatars ("Members · N of 20"; the creator swipes to remove, with a confirmation), and Settings → **Leave circle** (the confirmation says the circle passes on when the creator leaves).
- **Invite links in the app**: the `com.apple.developer.associated-domains` entitlement `applinks:birdquest.matthewcelia.com` (`BirdJournal/BirdJournal.entitlements`), **Debug configuration only** for now: with #88's switch off in Release the app would claim the links and ignore them, so a release build leaves them to the browser's fallback page. When #71 turns the switch on in Release, give Release the same `CODE_SIGN_ENTITLEMENTS`. `onOpenURL` and `onContinueUserActivity` hand an `/i/<token>` URL to `CircleStore.pendingInvite`; the root then opens the Circles tab and pushes the **join screen** (a pushed page, not a sheet: a sheet presented at a cold start from a link was dropped while the window settled). Signed out it shows the sign-in buttons first; then the circle's name, who invited and the size, and **Join circle**; joined (or already in) it opens the circle's page. Expired, revoked, full and unknown invitations say so.
- **QR codes** are Core Image's generator at correction level M, scaled by whole pixels; a test reads the code back with `CIDetector` and gets the link.
- **Debug flags**: `-fakeCircles empty|some` (with `-fakeAccount signedOut|signedIn`); `-autoScreen circles|circle|circle-invite|circle-new|join` and `-autoInvite <token>` (`fake-invite`, `expired`, `full`). Screenshots of every state are in `docs/screenshots/61/`.
- **Tests**: backend pytest (create, list, names and time zones, tokens required, hidden from non-members; invites by any member, seven-day expiry to the second, the ttl; accept, idempotent accept, several friends on one link; the 21st refused and a place reopening; ten friends racing for five places; leaving, joined and left times, rejoining, the creator's handoff, the last member; removal by the creator only, not themselves, the old invite refused after removal; several circles per account; the creator and the heir leaving at once, a double leave; `INVITE_BASE_URL`); infra pytest (`tests/test_invite_site.py`: aliases and the certificate, TLS, HTTPS, the private bucket and its policy, Retain, the rewrite function run in node over nine paths, the association file's JSON, content type and pruning, the API's `INVITE_BASE_URL`, the table's TTL, tags, outputs, a missing or non-us-east-1 certificate refused); app `CircleTests` (the client's requests and decoding, every refusal's mapping, invite link parsing, the QR round trip, the store over the fake, a 401 renewed and retried); UI `CirclesUITests` (signed out the tab offers sign-in; a created circle is listed and opens; the invite's QR code carries the link shown under it; an invite link opens the join screen and joining opens the circle).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy, the certificate and the two SiteGround records (they wait on the go-ahead and the wizard), and on a device: an invite link from Messages opening the join screen, the fallback page in a browser, and a phone camera scanning the QR code to the same link.

## Lore and captions (issue #56) — built 2026-11-01; Los Angeles lore approved and published in v8
- **The fact sheet** (`packbuilder/factsheet.py`, the `fact` table): up to eight sentences of the species' Wikipedia article at the pinned revision (the description's source, so a fully hand-written description takes no article facts), then its stats told as short sentences, each credited to its dataset ("It nests in a half cup or a cup." from BIRDBASE; the IUCN category as "The IUCN Red List rates it Least Concern."). The spec's "stats plus up to about eight short facts" read as eight from the article on top of the stats, about 15 a species. The article's come first because they are the richer: AVONET's "rock" habitat and BIRDBASE's "cup" are coarse beside "always near water" and "cemented in its place with mud". The sentences are picked by rule: the article's first (it names the bird), then sentences about how the bird lives (feeding, nesting, voice, movement), then where it lives, then neutral ones (a range, a date), then naming and classification; within each tier the sections are taken in turn (split at every heading, so Breeding under Behaviour counts as breeding; the lead, behaviour and diet, breeding, voice, people, names, range, status, appearance), at most two from one kind besides the lead, and a sentence that mostly repeats one taken is left out. A sentence fits when it stands alone (6 to 32 words, whole, no measurements, not opening on "This", "However", "Both" and the like). Facts are ids `f1`, `f2`, ... in sheet order. The first rule (one fitting sentence of each kind in turn, classification included) gave the Black Phoebe "the largest family of birds on earth" and three range sentences, and none of "always near water", sallying from a perch or the mud nest the lead holds; the maintainer's review of the trial asked for those, and the tiers find them.
- **Lore** (`packbuilder/lore.py`): 50 to 80 words and a tagline of 12 words or fewer, written by **`claude-opus-5-5`** through the Messages API with structured output (a JSON schema of sentences, each with the fact ids it rests on, and the tagline with its own), adaptive thinking at effort high, and the server-side refusal fallback (`fallbacks: "default"`). Opus over Sonnet: the voice is the point and the job is small (one request a species, a few dollars for a pack), so the cheaper model buys nothing worth having. The system prompt (prompts v2, after the maintainer read the first trial) asks for a small myth, a "why it does this" tale built around one or two things the bird does, leading with how it lives; the article's facts over the datasets' coarse labels, which are used only where the article says nothing; at most one number in the lore and none in the tagline (checked); and no new claims: no behaviour, food, colour, number, place, season or sound of the model's own, no gods, peoples or real people the sheet doesn't name, plain words. The legend lives in the framing. A "why" tale joins two true facts by a cause the article doesn't state ("it never strays from water: it needs the mud"); the maintainer reads every one in the review. Trial v1 (72 words: the family's size, weight and wingspan, "rocky places") read like a list of facts; v2's phoebe tells of the mud nest under a bridge, the sally from a perch and the tail.
  - **The checks**, at the build: every sentence and the tagline cite at least one fact, every cited id is on the sheet, each sentence is one sentence, the word counts hold, and the lore has at most one number (figures or words; "twenty-six" is one) and the tagline none. A failing draft goes back with what was wrong, three drafts at most; a species with none is logged and has no lore. A locked entry edited by hand that fails them stops the build.
  - **The key** is `BIRDJOURNAL_ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, else the login Keychain item `birdjournal-anthropic-api-key` (a shell started before the profile line has no variable), passed to the client explicitly (not `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, which would switch Claude Code itself to API billing), read only when a request is due; never logged or written. A key the API refuses (401) or any other API error the SDK doesn't retry stops the run at once with a message that never holds the key, and nothing unfinished is locked. The step is paced (one request at a time, two seconds apart). `packbuilder lore <definition>` runs the fact sheets and the lore alone, from the cache, without building the pack.
  - **The lock** (`packbuilder/packs/<id>/lore.lock.json`, committed) pins per species the fact sheet as the lore used it, the sentences with their citations, the tagline, the model, the prompt version and the review (pending, approved, rejected with the reason). A warm lock makes no request and needs no key. The pack ships the locked facts, so lore and fact sheet never disagree; a sheet that moved since (a new Wikipedia revision) is logged and the locked one kept. Lore is written again only on request: `--regenerate-lore <species>|all`, or a rejection, whose reason goes into the next prompt.
  - **The review**: every build writes `build/lore/<id>/review.md` (species, state, tagline, lore, and each sentence with the facts it cites); `packbuilder lore-review` approves or rejects. Only approved lore ships; the rest is listed under `unreviewed.lore` in `report.json`, and such a pack is not ready to publish.
  - **License**: CC BY-SA 4.0 as an adaptation of the Wikipedia text; the pack LICENSE names the model and credits the article revisions under "Text credits". The `lore.sources` column lists the sources of the cited facts (the revision link and dataset keys).
- **Captions** (app, `Journal/Captions.swift`): after each add, never blocking it, `Captioner` asks a `CaptionWriter` for one line of 18 words or fewer and stores it on the sighting with the writer's version (`apple-foundation-models caption@1`). The app's writer is Apple's on-device Foundation Models with guided generation (a `@Generable` draft); the tests use a fake.
  - **The prompt** carries only the species' name, its fact sheet and the moment: the season (meteorological, from the month and the hemisphere of the latitude; left out without a location), the local time of day (early morning 5–8, morning, afternoon 12–17, evening 17–21, night), Heard or Seen, and the place label (the city the phone's reverse geocoding gives). No note, coordinate or photo.
  - **No place** for a threatened species, and none for a species whose pack doesn't say (no threatened row): unknown counts as threatened, the safe side.
  - **Once**: a sighting with a caption is never captioned again, so reopening a card changes nothing; changing Heard to Seen later keeps the caption. A writer that is unavailable (no Apple Intelligence, model not ready) or fails, or two drafts over 18 words, leaves no caption, and the card shows the lore's tagline. Sightings added before captions, or while the model was unavailable, keep the tagline; a backfill can come later.
- **Phone**: the front shows the caption (or the tagline) in italics under the names. The back gains a "Lore" section with the lore, its credit ("Written by Claude (Anthropic) from this bird's fact sheet and reviewed by hand. Its facts come from its English Wikipedia article (revision …) by Wikipedia contributors, AVONET and BIRDBASE. Lore text: CC BY-SA 4.0.") and a link to the revision, or "No lore for this bird in your packs yet." Credits gain "Lore and captions"; a bird's Sources & credits gains its lore's credit and revision.
- **Debug flags**: `-captionWriter fixed` (a fixed line, for simulators without Apple Intelligence) or `none`; the seeded Black Phoebe carries a caption.
- **The Los Angeles runs** (2026-11-01). Prompts v3: 160 of 160 in 192 requests, $5.86, but 120 of them framed as "Ask why" (72 opening with it), and the Northern House Wren told as resident all year from AVONET's row for the pre-split House Wren complex. The maintainer asked for v4 and a fix:
  - **Borrowed rows** (`stats/fixes.json` "borrowed", with reasons): species whose AVONET row is a wider species before a split, the four AVONET fixes to the species before a split and *Troglodytes aedon* (the complex keeps the name). Their fact sheets leave out AVONET's habitat and migration, which vary across the wider species' range; mass, diet and lifestyle stay. The card back still shows #55's stats for them; that is #55's to revisit.
  - **Prompts v4**: varied openings and framings, each sentence only what its cited facts say, a "why" only when signposted. The build caps one two-word opening, and "ask why" anywhere, at 15 % of the set (`OPENING_SHARE`), sends back a draft past it, and names the crowded openings in each request.
  - **Result**: 160 of 160 in 194 requests (34 sent back: 14 for numbers, 11 for length, 8 for two sentences under one citation, 1 tagline), $5.40; the American Robin failed three drafts on the numbers check and was written again alone. 121 distinct openings, the commonest "in the" (10); no "ask why". About $11.37 spent on lore in all, trials included. All pending the maintainer's sign-off in the issue.
  - **Open question for #55**: BIRDBASE's nest type "other_nest", read as a brood parasite ("lays its eggs in other birds' nests"), is on the Northern House Wren, which nests in cavities and old nests; its lore repeats it. Answered in #86: "other_nest" is a reused nest, and brood parasites come from BIRDBASE's parasitism columns.
- **Los Angeles**: not rebuilt here. Its lore lock is filled and reviewed in this ticket; the pack is rebuilt and published once after #57 and #56 merge, as v7 with the art and the lore. `PackLoreTests`' check of every Los Angeles species runs from that version.
- **Tests**: pytest `test_factsheet.py` (the stats sentences and a known gap, the article's sentences across kinds, the cap of eight, the numbering) and `test_lore.py` against a stub client (the citation and word-count checks, a draft sent back with its problems, no lore after three failures, a warm lock making no request and left byte-identical, a moved sheet keeping the locked facts, regeneration only on request and a rejection's reason in the prompt, a hand-edited lock stopping the build, the key read only from its own variable, the review file, and a rebuild through `build_pack` shipping the same `fact` and `lore` rows); `PackLoreTests` (the fixture's facts and lore, a schema-3 pack without them, every Los Angeles species once v7); app `CaptionTests` (the prompt is the fact sheet and the moment and nothing else, no place for threatened or unknown species, stored once and not written again, the tagline fallback when unavailable, failing or too long, no fact sheet no caption, the seasons by hemisphere, an add captioned through the run ledger, the lore credit).
- **Los Angeles v8** (2026-11-01): the maintainer approved all 160 lore entries (prompt v4). Rebuilt from the shared cache with nothing repainted and no API calls (`pack art: 0 painted`, `lore: 0 requests`): 160 species, 785 photos, 266 sounds, 160 paintings, 160 lore entries; 197 MB on disk against the 200 MB budget, the zip 191.5 MB. Released as `pack-us-ca-la-v8`, pinned and published in the index. Lore cost about $11.37 in all, across the trials and the v3 and v4 runs.

## Finds in the circle feed: automatic sharing, private, a secret spot, first for the circle (issue #62) — built 2026-11-01, deploy, thumbnail upload and the two-device check pending
- **The find policy** (`Share/FindPolicy.swift`, pure, beside the share policy): an add publishes only when signed in and sharing (`publishesAutomatically`); private gives nothing. The payload is the species (BirdNET label, scientific and common names), Heard or Seen, the place, the time to the minute in UTC (`2026-11-01T08:15:00Z`, seconds dropped), the Legendary and rarity flags, `threatened`, and the art reference (a pack-art thumbnail's key). Never a coordinate, the note, the caption or a frame: the encoded JSON has exactly those ten keys (tested). The place is the city `PlaceNames` gives, else none; a label that looks like a coordinate (the album's fallback) is none. **A threatened species, or one no pack rates** (unknown counts as threatened, as for captions, #56), is always at "a secret spot". The Legendary flag is the add's and frozen there: Legendary passing to another card later publishes nothing (spec). Extras of a card shared by hand are #66's.
- **"Share my finds automatically"** (Settings → Account, under the account row; `shareFindsAutomatically` in UserDefaults, on when unset). Off is private: later adds stay on the phone, and turning it back on doesn't publish them. Finds already shared stay until the birder removes the sighting (my choice: the spec only says off is private; deleting everything on a switch felt too destructive to do silently). A published find's Heard-to-Seen still updates while private, as a correction.
- **The outbox** (`Circles/FindOutbox.swift`, `@MainActor`): one entry per sighting, the latest change winning (a put then a delete before either went is a delete; a removal undone is a put), persisted as JSON in Application Support (`FindOutbox.json`; the debug in-memory album keeps it in tmp). It remembers which sightings each account published (kept after a delete, so an Undo after the delete went puts the find back), so Heard or Seen (`JournalEdits.setMethod`) puts again, a removal (the journal's, at once rather than when the undo window closes, so a quit in the window can't leave the find behind; and undoing an add in a run) deletes, and Undo puts back. **Every entry belongs to the account (`sub`, now read from the ID token) that published it**: signed out nothing goes, and signing in as another account never sends the first one's; signing back in as the same one sends what waited. The payload is built when the entry is sent, through the policy, from the sighting as it is then, so an offline add carries the city once the phone can name it; a sighting gone by then drops its put. It sends after each change, on sign-in, when `NWPathMonitor` says the network is back, when the app becomes active, and on its own retry. Failures back off 2, 4, 8 … seconds up to five minutes; refusals a retry can't fix (422, 404, other 4xx but 408 and 429) drop the entry with a log line. One drain at a time; a caller during one waits for it.
- **On the server** (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/finds.py`, `finds_api.py`): `PUT /v1/finds/{sighting id}` (a UUID, stored lower-case; 201 new, 200 updated), `DELETE /v1/finds/{sighting id}` (204, also when there's nothing to delete), `GET /v1/circles/{id}/feed?limit=1..50 (default 20)&cursor=` (current members only, else 404; `next_cursor` opaque). Items in the single table, beside the circles':
  - `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `FIND#<sighting id>`: the find, `created_at`, `updated_at`, and `circle_ids`, the circles it went to (finds by owner; keyed by owner so two accounts' ids never meet).
  - `CIRCLE#<id>#FEED` / `<found at minute>#<sub>#<sighting id>`: a feed item per circle, the find's copy with its owner, read newest first. **Partitions of their own**, not under `CIRCLE#<id>`, so #61's meta-and-members queries never page through finds.
  - `CIRCLE#<id>#SPECIES` / `<scientific name>#<found at>#<sub>#<sighting id>`: the same items by species then time.
  - `FIND#<sub>#<sighting id>` / `REACTION#…`: **reactions go here (#63)**; a delete removes the whole partition.
  
  **No index** (no GSI): every access pattern is a key or a prefix. Fan-out reads the owner's current circles (`ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `CIRCLE#…`) on the **first** put only; a repeated put updates the circles recorded then and no others, so joining never backfills, not even through an update, and leaving keeps the items (a later delete still takes them out of a circle the owner left). A put that moves the time or species deletes the old items first. Not one transaction (an account can be in more circles than a transaction holds): a put writes the find, with its circles, before its feed items, and a delete removes the items before the find, so the find always names every circle that may hold an item of it; a half-done put or delete is finished by the outbox's retry (into the circles first named, even one left since), and every write is idempotent. The feed's owner names are read with the page, so a rename shows at once.
- **What the server refuses to hold**: any field beyond the policy's (`extra="forbid"`: `latitude`, `location`, `coordinate`, `note` are 422 and nothing is written); a place with a decimal number of two or more decimals, a degree sign or minutes after a number (`34 8' N`); over 80 characters or a control character. `threatened: true` stores "a secret spot" whatever place came. Art keys must be the art CDN's, of their kind (`packs/…` for `pack`, `art/…` for `painted`), `.jpg`/`.png`/`.webp`, no `..`. A time without a zone is 422; seconds are dropped.
- **First for the circle** is read with the feed (spec: "computed at read time"): for each species on the page, one query of `CIRCLE#<id>#SPECIES` with `begins_with(<scientific name>#)`, `Limit 1`, oldest first; the earliest surviving item is first, ties broken by owner and sighting id. So a delete passes the mark on with nothing to recompute, an offline find published later from earlier in the day takes it, and it is per circle and apart from Legendary. The push decision at write time (#63) is the same query.
- **The art CDN** is #68's `ArtCdn` (`infra/birdjournal_infra/art.py`, cherry-picked from `feature/68-scene-art` as one commit so the branches share it): one private bucket behind one CloudFront distribution on its default `*.cloudfront.net` domain (no certificate, no DNS), origin access control, Retain; `art/` for painted art, **`packs/` for the pack-art thumbnails**. The Lambda gets `ART_CDN_BASE_URL`, and the feed gives each item's `art` with `url = <base>/<key>` (null before the stack has one). One CDN, not a prefix on the invite distribution: the invite bucket's page deployment prunes, and the art needs neither the invite domain's certificate nor its rewrite.
- **Pack-art thumbnails**: `packbuilder thumbnails <pack dir> --out <dir>` writes one JPEG per `pack_art` row, at most 320 px on the long side (the feed shows 64 points), quality 82, no metadata; Los Angeles v8 gives 160 at about 20 KB each (3.2 MB). `scripts/publish-pack-thumbnails.sh <id>` (step 5 of `packs/README.md`'s publishing) uploads them with `aws s3 sync` to the stack's `ArtBucket` under `packs/<id>/<species id>.jpg`, `Cache-Control: public, max-age=86400`, deleting nothing. The key has no version: a repainted species reaches old finds within a day, and a species a later version drops keeps its picture. The phone's key (`FindPolicy.packArtKey`) is the same rule over `pack_art.file`; the pack is the set's pack first (`PackLibrary.packArt`), as the card does.
- **The circle feed** (Circles tab → a circle): "Recent finds" above the invite button, 20 at a time with **More finds**; each row: the art (the CDN's thumbnail, else the species' reference photo from an installed pack, else a typographic tile on the card face), the common name, "Heard by Maya Ortiz" ("by you" for one's own), "Pasadena · 2 hours ago" or "At a secret spot · …", and the marks Legendary (gold), First for the circle, and Rare or Exceptional. Pull to refresh reloads it; sign-out clears it. `FakeCircleClient` fans out and pages like the server, and the seeded Sunday Group has four friends' finds (`-fakeCircles some -autoScreen circle`).
- **Tests**: backend pytest `test_finds.py` (55 cases: fan-out to every circle and only the owner's; a find with no circles; no backfill on join, not even by a repeated put; past items kept after leaving and still removed by a later delete; a repeated put updating without a duplicate and keeping the first put's circles; a moved time moving the item; a put cut short finished into the circles it first named; owner-scoped ids; the delete cascade to feed items, species entries and reactions in every circle; idempotent and owner-only deletes; circle first, after a delete, per circle apart from Legendary, and an older find published later; 23 finds in three pages newest first; members only, bad cursors and limits; art URLs with and without the CDN; names read with the feed; the secret spot stored for threatened species; coordinates and extra fields refused; city names kept; the minute in UTC; art keys; malformed finds; the UUID; tokens); packbuilder `test_thumbnails.py` (the key, size, no EXIF, never enlarged, packs without art, the command); app `FindPolicyTests` (automatic versus private, the payload, the secret spot for threatened and unrated species, no coordinate even from a coordinate label and exactly ten keys, the minute, nulls, the art key), `FindOutboxTests` (an offline add published once online and once, a delete after publishing, signing out stops and signing back in sends, another account never sends the first one's, private adds never published, a repeated put with the add's Legendary flag, a removal undone before sending, a relaunch, backoff and a refused put dropped, a gone sighting), `CircleFeedTests` (the HTTP put, delete and feed with its query and decoding, refusals; the row's byline, place and marks; the store's feed over the fake, an add appearing in every circle and leaving on delete, paging); UI `FindsUITests` (a circle's Recent finds with their marks in order; the setting on by default, off says private).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy (the art CDN and the Lambda's new environment; it waits on the maintainer's go-ahead with #68's), the thumbnail upload for Los Angeles (after the deploy: `scripts/publish-pack-thumbnails.sh us-ca-la`), and on two devices with two accounts, an add on one appearing in the other's feed.

## Nest reuse and brood parasites (issue #86) — built 2026-11-01; Los Angeles v9 reviewed and published
- **What BIRDBASE's `O` means**: its Legend sheet gives Nest_Type `O` as "other bird's nest", beside `NO` "no nest", and its Nest Details sheet splits it out as `NestType_O`. It says where the eggs go, not who raises the young: in the packs it is on owls, falcons, the White-tailed Kite, the Solitary Sandpiper, the Mallard and other birds that take over an old nest another bird built. Across BIRDBASE only the Common Cuckoo and one honeyguide, among 233 species with `O`, are brood parasites. #55 read it as a brood parasite, so from Los Angeles v6 to v8 every `O` species' fact sheet said "It lays its eggs in other birds' nests". The card back said "other birds' nests" and the Northern House Wren's lore told it. Now `other_nest` is **an old nest another bird built**: the fact sheet says "It nests in a cavity, an old nest another bird built or a crevice." and the card back says "another bird's old nest".
- **Brood parasitism has its own field**, BIRDBASE's "Nest Parasitism" columns: `Para.1` 1 = nest parasite, `Para.2` 1 = victim of nest parasitism; a species that lays in nests of its own kind gets 1 in both (the Legend's note), which is most ducks. A **brood parasite** (`stats.brood_parasite`) has `Para.1` 1, `Para.2` not 1, and no nest of its own (its nest types, if any, only `NO` and `O`). Across BIRDBASE that gives 103 species, every one of them a cuckoo, whydah or indigobird, honeyguide, cowbird, or the Black-headed Duck. It leaves out the California Quail (1 and 0, but it scrapes a nest). It misses the Screaming Cowbird, which the Shiny Cowbird parasitizes, so it is 1 and 1; no pack has it. A brood parasite's nest is `brood_parasite` alone, in place of BIRDBASE's types. Its fact is "It is a brood parasite: it lays its eggs in other birds' nests, and they raise its young." and its card back says "Lays in other birds' nests (a brood parasite)". An app from before this change drops a nest value it doesn't know, so on v9 it shows the cowbird and whydah with no Nest line (their fact sheet and lore still tell it). `no_nest` with other types now tells them too ("It lays its eggs without building a nest, or nests in a scrape on the ground or an old nest another bird built.", the Turkey Vulture).
- **The `O` species of the five packs** (19), none a brood parasite: White-throated Swift (desert, LA), Egyptian Goose (LA, Orlando), Mallard (all five), Long-eared Owl (desert), Great Horned Owl (all), Turkey Vulture (all), Dark-eyed Junco (bay, desert, LA, SD), White-tailed Kite (bay, SD), Merlin (desert), Prairie Falcon (desert), Peregrine Falcon (all), American Kestrel (all), Eastern Screech Owl (Orlando), Western Screech Owl (bay, LA, SD), Common Merganser (bay), Barred Owl (Orlando), Violet-green Swallow (bay), Solitary Sandpiper (Orlando), Northern House Wren (LA, SD, Orlando). All are known to use old nests or holes other birds made; the swift (old House Finch nests) and the junco (rarely) only now and then, which the card's "or" list allows. The packs' **brood parasites** are the Brown-headed Cowbird (all five) and the Pin-tailed Whydah (LA). BIRDBASE codes both `NO`, so before this they read "no nest". **No fixes** were needed in `stats/fixes.json`: the mapping was wrong, not the data. The other four packs take the change when #58 rebuilds them.
- **A changed fact in the lore lock** (`lore.py`): the stats sentences are the datasets' own, so a lock's dataset facts are matched to the current sheet's by text. A fact the lore or tagline cites that is gone from the sheet sends that entry back to be written from the new sheet, and it comes back pending. A fact it doesn't cite is swapped for the new sentence under the same id (a gone one with no new sentence is dropped, extra new ones get fresh ids), and the lore and its review stay. A rewrite that fails its three drafts leaves the species without lore, as a regeneration on request would: its old lore cites a fact that is no longer true. The article's facts stay as locked, as before: a Wikipedia revision that moved is logged and changes nothing until a regeneration. The review file now names the pending species at the top.
- **Los Angeles v9**: 9 entries took the new nest sentence without being rewritten (Mallard, Dark-eyed Junco, Great Horned Owl, Turkey Vulture, American Kestrel, Egyptian Goose, Peregrine Falcon, White-throated Swift, Western Screech Owl). Three cited the changed fact and were written again in 3 requests ($0.10): the Northern House Wren, the Pin-tailed Whydah and the Brown-headed Cowbird. The maintainer approved all three. Rebuilt from the shared cache with nothing repainted and no API calls at the build (`pack art: 0 painted`, `lore: 0 requests`): 160 species, 785 photos, 266 sounds, 160 paintings and 160 lore entries, as in v8; 193.2 MB on disk against the 200 MB budget, the zip 191.5 MB. Released as `pack-us-ca-la-v9`, pinned and published in the index.
- **Tests**: pytest `test_stats.py` (the House Wren's `CV,O,CR` stays a reused nest, the cowbird and the Common Cuckoo are brood parasites, the Mallard, the California Quail and the Maccoa Duck are not; from the cached BIRDBASE: the wren, cowbird, whydah and Great Horned Owl, and the packs' only brood parasites being the cowbird and the whydah), `test_factsheet.py` (the reuse and brood-parasite sentences, `no_nest` with other types), `test_lore.py` (a cited changed fact rewrites only that entry, an uncited one is swapped in and the review kept, refreshing drops and appends); `PackStatsTests` (the bundled House Wren's nests and told text, the bundled pack's parasites; from v9); app `CardStatsTests` (the card back's wording, the bundled House Wren's back; from v9).

## The tip jar and listening test flakes (issue #93) — built 2026-11-01

- **The tip jar's "timeout" was a sandbox sign-in, not load.** A simulator's StoreKit uses an `SKTestSession`'s configuration (`BirdJournal.storekit`) only in processes launched after the first session registered it (in StoreKit's `group.com.apple.storekit` container, `…/Octane/com.matthewcelia.mybirdjournal/Configuration.storekit`). So the first purchase in a new simulator's first test host goes to the sandbox and waits on "Sign in to Apple Account" until xcodebuild's 180 s allowance; the restarted host then passes, which made it look like a load flake. Every field failure (#88, #57, #61) was the first run with the tip jar on a new `BJ-<n>` simulator; on an erased simulator it hung 4 of 4 times, alone, at load 18. Waiting in the same host (15 s) does not help, nor does a per-test session reset or not, nor a StoreKit configuration on the scheme's Test action (xcodebuild ignores it).
- **The fix is a primer, not a change to the tip jar**: `scripts/build-and-test.sh` checks the simulator for the registered configuration (`storekit_config_registered` in `scripts/lib/simulator.sh`) and, when it is missing, runs `TipJarTests/loadsThreeTips()` once as the `storekit-prime` run before the app runs (one more test host launch, about 15 s, once per simulator; every later run skips it). A failed primer is a warning, not a failed run: the runs below still go ahead. If StoreKit ever moves that file, the check says no every time and each run just primes again. Running the tip jar tests from Xcode on a brand-new simulator can still hang once; run anything that creates the session first, or use the script.
- **TipJarTests wait on nothing timed**: the Ask to Buy test awaits the outcome through `Observations` instead of polling 50 × 100 ms, and the suite has a one-minute time limit so any future wait that never ends fails in a minute, not three. With the hang gone, the first load loop (load 570–860) showed two races #59's checks had hidden: once `Transaction.unfinished` still listed the tip `TipJar` had just awaited `finish()` on, and once `session.allTransactions()` held one tip after two purchases. StoreKit's daemon and the session's record catch up a moment after the calls return, and neither has an event to await (`SKTestTransaction` has no finished state), so those reads go through `settles`, which re-reads until they hold with no deadline of its own: a tip that is never finished still fails, at the suite's time limit, naming what never settled. The shared session and its per-test reset stay as #59 left them (the experiments ruled them out).
- **The listening "window count" flake was an ordering race, not a clock**: the engine yields a window's `.window` report before the `.stack` it changed, and `playbackIsNotHeard` read the stack as soon as `windowsScored` reached 3, sometimes between the two (it saw 2). The count itself already runs on the fed audio's sample time (`ManualAudioSource` and the engine's window starts), so no clock seam was needed: the test now waits for the stack it asserts on, and checks the final stack after Stop, which drains the engine. `waitUntil` has no 5 s wall-clock deadline any more; the suite's one-minute time limit ends a wait that never comes true, and the cancellation names the wait.

## Scene art end to end (issue #68) — built 2026-11-01, retired by #137 before it was verified on the glasses
The bird painted into the glasses photo from the add (ADR-0003, ADR-0007), for a signed-in birder.
- **The lifecycle** (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/art.py`, `art_api.py`). The phone makes the job's UUID, so a create repeated after a lost response is the same job. The auto-paint rule decides first, so a photo never goes up for a job the server would refuse. Then:
  - `awaiting_upload`, with a presigned PUT (SigV4, signed for `image/jpeg`, an hour) for `uploads/<sub>/<job id>.jpg`;
  - submit: the photo must be there (`409 upload_missing` otherwise); the job is `queued` and goes on SQS once;
  - the worker's claim (`painting`; a redelivered message claims again and counts the attempt; `409` for a finished job, `410` for an expired one);
  - `ready` (the art at the job's own `art/<sub>/<24 random bytes>.jpg`, any other key `409 wrong_output`) or `failed` with a reason (`filtered`, `no_reference`, `painting_failed`, `bad_input`). Either way the upload is deleted at once; the bucket's one-day lifecycle is the backstop;
  - `received`: the phone has stored the result. Painted art is then deleted from the art bucket unless something shared shows it (`ArtRetention.is_shown`, `NothingShared` until finds carry art: **the hook for #70**), and the job leaves the open list (`ttl` 30 days later).
- **Expiry** is lazy: any read, the open list or a claim fails a job not finished 7 days after it was queued (created, if never submitted) with `expired`. That covers a job stuck `painting` after its message went to the dead-letter queue too (the issue says "still queued"; a job the box choked on five times is no better). No scheduler. The queue keeps a message 7 days to match.
- **A retry** is a new job for the same sighting with a new key, so the old painting stays where it is until the new one lands. The server never touches the sighting, which lives on the phone only (ADR-0004).
- **The auto-paint rule is server configuration**: `artAutoPaint` in `infra/cdk.json`, the Lambda's `ART_AUTO_PAINT`. `every_glasses_add` (the friends phase) paints every add with a glasses photo by a signed-in birder; `legendary_or_rare` (the public launch) paints Legendary and Rare-or-rarer adds, and requested ones (Try again, Retry art), whose daily allowance comes with #69. Refused: `409 not_eligible`; the card keeps its pack art.
- **The worker token** is a 48-character secret generated in Secrets Manager at deploy (`birdjournal/art-worker-token`). The Lambda reads it once an hour; the wizard copies it to the box. Worker routes (`/internal/art/jobs/<owner>/<id>/claim|ready|failed`) take only it, never an access token. The owner is in the path because the queue message names it: jobs are `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `ARTJOB#<id>`, so a birder's jobs are one query.
- **The silent push waits for #63**, which registers device tokens and holds the APNs key. `ArtNotifier.job_finished` is called on every ready, failure and expiry (`NoNotifier` for now). Until then the phone polls: on launch, on coming back to the app, and every 30 s while a pending card is open.
- **Infra** (`infra/birdjournal_infra/art.py`, `art_jobs.py`):
  - **The one art CDN**, agreed with #62: `ArtCdn`, a private bucket (Retain) behind CloudFront with origin access control, on CloudFront's own domain (no certificate, no DNS). Prefixes: `art/` painted art, `packs/` finds' pack-art thumbnails (#62, #70). Output `ArtCdnDomain`; the Lambda's `ART_CDN_BASE_URL` and `ART_BUCKET`. Keep these names: #62 builds on them.
  - The upload bucket: private, SSL only, objects expire after a day.
  - SQS with SSE and a dead-letter queue: 15-minute visibility, 20 s long polls, 5 receives, 7 days' retention (the DLQ 14).
  - The worker's IAM user, `birdjournal-art-worker`: consume the queue, `s3:GetObject` on `uploads/*`, `s3:PutObject` on `art/*`, nothing else. **No access key in the stack**: a key there would sit in a secret anyone with stack access reads. The wizard makes one and writes it straight to the box.
  - The API may presign and delete uploads, delete painted art, send to the queue and read the token. It never reads painted art: CloudFront serves it.
  - The template tests check all of it.
- **The worker** (`artworker/`, its own uv project over `packbuilder` as an editable path dependency, so `packbuilder.art` finds `art/`). It long-polls one message at a time and claims with the token. It paints with the committed `scene-art` workflow and `prompts.json`'s scene template: the edition's light from `editions` (Los Angeles and San Diego so far; a card in any other set, or none, gets a neutral "soft daylight of the photograph"), and the Legendary light for a Legendary card. The steps:
  - **The reference bird**: `<ref_image_0>` is the species' isolated reference bird, the one its approved pack art was painted from. `packbuilder art-references` exports them from the pack build's cache with each bird's length (`overrides.json`'s `art.length_cm`, else the pack's size text): all 160 for Los Angeles, about 41 MB. The cache names a bird by the seed its isolation started from, and the lock records the seed that won, so the export matches on the JSON beside each bird. The worker takes the card's own pack's bird first, else any pack's; a species with none fails `no_reference` (a pack art painted from text has no reference to give).
  - **The crop**: `<ref_image_1>` is `telephoto_crop` of the photo, zoomed for the bird's length around the photo's centre, where the wearer aimed the viewfinder. Glasses photos are 1080 × 1440 in-stream frames for now (#46), so a finch's 12× crop is about 90 px wide: soft. The painting softens the place anyway, and full-resolution capture (being changed on `lightsailvr/glasses-full-resolution-photos`) will sharpen it with no change here.
  - **No prompt names the species**: the template takes no names, the worker passes empty ones and refuses a prompt containing the scientific name, and a test checks every graph sent.
  - **The fidelity gate**, as pack art's: the painted bird against the reference bird, ΔE ≤ 10 and IoU ≥ 0.65 after the colour fit, three seeds at most. The seed is the job id's CRC32, so Try again paints a new picture. Pack art keeps its closest for review, but scene art has no reviewer, so **a painting that never passes fails the job** (`painting_failed`) and the card keeps its pack art, with Retry art. The fitted painting is the card art, a JPEG at quality 88, 864 × 1152, `Cache-Control: immutable`.
  - **The content filter** (ADR-0007's condition): Falconsai/nsfw_image_detection (Apache 2.0) as onnx-community's ONNX export, revision- and SHA-256-pinned (`artworker/scripts/download-filter.sh`, 343 MB), on the CPU. A painting with an nsfw probability above 0.5 fails `filtered` and never reaches the bucket. The model's provenance in `provenance`, which the API keeps with the job, names FLUX.3 Image Klein, the workflow, the prompts version, the reference photo, the seed and the fidelity.
  - **Not now**: the worker checks that ComfyUI answers before each poll, so while it is down (crashed, restarting) no message is taken and no job spends its five receives toward the dead-letter queue. A painting that fails mid-way, the API not answering or S3 refusing leaves the message, visible again after 5 minutes. A job the API calls finished, expired or unknown is dropped.
  - **A photo older than a day**: the upload bucket keeps one day (the issue's rule), a queued job 7. A claim whose photo is gone sends the job back to `awaiting_upload` (`409 upload_expired`; the worker drops the message), and the phone, which keeps the original, uploads it again on its next refresh. The job keeps its first queueing time, so its 7 days don't restart.
  - **A painting reported after its job expired** is deleted by the API (`409`), so nothing is left in the Retain art bucket that no one will fetch.
  - **On comfybox**: `birdjournal-artworker.service` (after `comfyui-flux3.service`, `Restart=always`, the settings in `~/birdjournal-art/artworker.env`, mode 600). `artworker/comfybox/deploy-to-comfybox.sh` copies `art/`, `packbuilder/`, `artworker/` (the repo's layout) and the reference birds, installs with `uv sync --frozen` and fetches the filter. `install-artworker-service.sh` runs with sudo. **Written, not run**: the install, the IAM user and the deploy wait on the maintainer's go-ahead.
  - **The art wizard** (`infra/scripts/art-wizard.sh`, 6 stages, `WIZARD_FROM` resumes, the circles wizard's library): export the references; deploy; make the worker's access key and write the key, the stack's outputs and the token to the box over ssh (nothing on the Mac's disk); copy the worker; install the service; check the log says it polls.
- **The phone** (`BirdJournal/Art/`):
  - **`ArtPhoto.prepare`**: Vision's face rectangles, each grown by 35 % a side and blurred past recognition (a coarse pixellate under a Gaussian, feathered by a radial mask). The picture is turned upright and written as a new JPEG from the pixels alone, so no GPS, time, device or orientation tag can carry over. The original on the phone is untouched.
  - **`ArtJobs`**: after an add (`RunSightings`) with a glasses photo by a signed-in birder, it waits out the add's undo window (7 s) so an undone add never sends a photo. Then it writes the job to the sighting **before** any network call, so a pending card survives a killed app, no connection or the box off for days. Then create, prepare the photo (off the main actor), PUT, submit. `refresh()` moves every pending job on: it creates one the server never got, uploads one whose upload was lost, lands a ready one (download, store beside the frames, delete the replaced painting, `received`) and fails a failed one (`received`). It also acknowledges finished jobs no card waits on (an undone add, a deleted card), so their art leaves the server. Nothing in it inserts a sighting: Try again and Retry art change the one sighting's `art`, so **a retry never duplicates the sighting**.
  - **`CardArt` transitions** (Album, `CardArtJobs.swift`): start (attempts count up), painting, ready (replaces the scene art and shows it, even after "Use pack art": a painting asked for again is one the birder wants to see), failed, dropped, Keep current art; a result for a job the card no longer waits on changes nothing.
  - **The card**: under the art, "Your encounter is saved. Artwork is on its way." while a job is pending (the last painting stays meanwhile) and "The artwork couldn't be painted this time." after a failure. Under the card: **Try again** and **Use pack art** on a painting, **Use painted art** to go back, **Retry art** and **Keep current art** after a failure. Asking for a painting needs the account; choosing between the painting and pack art doesn't. Labels and provenance are #57's ("AI illustration", FLUX.3 Image Klein on the back).
  - Gated like the circles: `ArtJobs.isAvailable` needs an art server (the account's) and a signed-in birder, so Release builds (`ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` off) send nothing.
- **Debug flags**: `-fakeArt paints|pending|fails` (the in-memory art server; `-fakeAccount` alone paints) and `-autoArt pending|ready|failed` (the newest seeded card with a glasses photo; ready is a warm wash over its photo).
- **Tests**:
  - Backend pytest `test_art_jobs.py` (41): create and its upload, idempotent create, submit before and after the photo, claim, redelivery, ready, wrong key, failed, the worker token, expiry at 7 days to the second (queued, never uploaded, painting), the expired claim, retry and Try again as new jobs with new keys, received with deletion and the shown-by-a-find hook, ttl, the rule both ways, settings, the real presigner offline.
  - Infra pytest `test_art.py` (the CDN, uploads, the queue, the worker's grants and no access key, the token, the API's environment and grants, a bad rule refused).
  - Packbuilder `test_art_references.py`.
  - Artworker pytest (20) against packbuilder's stub ComfyUI, moto's in-process queue and buckets and a stub API: painted and reported, the inputs (the bird, the card-size crop, the seed), no species name in any prompt, the edition and Legendary light, a neutral edition with another pack's bird, no reference, filtered, three unfaithful paintings, ComfyUI down, the API gone or down, a missing or broken photo, a bad message, Try again's new seed, the cached reference cut-out; the real filter on benign pictures when downloaded.
  - App: `ArtPhotoTests` (the fixture's face found and gone after the blur, only the face changed, metadata stripped, orientation baked in, no face, unreadable bytes); `ArtJobsTests` (an add lands art on the same card with the prepared photo, nothing sent without an account, a photo or past an undo, pending surviving the box off, an unsent job sent once, an interrupted upload resumed, Try again keeping the last art, Retry art, expiry, Keep current art, Use pack art, the launch rule refusing, orphans acknowledged); `ArtClientTests`; Album `CardArtJobsTests`; UI `SceneArtUITests` (Use pack art and back, Try again pending with the painting kept, Retry art painting the same card, Keep current art, signed out, pending).
  - **The face fixture** is NASA's public-domain portrait of Sally K. Ride (S84-37256; `fixtures/README.md`).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy, the worker user's key and the install on comfybox (they wait on the go-ahead and `art-wizard.sh`); a real painting from the queue on the box; on the glasses, an add yielding scene art on the card, the pending state with the box off, and a retry leaving one card. BiRefNet's re-hosted weights still need their provenance confirmed (`art/README.md`), as #49 noted for this ticket.

## Pushes: notable finds now, the rest in a digest (issue #63) — built 2026-11-01, deploy, the APNs key and the two-device check pending
- **The decision** (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/pushes.py`): only a **new** find pushes (a repeated put sends nothing, as the spec says). Its recipients are the current members of the circles it went to, minus the owner (never pushed about their own finds), anyone who muted the owner, anyone for whom every one of those circles is muted, and anyone with no registered phone. A member in two of the circles hears once. **Notable** (Legendary, Rare or Exceptional, or the circle's first of the species in a circle the recipient hasn't muted) pushes at once to each of the recipient's phones: an alert (title "Maya Ortiz found a Black Phoebe", body the reasons and the place, "Legendary · First for Sunday Group · Pasadena"; a secret spot reads "At a secret spot") plus `content-available`, with `find`, the summary the lens banner (#67) reads: owner and name, sighting, species, method, place, minute, Legendary, rarity, `circle_first`, and `circle_ids`, only the recipient's own circles the find reached (a tap opens the first; other members' circles stay theirs). Circle first at write time is the feed's own query (`FindStore.is_circle_first`) run after the items are written, so an offline find published late from earlier in the day takes the first and pushes. Goal completion (also notable in the spec) arrived with #65 (below).
- **The digest**: any other find goes into `DIGEST` / `<recipient>#<friend>` (string sets of the sighting ids and of the circles they came through, and `first_at`), and the recipient's phones get a **silent push** (`content-available` only, `apns-push-type: background`, priority 5) so the feed refreshes. Every 15 minutes the flush sends each buffer whose recipient's last alert about that friend (`ACCOUNT#<recipient>` / `PUSHED#<friend>`, written by notable and digest pushes alike, not by silent ones) is two hours old or more, or never was. So a friend's first ordinary find of the day arrives within 15 minutes and later ones at most every two hours. The flush reads the finds back, so a find deleted meanwhile is left out and a buffer whose finds are all gone sends nothing; it drops the buffer, unsent, when by then the friend is muted, or every circle the finds came through is muted or left, or the recipient has no phone. Until the APNs key exists every send is skipped and buffers keep waiting (one digest each once the key is stored). The buffer takes out only the ids it read (a find buffered during the flush waits for the next).
  - **The words** (my reading of "Maya found 6 birds in Pasadena"): the number is **distinct species**, not finds (three Black Phoebes on one walk are one bird; the payload's `count` is the same number); one species is named with its article ("Maya found an Oak Titmouse in Ojai"). The place is the most common place label among the finds, a tie going to the latest; a secret spot never counts, and with no place the sentence ends at the birds. A friend without a display name is "A friend". `thread-id` `friend-<sub>` groups a friend's alerts; a digest's `apns-collapse-id` `digest-<sub>` replaces an unread older one.
  - **One partition** for every buffer, so the flush is one query (no scan, no index); the friends phase has a few hundred pairs at most. Shard it if that changes.
- **Devices** (`devices.py`): `PUT /v1/account/devices/{token}` with `{"environment": "sandbox"|"production"}` (204), `DELETE` the same (204). Items `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `DEVICE#<token>` (devices by account, with the environment) and `DEVICE#<token>` / `DEVICE` naming the holder: a phone signed into another account moves to it, so a phone never gets two accounts' pushes, and a stale sign-out from the old account leaves the new one's alone. Tokens are lower-case hex, 64 to 200 characters. Registration is a read and three writes, not a transaction: two accounts registering one phone's token at the same instant can't happen (one phone, one signed-in account). APNs' `410`, `BadDeviceToken`, `DeviceTokenNotForTopic` and `Unregistered` forget the device.
- **Mutes**: `GET /v1/mutes` (`{circles, friends}`), `PUT`/`DELETE /v1/circles/{id}/mute` (current members only, else 404) and `PUT`/`DELETE /v1/friends/{sub}/mute` (only someone the birder shares a circle with, else 404; oneself 422; unmuting always works). Items `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `MUTE#CIRCLE#<id>` and `MUTE#FRIEND#<sub>`. The friend routes sit under `/v1/friends/` rather than under a circle, since a friend mute holds across circles. Mutes suppress pushes only here; the spec's "hide content" goes with blocks (#71), and a muted circle's feed still shows its finds (the page says so).
- **Delivery** (`apns.py`): APNs over HTTP/2 (`httpx2[http2]`, pure Python wheels, so the uv bundle still builds without Docker), the provider token an ES256 JWT (`iss` team, `kid` key) made again every 50 minutes and once more on `ExpiredProviderToken`; the host by the device's environment. `PushSender` is the interface; the harness's `FakeApns` records what it was asked to send. No SNS, no Firebase. A failure never fails the put (logged). Sends are sequential inside the request: at most 19 friends with a phone or two each.
- **The key** lives in Secrets Manager as `birdjournal/apns` (`{"key_id","team_id","private_key"}`), created by **`infra/scripts/push-wizard.sh`** outside the stack like the sign-in secrets: the .p8 never passes through CloudFormation, the repo or `~/keys/birdjournal.env` (only the Key ID and Team ID go there). The API reads it on the first push and keeps it; until it exists pushes are skipped and the secret looked for again every five minutes, so a deploy before the wizard works and storing the key needs no redeploy. The wizard (5 stages, `WIZARD_FROM`, the accounts wizard's library): the key in the portal (Sandbox & Production, team scoped), storing it tagged, Push Notifications on the App ID, the deploy, and a check with a test push to a phone's token through the backend's own sender.
- **The schedule** (`infra/birdjournal_infra/pushes.py`): an EventBridge rule, `rate(15 minutes)`, invoking the API's function directly with `{"birdjournal_job": "flush_digests"}` (no retries: the next flush makes up a missed one). Not the spec's shared-secret HTTP route: only EventBridge may invoke the function that way, so there's no secret to keep and nothing internal on the public API. `handler.py` routes the job before Mangum; an unknown job raises. Same function and 10 s timeout as the API (a flush is a few reads per pair). The function's role may read `birdjournal/apns-??????` only; its environment gains `APNS_SECRET_NAME` and `APNS_TOPIC` (`apnsSecretName`, `apnsTopic` in `cdk.json`).
- **The app** (`Circles/PushCenter.swift`, `Circles/PushAppDelegate.swift`): `PushCenter` over a `NotificationAuthorizing` protocol (the system's, or the debug fake). **Permission**: the explanation alert ("Hear about your friends' finds", what pushes and what's bundled, and that mutes exist) comes after the birder **creates or joins a circle**, once per install (`pushPermissionExplained`, set once answered since #107), only while the system permission is undetermined; the system prompt follows "Turn on notifications". Never at launch or sign-in; on opening the tab or a circle page only as #107's one retry of an unanswered explanation. "Not now" and a denial are kept: the circle page's settings offer **Turn on notifications** (undetermined) or **Open notification settings** with a footer saying finds then arrive only in the feed (denied). The alert waits 0.6 s after the create sheet or join screen goes, since an alert raised while a sheet dismisses is dropped. Existing members who never saw it get it at their next create or join, the "first" circle that asks (my reading for members from before this ticket).
  - **Registration**: allowed and signed in, the app asks iOS for the token at launch and on becoming active; the token goes to the account once per account (`registeredFor`). The environment is the provisioning profile's `aps-environment` (production for TestFlight and the App Store), sandbox without a profile (the simulator). At **sign-out** `AccountSession.beforeSignOut` deletes the device with the access token before the refresh token is revoked (revocation ends the access token), renewing it first when it's near its end.
  - **Handling**: the delegate (`@UIApplicationDelegateAdaptor`) hands the token, background pushes and taps to the center. Every push refreshes the circle list and the feeds it names (every open feed for a digest); a notable find becomes `latestFriendFind` and is yielded to `friendFinds()` streams (deduplicated by owner and sighting, since a foreground notable arrives twice) for #67's banner; a tap opens its circle, or the list for a digest. Foreground alerts still show as banners.
  - **The entitlement** `aps-environment` = `development` is in `BirdJournal.entitlements`, Debug only like the associated domain (#61): Release has no entitlements file until #71 turns accounts on there. `UIBackgroundModes` gains `remote-notification`; the Info.plist is shared, so Release lists it too, harmless without the entitlement (Release never registers for pushes, since `PushCenter.isAvailable` is false with accounts off). **Mutes**: Settings → **Mute this circle** on a circle's page, and a member's swipe or context menu **Mute <name>** (a bell-slash marks them); mutes load with the circle list and clear at sign-out. DEBUG Developer shows the permission, environment and token for the wizard's test push.
- **For account deletion (#71)**: an account's devices, mutes and last-push times are under `ACCOUNT#<sub>` with the rest of it; also delete `DEVICE#<token>` for each of its devices and its buffers `DIGEST` / `begins_with(<sub>#)`. Buffers about a deleted friend drop themselves at the next flush (their finds are gone).
- **Debug flags**: `-fakePushPermission notDetermined|denied|authorized` and `-fakePushGrant NO`. With the fake circle server and no flag, notifications count as allowed, so the other UI tests never meet the alert; under a fake the "explained" flag lasts one launch. Screenshots in `docs/screenshots/63/`.
- **Tests**: backend `test_pushes.py` (38: device registration, environment, a phone moving accounts, a stale sign-out, bad tokens; Legendary, Rare and Exceptional notable with the alert, `content-available` and the summary; uncommon, common and unrated not; circle first notable and the next of its species not; a late offline find taking the first; a first only in a muted circle not notable; a repeated put silent; one push across two circles; the owner never pushed; members who left and friends without a phone; a gone token forgotten; a failing APNs not failing the put; the silent refresh; the digest after 15 minutes, a burst of seven finds as one "6 birds in Pasadena"; the two-hour wait after a notable and after a digest, per friend; secret spots and no place; deleted finds left out and an emptied buffer dropped; "A friend"; friend and circle mutes, a mute after buffering; mute routes; the schedule's event through the Lambda handler; the words), `test_apns.py` (10: headers, hosts, the JWT verified with the public key, background priority 5, the collapse id, token reuse for 50 minutes, gone tokens, the expired-token retry, refusals and network errors, the missing key looked for again), settings from the environment; infra `test_pushes.py` (the environment, read access to the secret only and no Secret resource, the rule's rate, input, no retries and its invoke permission; the template test checks the rule's tag); app `PushTests` (the device and mute routes, the profile's environment, payload parsing, the center: once at the first circle and on joining, never elsewhere, not when already decided, turning on registers and sends the token once with its environment, a denial kept and the prompt never twice, the token following sign-in and sign-out, a notable push kept, streamed and refreshing its feed, taps, mutes over the fake); UI `PushesUITests` (the explanation at the first circle only and allowing it, a denial leaving Settings on the circle page, "Not now" leaving the choice there, muting a circle and a friend).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy, the APNs key and Push Notifications on the App ID (they wait on the go-ahead and the push wizard), and on two devices: a notable find on one phone pushing to the other, a burst of ordinary finds arriving as one digest, and a silent push refreshing a backgrounded app's feed (iOS throttles background pushes, so the feed also reloads on opening).

## The notifications explanation counts as asked once answered (issue #107) — built 2026-11-02
- **Asked means answered**: `pushPermissionExplained` is set when the birder taps **Turn on notifications** or **Not now** (or the circle page's Turn on notifications), not when `joinedCircle()` schedules the alert. SwiftUI says nothing when an alert raised during a sheet's dismissal is dropped, so "presented" can't be seen; the answer is the proof it was on screen. A create or join with the permission already decided marks nothing (the permission guard covers it).
- **One more chance**: raising it sets `pushPermissionExplanationPending` (in the defaults, so a kill or a dropped alert both count). The next time the Circles tab or a circle's page appears, `PushCenter.circlesAppeared()` offers it once more if it's still pending, the birder is signed in (signed out it waits) and the permission is still undetermined, then clears the flag: one retry, after which only the next create or join (or the page's button) asks. A page that appears while the first alert waits out its 0.6 s (the new circle's own page) adds nothing. The retry lowers `isExplaining` before raising it after the delay, so a binding a dropped alert left up shows again. It can't tell a stuck binding from an alert really up, so a page appearing under a showing alert would take it down and up again; a page can't be opened under the alert, so that takes a programmatic navigation in the same moment. An answer during the delay (the page's button) stops the alert from following.
- **"Not now" is kept**: it marks asked, so neither the retry nor a later create or join raises the alert; the circle page's **Turn on notifications** button is unchanged.
- **Tests**: app `PushCenterTests` (an unanswered explanation offered again once; "Not now" never asking again by itself while the page's button still prompts; a page during the delay; a stuck binding raised again; the page's button during the delay; signed out the retry waits; nothing pending, nothing asked); `PushesUITests` unchanged and passing.

## Shared cards by hand, and the card as the system share (issue #66) — built 2026-11-01, deploy and device check pending
- **"A story to share"** (`Share/ShareCardView.swift`) replaces the postcard sheet in all three places (the card's Share, Sighting details, the Legendary reveal). At the top is the rendered card face itself, so the preview is exactly the image the system share sends. **Include**: Your note (off; disabled with no note) and City (on). **Your circles** (only with `AccountSession.isEnabledInThisBuild`, as the Circles tab): one switch per circle (none chosen at first: sharing by hand is an explicit choice) and Original photo (off; disabled with no original photo). A bottom bar always holds **Send to N circles** (shown when signed in with a circle) and **Share card image…**. Signed out it says where to sign in; with no circle, it points to the Circles tab.
- **The place**: City on gives the find's rule (a city from `PlaceNames`, or "a secret spot" for a threatened or unrated species, never a coordinate). Off gives **no place at all**, not even "a secret spot". The same place goes on the card face, in the system share's text and to the circles: the secret spot holds for the system share too (my choice; the share policy alone would have published the city of a threatened bird to anyone).
- **The system share** sends the card face (`CardFront`, now internal, given the shareable place and its art already loaded, so `ImageRenderer` has the pixels) as a fresh PNG through ImageIO with no properties (`CardExport.png`), and the share policy's text (the name, date and place, the note when chosen, "Heard with Bird Journal", a pack photo's credit). A pack **reference photo** stays on the face only when its license is in `SharePolicy.shareableLicenses`; otherwise the face falls back to the typographic art. The original photo **never** goes in the system share, only to circles (the sheet says so). The postcard view and its export are gone.
- **The find policy's extras** (`FindPolicy.card(for:choices:circleIDs:photoKey:)`): the find whatever the sharing setting (sharing by hand is the only way a private birder's sighting reaches a circle), the note only when chosen and not empty, the photo only when chosen and uploaded, and the caption whenever the sighting has one (spec: the card face carries it). The encoded card has exactly `find`, `circle_ids`, `include_place`, `note`, `caption` and `photo` (tested). An automatic find still never carries a note or caption.
- **Face blur**: #68's `Art/ArtPhoto.swift` (`ArtPhoto.prepare(jpeg:)`: Vision face boxes blurred, upright, a fresh JPEG with no metadata), checked out unchanged from `origin/feature/68-scene-art` (c235011) with `ArtPhotoTests` and its fixture, so the branches merge cleanly. There's one face blur, not two.
- **Sending** goes directly, not through the outbox: the birder is watching and the photo upload needs the network anyway. Failures show on the sheet (Send again). On success the outbox is told (`FindOutbox.sharedByHand`), so a later Heard-to-Seen puts the find again and removing the sighting deletes it, as for an automatic find, even while private. The Legendary flag is the one the find was published with if it was, otherwise the card's now.
- **On the server** (`finds.py` `share`, `PUT /v1/finds/{id}/card`; body `{find, circle_ids, include_place, note, caption, photo: {key}}`, `extra="forbid"`): every chosen circle must be one of the owner's current circles (404 otherwise, and nothing is written); 1 to 50 circles. A new find goes **only** to the chosen circles. An existing one (automatic, or shared before) is added to the chosen circles it wasn't in, keeps the others, and its `circle_ids` grows, so a delete still reaches every item. **One share record per circle**, `FIND#<sub>#<sighting id>` / `SHARE#<circle id>` (note, caption, photo key, `place_hidden`, `shared_at`), in the reactions' partition, so #62's delete cascade takes it. Every write of the feed items (a share **or a plain put**) lays each circle's share over the find. So the extras show only in the chosen circles, an outbox correction (Heard to Seen, sent with the city) keeps them and keeps the place hidden there, and city-off on an existing find hides the place in the chosen circles while its other circles keep their city. Sharing again to a circle replaces what it showed. The feed gives each item `card: {note, caption, photo: {key, url}} | null`. Notes are at most 2000 characters, captions at most 300, with line breaks and tabs the only control characters allowed.
- **The original photo** lives in #68's ArtCdn bucket under **`photos/<sub>/<sighting id>/<unguessable>.jpg`** (approved; no new bucket, `art/` and the uploads bucket untouched). `POST /v1/finds/{id}/photo` gives a **presigned POST** (15 minutes), not a PUT, so the signed policy caps the size (`content-length-range` 1 byte to 5 MB) and fixes the key, the `image/jpeg` type and the `pending=true` tagging. A PUT can't cap size, so anyone signed in could have parked any amount of anything behind the CDN for a day. The phone sends the fields, then the file last, as multipart. A share checks that the key is the owner's own **for that sighting** (one photo never ends up on two finds, where deleting one would take the other's picture), that it arrived (else 422 `photo_missing`) and that it is at most 5 MB (else it's deleted and 422). The tag comes off only **after** the share is written, so a share that fails part way leaves the photo pending to expire. The bucket's lifecycle rule `DeleteUnsharedPhotosAfterADay` (prefix `photos/`, tag `pending=true`) expires photos never shared. The server deletes a photo when no share shows it any more: a re-share without it, or the find deleted (photos go first, so a delete cut short still finds them). ADR-0004: kept only while a shared card shows it. Without `ART_BUCKET` the photo routes answer 503 and cards without photos still share.
- **A queued automatic find goes first**: the sheet drains the outbox before sending. If the sighting's automatic put still can't go (offline, backing off), the share waits ("still on its way to your circles"). Otherwise the hand share would create the find in the chosen circles alone, and the server, which keeps a find's first circles, would never spread it to the others. A chosen photo that can't be read or prepared fails the share rather than going out without it.
- **Infra** (`infra/birdjournal_infra/shared_photos.py`, `SharedPhotos`): the lifecycle rule on the art bucket and the API's grant of `s3:PutObject`, `PutObjectTagging`, `GetObject`, `DeleteObjectTagging` and `DeleteObject` on `photos/*` only. Two assertions in #68's `test_art.py` now also allow that prefix. **Not deployed**: the deploy waits on the maintainer's go-ahead with #62's and #68's.
- **Moderation** of shared photos (Rekognition, spec "Safety") is for the public launch, switched on by configuration, and isn't built here.
- **The feed row** shows a shared card's caption in italics, the note in quotes and the photo (3:2, from the CDN) under the marks. The fake server's seeded Sunday Group has Jo's hummingbird shared by hand with no place. `-autoScreen share` honours `-autoCardSpecies`. Screenshots are in `docs/screenshots/66/`.
- **Tests**: backend `test_cards.py` (31 cases: extras only in the chosen circles; extras only when chosen; a private birder's card only in the chosen circle; city off hides the place there and not elsewhere, and ignores a place that came anyway; a later put keeps the extras and the hidden place; a re-share replaces them and deletes the photo nothing shows; a photo still shown elsewhere stays; a new circle joins the find and the delete reaches it; circles not the owner's refused with nothing written; the photo kept on share and deleted with the find; upload URLs on the owner's prefix; foreign, malformed, missing and oversized photos refused; 503 without a bucket; malformed extras and coordinates refused; line breaks kept; the secret spot; owner-scoped ids; tokens); infra `test_shared_photos.py` (the lifecycle rule and Retain, the API's grant only under `photos/`, `ART_BUCKET`); app `SharedCardPolicyTests` (defaults; note and photo only when chosen; city off gives no place, not even a secret spot; city on keeps the find's rules; automatic finds have no extras; the encoded keys), `ShareCardTests` (the face's PNG has no Exif, GPS, TIFF, IPTC or orientation, even from art that carries them; a pack photo only under a shareable license), `SharedCardClientTests` (the upload URL, the signed PUT with its headers and no token, the card; a refused upload; the feed's extras; over the fake, extras only in the chosen circles and kept by a later put; a private birder's card; a foreign circle refused), `FindOutboxTests` (a card shared by hand is corrected and removed like a published find), UI `ShareCardUITests` (the sheet's defaults, the card image ready, city off explained; signed in, Send disabled until a circle is chosen, then sent to Sunday Group with the note and photo, shown there "by you" with the note and absent from Family).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy (the lifecycle rule and the grant), and on a device the photo upload to the real bucket and a friend's phone showing the photo.

## Photo art and the painting allowance (issue #69) — built 2026-11-01, retired by #137 before it was verified on a device
The birder's own photo of the bird, embellished into card art, on #68's pipeline; and the public launch's rules as a switch.
- **One pipeline, a second kind.** `kind` is `scene` or `photo` on the same job routes, queue, buckets and worker. The worker paints `photo` with the committed `photo-art` workflow and `prompts.json`'s photo template (edition light, the Legendary light for a Legendary card). It needs no reference bird: the birder confirmed the species, so the photo is the identity. It finds the bird with BiRefNet and crops 3:4 around it with the bake-off's photo padding (0.8); a photo with no bird fails **`no_bird`** (a new failure reason). **The fidelity gate judges the painted bird against the bird in the birder's own crop** (ΔE ≤ 10, IoU ≥ 0.65 after the colour fit, three seeds), never a pack reference, and no prompt names the species (the shared `edition_prompt` refuses one that does). A painting that never passes fails `painting_failed`.
- **No automatic ID check** (the maintainer's rule): the request sheet shows the photo and asks "Is this bird a House Finch?" with "Yes, it's a House Finch"; nothing else checks it.
- **The switch** is `artAutoPaint` (`ART_AUTO_PAINT`), as #68 named it, plus `artDailyAllowance` (`ART_DAILY_ALLOWANCE`, default 5) in `infra/cdk.json`. `paint_policy` decides each job's cost:
  - `every_glasses_add` (friends phase, unchanged): everything is free, photo art included; `GET /v1/art/allowance` answers `daily: null`.
  - `legendary_or_rare` (public launch): a Legendary or Rare-or-rarer add's scene art is free; scene art asked for (Try again, Retry art) and all photo art take one of the day's paintings; any other add isn't painted (`409 not_eligible`, as before).
- **The allowance** is a counter per account and **UTC day** (`ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `ARTDAY#<yyyy-mm-dd>`, `ttl` two days after), taken in **one transaction with the job's put**, so a refused painting writes no job and offers no upload, and a spent day writes nothing (`409 allowance_spent`). A repeated create returns its job and costs nothing again. UTC, not the phone's zone, so changing the zone gains nothing; it starts again at midnight UTC (`resets_at`). **A failed painting still counts**: refunds would need a second write on every failure for little gain, and a job only expires after 7 days, a different day anyway. `GET /v1/art/allowance` gives `daily`, `used`, `left`, `resets_at`; the phone shows **"N left today"** in the request and comparison sheets (`CardText.allowanceLine`), refreshing it after each request.
- **The phone** (`Art/PhotoArtViews.swift`, `ArtJobs`, Album `CardPhotoArt.swift`):
  - **A job of its own**: `CardArt.photoJob` beside the scene `job`, so photo art never loses a scene painting on its way. `CardArt` is JSON in `artState`, so the new optional fields (`photoJob`, `photoPreview`, `source` on `ArtJob` and `ArtImage`) need no schema version; a test decodes art saved before them.
  - **Sources**: the card's glasses photo, or a library photo (PhotosPicker). A library photo is **attached as an original** only once the birder confirms the species: written byte for byte as its own file in `attachedPhotoPaths`. It leaves prepared like scene art (faces blurred, metadata stripped), scaled to at most 2048 px a side (`ArtPhoto.libraryMaxSide`; any ImageIO format, always a JPEG out).
  - **The original is never overwritten**: a job names its `source` and only reads it; every painting is a new file; Approve, Keep original and Try again delete only paintings. `PhotoArtTests` hashes the original and the glasses photo before and after painting, Try again, Approve and Keep original.
  - **Preview, then approval**: a painting lands as `photoPreview` (not on the card); the comparison sheet shows "Your photo" and "AI-embellished from your photo" side by side with **Approve** (it becomes `photo`, first in the card's art, labelled "AI-embellished from your photo"), **Keep original** (the painting goes; the card and the original stay as they were) and **Try again** (a new job from the same original; the waiting painting stays until the new one lands). Approved photo art stays while a new one is painted. A failure offers Retry art and Keep current art, as scene art does.
  - **"Did you see it?"**: attaching a library photo to a Heard card asks, after the request ("Yes, I saw it" makes it Seen; "No, I only heard it" keeps Heard). Painting the card's own glasses photo isn't an attachment and asks nothing (`PhotoArtFlow.asksDidYouSeeIt`).
