  - **Scene art on request**: a glasses card with no scene art and nothing under way offers **Paint this scene** (the public launch's ordinary adds, which aren't painted automatically, and cards added while signed out). Under the scene and photo controls, "N left today" when the day is limited; a refusal (`allowance_spent`) is said under the controls of the card and kind that asked (`ArtJobs.Refusal`).
  - A library photo is kept in its own format, named for it (`.heic`, `.png`, `.jpg`; `ArtPhoto.pathExtension`, `FrameStore.write(_:pathExtension:)`).
  - Gated like scene art: `ArtJobs.isAvailable` (an art server and a signed-in birder), so Release builds show no photo-art button.
- **Debug flags**: `-autoArt photo-preview|photo-approved`, `-fakeArtDaily <n>` (the fake keeps the allowance), `-fakeLibraryPhoto` (the library button picks the glasses photo as a library photo; the system picker is out of XCUITest's reach), `-autoPhotoArt request|review` (opens the sheets for screenshots; `docs/screenshots/69/`).
- **Tests**: backend `test_art_allowance.py` (the policy both ways, five a day then refused with no job or upload, Legendary and Rare free, ordinary adds refused at no cost, repeated creates, per account, midnight UTC, failures count, a smaller allowance, settings) and photo-art cases in `test_art_jobs.py`; infra (the allowance variable, the switch by context, a bad allowance refused); artworker `test_photo_art.py` (painted from the photo alone on `photo-art`, the crop around the bird, no species name, the Legendary light, no reference needed, `no_bird`, unfaithful); Album `CardPhotoArtTests`; app `PhotoArtTests`, `DidYouSeeItTests`, allowance and kind in `ArtClientTests`, the scaled library photo in `ArtPhotoTests`; UI `SceneArtUITests` gains Paint this scene within "5 left today"; `PhotoArtUITests` (glasses photo through the species check, comparison and Approve; Keep original; Try again with "5 left today" then "4"; none left; "Did you see it?" making a Heard card Seen; the glasses photo asking nothing; signed out).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy (the new `ART_DAILY_ALLOWANCE` variable), the photo-art path on comfybox with real photos and FLUX.3 (no worker change beyond the code; the workflow and template are the bake-off's), and the library picker on a device.

## Friend banner on the lens (issue #67) — built 2026-11-01, the two hardware checks pending
- **The state machine** (`LensSession`, pure, toolkit-free): a friend's notable find is `LensPage.friend(LensFriendBanner)`, an overlay page like the problem page, rendered as "A friend's find" (strip), "Maya heard a Black Phoebe · Pasadena" (heading) and "Tap or swipe right to close". The line is the friend's first name (the push's display name cut to its first word, for the word budget; "A friend" without one), "heard" or "saw", the species with its article, and the place label as the find carries it ("a secret spot" as is; none ends the line at the bird).
  - **Shows** only over the species list or a photo page. On the viewfinder, a details page, a sounds page (playing or not: the spec lists "a playing sounds page", but "only over the list or a photo page" covers an idle one too) or a problem page it is **held**, and shown the moment a gesture lands the wearer on the list or a photo page (including the swipe that stops a clip, and the dismissal of a problem).
  - **One at a time**: a newer find replaces the banner showing (the way back still leads to the page it covers) or the one held (the hold restarts). A find already bannered in this run (owner and sighting; a foreground notable arrives twice, and #63's dedup only catches a repeat of the latest) changes nothing, so a dismissed banner never comes back, even after another find in between.
  - **Expiry**: a held banner is dropped when it has waited more than five minutes (`bannerHoldLimit`, 300 s; exactly 300 s is not more) since it arrived. A banner **showing** never expires (my reading: the spec drops held ones; one on the lens waits for the wearer, who closes it with one tap).
  - **Dismiss**: a tap, swipe right or Back returns to the covered page; the other swipes do nothing there. The Add button does nothing on it.
  - A problem reported over a banner covers the page under it and holds the banner, which comes back once the problem is dismissed. A new session's stack (the pages restart on the list) keeps the banner. A banner pushed back like that was already on the lens, so it never expires (it would otherwise be dropped at once if it had shown for five minutes).
  - The machine takes times as plain seconds on any steady clock (`friendFound(_:at:)`, `expireBanner(at:)`, `heldBannerExpiry`), so it stays pure.
- **The adapter** (`GlassesLensSession.friendFound(_:)`): takes a banner while it holds a run's pages (running, or suspended until the glasses come back, when it waits with them); with no run it is dropped (a find "while we're both out"). Its banner clock is its own `ContinuousClock`; a one-shot task drops a held banner at its limit, and every gesture checks the limit first, so a late timer can't show a stale one. Landing on the list under a banner still tells `onStreamNeeded` (`pageUnderBanner`).
- **Delivery** (`Lens/FriendBanners.swift`): the app subscribes once at launch to #63's `PushCenter.friendFinds()` and hands each find to both lenses (the glasses run's and the Developer lens on its own); the one with a run shows it. Foreground and background are the same path: `didReceiveRemoteNotification` (`content-available`) calls `received`, which yields to the stream before awaiting the feed refresh, so the banner is sent while iOS keeps the app awake for the push. A glasses run keeps the app running in the background (the camera stream), so the push wakes a live process; whether iOS delivers it promptly is the hardware check.
- **Checks without a second phone**: Settings → Developer → Friend banner ("Friend's find on the lens in 10 s" runs a sample notable push through `received`; "Post a local notification in 10 s" for the mirroring check), and the push wizard's test push now carries a sample find, so it makes a banner on a running lens. DEBUG `-autoMockLensFriend YES` (`scripts/mock-lens-in-chrome.sh --friend`) shows it on the mock lens. Screenshot: `docs/screenshots/67/` (the in-app mock lens; the Chrome Display Simulator couldn't be captured with the Mac's screen locked).
- **Tests**: `LensStateMachineTests` (the gesture table's banner rows; shown over the list and a photo page; held on the viewfinder, details and sounds pages and shown on return; held while a clip plays and shown when the swipe stops it; replaced showing and held; the same find twice; expiry past five minutes and not at; a showing one never expiring; problems over and under it; the Add button and new species under it; a new session), `LensCardRendererTests` (the card, the word budget, saw, an, a secret spot, no place), `FriendBannerTests` (the find to the banner, one banner per find, a lens with no run dropping it), and the Mock Device Kit smoke test (a notable push through the push center and the relay to a running lens on the mock Display: shown over the list and dismissed by a tap, held on a details page, shown on swiping up, dismissed by a swipe right).
- **Not yet verified** (DAT-SETUP-CHECKLIST.md step 14): the banner on real glasses; whether a backgrounded glasses run gets the push in time; whether the glasses mirror an ordinary iOS notification (if they do, a friend's push may show twice on the lens).

## Reactions and Compare birds (issue #64) — built 2026-11-01, deploy and the device check of a reaction push pending
- **Reactions** (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/reactions.py`, `reactions_api.py`): `PUT` and `DELETE /v1/finds/{owner}/{sighting id}/reactions/{lovely|jealous|together}`, no body (a comment can't be sent), each answering 200 with the find's counts. A member gives each kind at most once per find (several kinds are fine; again is no change); a delete of one that isn't there is no error. Only someone who **now** shares a circle the find went to may react (anyone else, a stranger or a member of an owner's other circle the find never reached, 404, so a find's existence never leaks); the owner never reacts to their own (422). Items: `FIND#<owner>#<sighting id>` / `REACTION#<reactor>#<kind>` (#62's reserved partition; the counts read it) and its copy `ACCOUNT#<reactor>` / `REACTION#<owner>#<sighting id>#<kind>`, so account deletion (#71) finds an account's reactions on others' finds without a scan. A find's delete takes the partition, each reaction's copy, the push time and any waiting push.
- **Counts on feed items**: every feed item gains `reactions`, all three kinds in order with `count` and `mine`, read at feed time (one query of each item's small reaction partition, 20 a page) rather than counters on the feed items: a find's repeated put rewrites its items, and counters there would race it. Counts are anonymous; who reacted reaches only the owner, in the push.
- **The owner's push, collapsed per find within 10 minutes** (my reading): the first reaction pushes at once ("Bo reacted to your Black Phoebe" / "Lovely find", kind `reaction`, `sighting_id`, the owner's `circle_ids` the find went to, `thread-id` `find-<id>`, `apns-collapse-id` `reaction-<id>`). Reactions within 10 minutes of that alert wait in `REACTIONS` / `<owner>#<sighting id>` (one partition, like the digests); the 15-minute digest schedule (`flush_digests`, which now also runs the reaction flush and reports both) sends them once the 10 minutes are up as one alert that replaces the first on the phone, so it names the first alert's reactions too (kept on `ACCOUNT#<owner>` / `REACTIONPUSH#<sighting id>` as `shown`): "Bo and Cy reacted to your Black Phoebe" / "Lovely find · Jealous! · Let's go together" (three or more people: "Bo and 2 others"). So a burst reaches the phone as at most two alerts, the second 10 to 25 minutes after the first. A reaction after the window pushes at once, with anything still waiting. The flush reads the reactions back: one taken back meanwhile is left out, and a buffer with nothing new left sends nothing. Mutes hold as for finds: a muted friend's reactions never push, nor those that reach the owner only through circles they muted. No phone: nothing is sent and nothing waits (the next reaction pushes at once). A push failure never fails the reaction. No new resources: the same function, table and schedule.
- **Compare birds** (`GET /v1/friends/{sub}/compare`): `{friend: {id, display_name}, species: [{species_id, scientific_name, common_name, method}]}`, the friend's species with their best method (Seen beats Heard), from **their finds whose circles include one the two share now** (one query of `ACCOUNT#<friend>` / `FIND#…` filtered on `circle_ids`, the same set as their feed items in those circles, without paging through every shared feed). Not a circle route: a friend is anyone in any circle with you (CONTEXT), so the side covers every circle you share, as the spec says. Someone you share no circle with is 404, yourself 422. Deleted finds drop out with their find.
- **The app** (`Circles/CircleFeedView.swift`, `Circles/CompareBirdsView.swift`): under each friend's find, the three reactions as chips with counts (filled when given; a tap gives, a second takes back), shown at once and replaced by the server's counts, put back as they were on a failure; the titles shrink to icons only when a narrow screen can't fit them (VoiceOver always hears "Lovely find, 2 reactions", selected when given). The birder's own finds show what friends gave as a line ("Lovely find 2 · Let's go together 1"), nothing to tap. The circle page's **Recent finds | Compare birds** switch (the spec's "Recent finds or Compare birds"): Compare lists the circle's friends; one opens **You and Maya**: the tallies (You, Maya, Both), then "Maya has, you haven't yet" (the next walk), "You both have" and "Only you", each row the species with Heard, Seen or Not yet in a You and a Maya column. **Your side is every sighting on the phone**, private ones included (it never leaves the phone), Seen beating Heard, species matched by scientific name (`BirdComparison`, pure). A reaction push refreshes the feeds it names; a tap opens the find's circle. `FakeCircleClient` counts reactions (seeded: Jo and Sam find Maya's Black Phoebe lovely, Maya is jealous of Sam's Gnatcatcher), compares from the friends' items in shared circles, and Maya gains an older Acorn Woodpecker and House Finch so the comparison has all three groups. `-autoScreen compare` opens Compare birds with the first friend. Screenshots in `docs/screenshots/64/`.
- **Tests**: backend `test_reactions.py` (33: put, counts and the reactor's copy, and again after a put cut short; once per kind; several kinds; taking back, and again; only the fixed kinds; no comment; never one's own; only members of a circle the find reached, not after leaving, though taking one back still works; unknown finds, bad ids, no token; feed counts with the reader's own; the delete cascade; the first reaction pushing at once with its words, thread and collapse id, to the owner only; a burst collapsing into one later alert naming the first's reaction too; a reaction after the window carrying what waited; one taken back left out; windows per find; a repeated reaction silent; friend and circle mutes; a mute after buffering; an owner without a phone; a find deleted while waiting; the schedule's job; a failing APNs; the words; compare's best method, shared circles only (no backfill, another circle's finds hidden), 404 and 422, an empty side, deleted finds), `test_pushes.py`'s schedule test reports the reaction flush too; app `ReactionTests` (the routes and counts over HTTP, decoding with an unknown kind and from before reactions, compare over HTTP, giving and taking back on an item, the own-find summary, the fixed set, the reaction push, the comparison's three groups with Seen beating Heard and an empty one, the store's optimistic reaction and its reload, a refused one put back, no reaction on one's own find, the fake's compare over shared circles); UI `ReactionsUITests` (a reaction given and taken back by taps, exactly three and nowhere to type; Compare birds from the circle page, never yourself, the rows' words); `FindsUITests` and `CirclesUITests` scroll past the longer seeded feed.
- **Edges**: a put retried after it was cut short writes the reactor's copy again (it is written every time, so #71 never misses one). Taking a reaction back works after leaving the circle, like unmuting (it only removes one's own; 404 only when the find is gone). A reaction taken back after its push leaves that push on the phone; a reaction given again after the window pushes again. On the phone, overlapping taps on one find show only the latest tap's answer. **Blocks** suppressing reactions and their pushes are #71's, with blocks themselves.
- **For account deletion (#71)**: an account's reactions on others' finds are its `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `REACTION#<owner>#<sighting id>#<kind>` copies: delete each and the matching `FIND#<owner>#<sighting id>` / `REACTION#<sub>#<kind>`. Its own finds' reactions, push times (`REACTIONPUSH#…`) and buffers (`REACTIONS` / `<sub>#…`) go with the finds' delete (`FindStore.delete`). Buffers naming a deleted reactor drop it at the next flush (its reaction is gone).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy (no new resources, only the Lambda's code) and, on two devices, a reaction on one phone reaching the other as a push, and a burst arriving as one replacement alert.

## Goals: the automatic monthly goal and custom goals (issue #65) — built 2026-11-01, deploy and the two-device check pending
- **The monthly goal** (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/goals.py`): one per circle per calendar month **in the circle's time zone** (the creator's device's, #61). Target: the larger of 5 and 115 % of last month's distinct species, **rounded half up in integer hundredths** (`(n × 115 + 50) // 100`: 10 gives 12, 20 gives exactly 23, where floating point gives 22.999…), or **10 in the month the circle was made** (the calendar month, so a circle made on the 31st has 10 for a day and its second month usually the floor of 5, as the spec words it). The target is fixed when the goal is made; deletes in last month don't move it. Its title is "<Month> goal"; it can't be deleted (403 `automatic_goal`).
- **The rollover** is a second EventBridge rule beside the digest flush (#63), `cron(0/15 * * * ? *)`, invoking the API's function with `{"birdjournal_job": "roll_over_goals"}` (no retries; `infra/birdjournal_infra/goals.py`). On the quarter hours, not hourly, because every UTC offset is a whole, half or (Nepal, Chatham) three-quarter hour, so each zone's midnight is caught at once. It reads one partition, `GOAL_ROLLOVER` / `<circle id>` (time zone, the last month it made), and touches only circles whose month has turned; an empty circle is passed over. The goal's id is `monthly-<yyyy>-<mm>`, written on `attribute_not_exists`, so the job, a read and a find racing make it once. **Reading the goals or a new find also makes the month's goal** if it's missing, so a late job, a missed run or a circle from before this ticket (registered then) never shows no goal. A circle's creation makes its first goal at once.
- **Custom goals**: any member; a title (1–40 characters, no control characters), a target 1–999, and a **first and last day** as calendar dates in the circle's zone, both included (a Big Day is one date; the period runs from midnight starting the first to midnight ending the last). My choices: the last day may not have passed (`ends_in_the_past`), the span is under 366 days, a start in the past is fine (a trip already under way). The goal's creator or the circle's **current** creator (after a handoff, #61) deletes it; anyone else 403 `not_allowed`, non-members 404.
- **Progress** is read when asked, never stored: distinct scientific names among the circle's feed items (`CIRCLE#<id>#FEED`, #62) whose found minute is in the period, one key range on the feed's sort key (`FindStore.species_between`). So repeats and several members' same bird count once, **a delete lowers it at once**, and a member who left still counts (their items stay). Items `CIRCLE#<id>#GOALS` / `GOAL#<id>`; no index.
- **Completion, pushed once**: `completed_at` is set on `attribute_not_exists(completed_at)` by the **new** find (`PushService.find_added`, first thing, even in a circle of one) that brings progress to the target (its species new to the period and the count reaching the target exactly with it, so a repeat bird never takes the credit), and only the writer pushes. A goal that has **ended** completes no more: a late find shared by hand doesn't announce last March's goal. "Once" is at most once: completion is noted before delivery, so a failed or fully muted delivery isn't retried. Progress falling after a delete and rising again pushes nothing; `completed_at` stays (the card shows live progress). A custom goal made already reached is noted at creation with no push. Only new finds complete goals (a repeated put sends no push, #63); a goal reached by a moved find is completed by the next new find in its period. Completion makes the find **notable** for members whose circle is open (mutes as for any notable find; never the finder): **one** alert carries both, title "Sunday Group reached the November goal" (or "reached “Big Day”"), body "Ada found a Black Phoebe, species 10 of 10 · Legendary · Pasadena", with `find` as before plus `goals` (circle, id, kind, title, target, progress).
- **API**: `GET /v1/circles/{id}/goals` (this month's goal, then custom goals active, upcoming, then ended within the last week), `POST` (201), `DELETE /v1/circles/{id}/goals/{goal id}` (204). Each goal: dates and instants, progress, `status`, `completed_at`, `created_by`, `can_delete`.
- **The app**: the goal card heads a circle's page (`Circles/CircleGoalsView.swift`): the month's goal on a raised card ("10 of 14 species", a moss bar, gold once reached, "4 more to go · 30 days left"), the custom goals as rows (count, bar, "Oct 12 – Nov 10 · by Maya Ortiz · 10 days left"), swipe or context menu to delete when allowed, and **New goal** (a sheet: title, target stepper, first and last day in the circle's zone). Its sheet and dialog hang on the New goal button: on the List section they never presented. Goals reload with the page and after any push about the circle. `FakeCircleClient` keeps the server's rules; seeded Sunday Group has a month's goal, a running, an upcoming and a reached custom goal. Debug `-autoScreen circle-goal` opens the sheet. Screenshots in `docs/screenshots/65/`.
- **For account deletion (#71)**: goals belong to the circle; a deleted member's custom goals stay (the circle's creator can delete them) with `created_by` naming no account.
- **Tests**: backend `test_goals.py` (the target table; the first month's 10; the rollover from last month's distinct species with finds at 23:59 and 00:00 local on the DST-ending 1 November in Los Angeles; Tokyo, Kathmandu (:45) and Los Angeles each rolling at their own midnight; December to January; a quiet month's 5; a lazily made goal; the target fixed; an empty circle passed over; the schedule's event through the handler; members only; custom goals by any member, refusals, delete permissions including after a handoff, the monthly undeletable, ended goals for a week; distinct counting, other circles' finds, a delete reducing progress, a member who left; completion pushed once and not after falling and rising, the monthly's words with a Legendary in one alert, made already reached, outside the period, a muted circle, reached alone, two circles at once); infra `test_goals.py` (the rule's cron, input, no retries, the invoke permission); app `CircleGoalTests` (HTTP list, create, delete and refusals; the card's words; the store over the fake: first month, order, distinct progress and a delete, who deletes); UI `GoalsUITests` (the card on the seeded circle; make a goal and delete it); `FindsUITests` scrolls past the card.
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy (the new rule; it waits on the go-ahead), and on two devices a goal completion pushing to the other phone.


## Painted art in finds (issue #70) — built 2026-11-02, retired by #137 before the two-device check
- **The rule** (ADR-0004): the server keeps a painting (scene or photo art, `art/<sub>/…`) only while the sighting's find shows it, and otherwise deletes it once the phone has downloaded it. A card shared by hand is the same find in the circles chosen, so it counts as "a shared card showing it"; a private birder with no find has nothing showing it. A painting goes at whichever comes **last** of the phone's `received` and the last find showing it moving on (another painting, pack art, or the find deleted). #68's `NothingShared` is gone: `ArtRetention.is_shown(owner, sighting id, key)` is now `FindsShowArt`, the find's `art_key` read by key.
- **The repeated put**: the phone sends the painting on the find through #62's ordinary `PUT /v1/finds/{id}`, which only pushes for a **new** find, so #63's notable and digest logic never fire again for it (a repeated put writes no digest buffer and sends no silent push; tested). The art object gains an optional `fallback` (pack art only, on painted art only, else 422): `{"kind":"painted","key":"art/<sub>/…","fallback":{"kind":"pack","key":"packs/…"}}`. The find's JSON still has exactly ten keys.
- **What a find may show** (`finds.py` `_held`, `art.py` `PaintedArt.is_held`): painted art only while the server still holds it **for that sighting**: the art the find already shows, or the output of one of the owner's own jobs for that sighting, ready and not deleted (`art_deleted_at`, a new job attribute). Anything else (another account's key, another sighting's painting, one deleted, one never painted) gives way to the fallback, else what the find showed before, else none. So a find never points at nothing, and one account can't keep another's painting alive. The job is found by its key with one query of `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `ARTJOB#…` and a filter (no index; a birder has a few jobs a day, kept 30 days after receipt). A job the `ttl` already removed counts as received.
- **Letting go** (`PaintedArt.release`): a put or share that replaces painted art, and a find's delete (before the find item, so a delete cut short is retried while the find still names it), release the old painting: deleted now if the phone has it, else left for `received`, which deletes it since nothing shows it. `received` checks the find, deletes and stamps `art_deleted_at` in the same update as `received_at`. The job response gains `art_key` once ready (until the painting is deleted), for the phone to name it.
- **Order on the phone** (`ArtJobs`): the server deletes a painting nothing shows at `received`, so the phone puts the find first. When the art a card shows changes (a scene painting lands, Approve, Use pack art or back), `artChanged` enqueues the find's put (`FindOutbox.changed`, only for published sightings) and flushes, and the phone says it has the painting only when no put for the sighting still waits (`holdsReceipt` = `FindOutbox.isWaitingToPublish`). Offline, the next refresh's orphan pass acknowledges it once the put has gone. **A photo painting waiting for Approve or Keep original on a card with a find (`hasFind`: published or shared by hand) isn't acknowledged until the birder decides** (`CardArt.photoJobAwaitingDecision`; the refresh leaves it too), so Approve can still put it on the find; on a card with no find it is acknowledged at once, since nothing could show it; Keep original acknowledges it and the server deletes it. A preview replaced by Try again is acknowledged by the next refresh. No new state on the sighting beyond `ArtImage.serverKey` (optional JSON in `artState`, no schema change); `CardArt.shownPaintingKey` follows the card face's order (approved photo art, then scene art unless pack art is chosen).
- **Choices** (mine): **Use pack art lets the painting go** on the server; going back to the painting puts it again and the server falls back to the pack art, so friends see pack art until a new painting (Try again). Keeping it would have meant keeping paintings no find shows, against ADR-0004. Paintings landed before #70 have no key, so their finds keep pack art. **A private birder who shares a card by hand after its painting landed** shares pack art: the painting was deleted at download because nothing showed it then (ADR-0004 as written; bringing it back would need the phone to upload it again). A card shared first and painted after shows the painting, and so does Try again after sharing. A preview the birder never decides on stays on the server while the card waits (no expiry on `art/`); the card keeps offering Approve and Keep original. A painting acknowledged before its find existed (an offline add whose first put went after the art landed: rare, since a job needs the network) is deleted, and the find shows its pack art. A `received` racing a put that names the same painting can delete it from under the find; the phone's ordering keeps the two apart, and the fallback covers the later puts.
- **Deleting a sighting** deletes its find (#62's outbox), and the find's delete deletes its painting; the phone already deletes the painting files with the sighting (`Sighting.filePaths`). A painting whose receipt was still waiting is acknowledged by the next refresh as an orphan, and goes then.
- **The secret-spot and no-coordinate rules** are unchanged: art travels beside the place, and a threatened species' find with a painting still says "a secret spot" (tested).
- **Deploy**: Lambda code only (no new resource, permission or variable: the API could already delete `art/*` and read the table). It rides the next deploy.
- **Tests**: backend `test_painted_art.py` (19: kept while a find shows it; kept while only a card shared by hand shows it, and gone with the card; a private birder's painting gone at receipt; one the find never showed gone at receipt; a put naming a deleted painting showing pack art; deleted with the find, and a restored find on pack art; a find deleted before the receipt leaving it for the phone; the repeated put swapping pack art for the painting with no alert, no silent push, no digest and no duplicate; a re-put after a hand share silent; Try again replacing it and the old one going, before and after its receipt; Use pack art and back; approved photo art kept; photo art kept original instead of approved going while the scene painting stays; another account's or sighting's painting refused; no fallback keeping what the find showed; fallbacks only from painted to pack; the secret spot and coordinate refusals with painted art); `test_art_jobs.py` loses the fake retention (the harness now uses the real rule). App `PaintedArtFindsTests` (a landed painting put before its receipt; the receipt waiting for an offline put and sent by the refresh after; Use pack art and back; Approve putting then acknowledging, the refresh leaving an undecided preview; Keep original acknowledging without a put; a private birder acknowledging a scene painting and a photo preview at once), `FindPolicyTests` (painted with its fallback, the secret spot, ten keys, pack art without a fallback), Album `CardArtJobsTests` (the shown key's order, keys absent before #70 and old JSON decoding, Keep original); `PhotoArtTests` now expects no receipt for a preview.
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy, and on two devices a painting reaching the other member's feed (the acceptance check).

## Public launch safety: report, block, moderation, account deletion, privacy policy (issue #71) — built and deployed 2026-11-02, the device deletion pending
- **Reports** (`backend/src/birdjournal_api/safety.py`, `safety_api.py`): `POST /v1/reports` `{kind: find|account, account_id, circle_id, sighting_id (a find's), reason: inappropriate|harassment|spam|other, details (≤ 1000 characters, optional)}`, 201. Only someone who shares the named circle with the reported account **now** may report (404 otherwise; a find must have reached that circle), never oneself (422). The report keeps a **snapshot** of what the reporter saw: the find as that circle shows it (the place hidden where a card shared there left it out) with that circle's note, caption and photo key, the art key, and both names and the circle's name. Stored as `REPORTS` / `<created at>#<id>` (one partition, like the digests, so the maintainer reads them all with one query), then mailed through SES: subject "BirdJournal report: a find by Maya (Inappropriate content)", body with the ids, the snapshot and CDN links. A failed mail never fails the report (logged; the table has it). **At most 20 reports a reporter a UTC day** (`ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `REPORTDAY#<date>`, `ttl`; 429 `too_many_reports`), so nobody can flood the maintainer's inbox (my choice).
- **SES: the verified domain, no identity in the stack.** The shared account already has `lightsailvr.com` verified in SES with production access, and a verified domain covers every address in it, so the stack creates nothing for SES: the API may `ses:SendEmail` as the report address's domain or the address itself, nothing else, and sends from the report address to the report address. The address stays out of the repo like the budget's: `-c reportEmail` or `REPORT_EMAIL` (`~/keys/birdjournal.env`, read by `deploy.sh`), else `BUDGET_ALERT_EMAIL`. `infra/scripts/safety-wizard.sh` checks that the domain (or the address) is verified and verifies the address on its own only when neither is; production access is needed only to send reports to another address. A failed mail leaves the report in the table.
- **Blocks** (`PUT`/`DELETE /v1/blocks/{account}`, `GET /v1/blocks` with names): `ACCOUNT#<blocker>` / `BLOCK#<blocked>`, read with the mutes (`MuteStore.blocked_by`; `Mutes.blocked` and `silences()`), since pushes read both. Only someone the birder shares a circle with now can be blocked (404), never oneself (422); unblocking always works, like unmuting. **What it hides, from the blocker only** (spec: "hides the blocked account's finds, reactions and pushes from the blocker"): their finds in every feed (filtered after the page's query, so a page may hold fewer than `limit` items while `next_cursor` still leads on), their reactions in the counts (also on the counts a reaction returns), Compare birds with them (404), and every push about them: notable finds, digests (a block after buffering drops the buffer at the flush, as a mute does) and reaction pushes. **One-way** (my reading): the blocked member still sees the blocker's finds and can react; they aren't told. Blocks don't show in `GET /v1/mutes`. Goal progress still counts their finds (a circle's goal is the circle's), and the member list still shows them, marked blocked.
- **Moderation** (`moderation.py`): Rekognition `DetectModerationLabels` on the object in place (S3Object, same region), at **60 %** confidence, on an art job's input at **submit** (flagged: the job fails `flagged`, the upload is deleted, nothing is queued), on the painting when the worker reports it **ready** (flagged: the painting is deleted and the job fails `flagged`; the worker's own nsfw filter, #68, stays as a first gate), and on a card's original photo when it is **shared** (flagged: 422 `photo_flagged`, the photo deleted and nothing written, so the card can go again without it: "withheld from the circle" as a refusal the birder sees, my choice over silently dropping the photo). Withheld categories (taxonomy v7 top level and the older names): Explicit, Non-Explicit Nudity of Intimate parts and Kissing, Suggestive, Violence, Visually Disturbing, Rude Gestures, Hate Symbols; an image Rekognition can't read is withheld too. Swimwear, alcohol, tobacco and gambling pass (a birder's photo may show any of them). Automatic finds carry no picture of the birder's, only art keys, so they need no check beyond the painting's. **The switch** is `moderation` in `infra/cdk.json` (`MODERATION`, `off` by default, `on` for the public launch), checked at synth; off, Rekognition is never called. The grants are there either way (`rekognition:DetectModerationLabels` on `*`, which has no resource-level permissions; `s3:GetObject` on `uploads/*` and `art/*`: the first time the API reads a painting), so turning it on is a context change and a deploy. AWS may keep Rekognition's inputs to improve the service unless the organization opts out; the wizard's last stage points at the AWS Organizations AI services opt-out policy (payer account).
- **Account deletion** (`deletion.py`, `DELETE /v1/account`, 204, body optional `{apple_authorization_code}`): everything goes, each step idempotent so the phone's retry finishes a deletion the Lambda's 10 s cut short (the timeout stays 10 s; a very full account may take two calls): finds (#62's delete: feed items, species entries, shares and their photos, reactions with each reactor's copy, reaction-push items, the painting a find showed, #70); the reactions it gave, their find-side items, and its entries in owners' `REACTIONPUSH` `shown` sets and waiting reaction buffers; digest buffers it is in either way; goals; circles; invites; devices with their pointers; other members' mutes, blocks and last-push items naming it; art jobs' uploads and paintings in any state (a queued message then finds no job and the worker drops it); reports it filed or that name it (the maintainer keeps the mailed copy of a report about it; the stored one goes with the account, my reading of "leave nothing behind"); and the rest of `ACCOUNT#<sub>` (profile last; the `LEFT#` copies only here, so a retried deletion still finds the circles it left). Before all of that, other members' mutes, blocks and last-push items naming it go first, while the circles they are found through are unchanged, and a **tombstone** `DELETED` / SHA-256 of the subject (two hours, longer than any access token of it lives) makes every other call with its still-valid access tokens a 401, so an outbox put or a refresh in flight can't write the account back (`GET /v1/account` would otherwise recreate the profile); only a retried `DELETE /v1/account` gets through. The hash names nobody, so the scan test still finds nothing. Then the Apple token is revoked and the Cognito user deleted (`AdminDeleteUser` by the token's `username` claim, the federated `signinwithapple_…`/`google_…` name, last, so a retry still has a valid token until the end).
  - **Circles**: one where others remain is left through #61's `leave` (a creator's circle passes to its longest-standing member) and its membership item is deleted; one with nobody else in it is deleted outright with its feed, species entries, goals and rollover entry (other past members' feed items in it go too: the circle is gone). A circle it left earlier keeps no membership item of it; one it was the last to leave and still names as creator is deleted.
  - **Goals: reassigned to nobody** (the choice the ticket asked to record): a custom goal it made stays with its circle with `created_by` removed (it shows no "by", and only the circle's current creator may delete it), since the circle chases it together; a goal its find completed keeps `completed_at` and drops `completed_by`.
  - **New items so deletion needs no scan**: `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `INVITE#<token>` (written beside each new invite, same `ttl`) and `ACCOUNT#<sub>` / `LEFT#<circle id>` (in the leave or removal transaction). Everyone who ever shared a circle with the account is read from those circles' membership items before anything changes. **Gap**: invites made and circles left before this deploy have no such item; the invites expire and leave with their `ttl`, and only the maintainer's test accounts exist that far back (accounts are Debug-only, #88).
  - A shared photo uploaded for a card never sent is unknown to the server (the API can't list the bucket); the art bucket's one-day rule for pending photos removes it (#66).
  - **The test** "deleting an account leaves nothing behind" (`backend/tests/test_account_deletion.py`) builds an account with something of everything (circles made, shared and alone, one of another's, one left; finds automatic, shared with a photo, painted; reactions both ways, one buffered; pushes, digests, mutes and blocks both ways; goals made and completed; invites; art jobs ready and queued; reports both ways), checks more than 40 items name it, deletes it, and scans the whole table for any key or value (sets and maps included) containing its id: none. Further tests: the handoff and the solo circle deleted, the others' feeds, reactions, goals and flushes carrying on, no Apple code or Apple refusing, a Google account, deleting twice, a circle others left, one it left last, and the Apple adapter (the client secret verified as ES256, the code exchange and the revocation form, a refused code).
- **The Apple token** (what's possible with Cognito in the middle): Cognito does the Sign in with Apple code exchange itself, as the Services ID, and keeps Apple's tokens; neither the app nor the API ever sees one (Cognito has no API to read or revoke a federated identity's upstream tokens). Apple's `/auth/revoke` takes a refresh or access token issued to the client asking. So at deletion the app asks Apple once more, natively (`ASAuthorizationAppleIDProvider`, `SystemAppleReauthorization`, one Face ID sheet), which gives an authorization code for the **bundle id's** client; the API exchanges it at `/auth/token` and revokes the refresh token at `/auth/revoke`, both with a client secret (ES256, 5 minutes) signed by the key in `birdjournal/apple-sign-in` (the accounts wizard's; the Lambda may read it). The Services ID is grouped under the app's primary App ID, so revoking the app's token ends the Apple ID's authorization of the group, Cognito's Services ID included (to confirm on a device: Settings → Apple ID → Sign in with Apple no longer lists the app). A cancelled sheet, a Google account or Apple refusing never stops the deletion. The app gains the `com.apple.developer.applesignin` entitlement (Debug's entitlements file, like the others; the App ID already has the capability from the accounts wizard). `GET /v1/account` gains `provider` (`apple`/`google`, from the token's username) and the ID token's `cognito:username` says the same on the phone, so only an Apple account sees the sheet.
- **The app**: Settings → Account, signed in, gains **Blocked accounts** (each with Unblock) and **Delete account** (an alert saying what goes, that it can't be undone, that the journal stays, and that Apple will ask for an Apple account; then Apple's sheet; three tries on a server error; then the session forgets its tokens without revoking or unregistering, since the server already did both, and the screen says the account was deleted). The circle page's member menu (touch and hold) gains **Report <name>…** and **Block <name>…** (a confirmation saying what it hides and that they aren't told; a hand marks a blocked member; Unblock in the same menu), and a friend's find in Recent finds gains **Report this find…** and **Block <name>…** (the page presents the sheet and dialog: a sheet hung on a list section never presents, #65). The report sheet: the four reasons, optional words, Send, "Thanks. Your report was sent." Blocking reloads every open feed. Settings → About gains **Privacy policy** and **Terms of use**, in every build, opening `https://birdquest.matthewcelia.com/privacy` and `/terms`. Debug: `-autoScreen account-delete|blocked|circle-report`, `-fakeAccountDeletion fails`, `-fakeAppleReauthorization cancel`; the fake circle server keeps reports and blocks (a blocked owner's finds leave its feed). Screenshots in `docs/screenshots/71/`.
- **The site**: the home page, privacy policy and terms now live in `infra/site/` on the invite domain (CloudFront's viewer function serves `/privacy` and `/terms` from their `.html`), updated for accounts, circles, finds (what a find carries and never carries), shared cards and photos, notifications, reactions, goals and Compare, scene and photo art painted on Lightsail VR's own machine with the uploads' and paintings' retention, moderation, reports, blocks and in-app deletion (what it removes). The invite page links them instead of GitHub Pages. **The GitHub Pages copies** stay, each with a "moved" notice and a canonical link to the new address: pushed to `gh-pages` once the deploy had put the new pages up.
- **Accounts stay off in Release** (#88): `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` is still Debug-only. Turning it on is the maintainer's call; the PR lists the steps (the build setting, Release's entitlements: associated domains, `aps-environment` production, Sign in with Apple; `AccountSwitchUITests`; the Accounts "Off in Release" bullet; App Store review notes and the privacy questionnaire).
- **Tests**: backend `test_safety.py` (22: a find's report stored with its snapshot and mailed, a shared card's note in it, an account's report, who may report, never oneself, a find's report names the find, malformed reports, 20 a day then 429 and again tomorrow, a failing mail, tokens; blocks hiding finds from the blocker only and coming back on unblock, reactions out of the blocker's counts only, no reaction pushes, no find pushes while others still hear, a buffered digest dropped, compare 404, the list with names, who can be blocked and unblocking always, not in mutes), `test_moderation.py` (13: a flagged upload failing at submit and never queued, a clean one queued, a flagged painting failing and deleted, a repeated ready after it asking Rekognition nothing, a clean one ready, alcohol passing, a flagged shared photo withheld and the card sent again without it, a clean one reaching the circle, nothing checked with the switch off, off by default, the Rekognition adapter's request and an unreadable image), `test_account_deletion.py` (13, above, with the old tokens refused after deletion and allowed again past the tombstone), `test_accounts.py` (the provider); infra `test_safety.py` (no SES identity in the stack, `SendEmail` on the address and its domain only, Rekognition and the two S3 reads, `AdminDeleteUser` on the pool only and the Apple secret, the environment, the switch and address by context, a bad switch refused), `test_invite_site.py` (`/privacy` and `/terms` rewritten, the pages present with the topics, no page pointing back to GitHub), `test_art.py` allows the painting read; app `SafetyTests` (deletion for Apple with the code and Google without, a cancelled or failed Apple sheet still deleting, retries, the username's provider, the alert's words; a find's and a member's report bodies, 429, the blocks routes, blocking over the fake hiding and unblocking restoring a friend's finds, the fake's report rules), UI `SafetyUITests` (delete account through the alert to the signed-out screen with the notice, cancelling keeps it, Blocked accounts empty, reporting Maya, blocking Maya hiding her finds and unblocking bringing them back) and `PrivacyLinkUITests` (the link in Settings).
- **Deployed 2026-11-02** (with #70 and #107 from `dev`): the stack UPDATE_COMPLETE, the API's environment with `MODERATION=off`, the report, blocks and deletion routes refusing a missing token (401), `birdquest.matthewcelia.com/`, `/privacy` and `/terms` answering 200, and the GitHub Pages privacy page showing the moved notice. CDK's broadening-approval prompt needs a terminal, so from an agent the deploy ran `deploy.sh`'s steps with `--require-approval never` after reviewing the IAM diff.
- **Not yet verified**: a report mail through SES end to end (`infra/scripts/safety-wizard.sh` sends a test one), Rekognition on real pictures (once switched on), and on a device: deleting a real Apple account (the app signs out, the next sign-in is a new, empty account, and Settings → Apple ID → Sign in with Apple no longer lists the app) and a Google one.

## Featherguild: the name in every user-facing string (issue #115) — built 2026-11-02, the maintainer's console renames pending
- **The name**: the product is **Featherguild** wherever a person reads it (#110 slice 0). The home screen (`INFOPLIST_KEY_CFBundleDisplayName`, Debug and Release), the launch splash, the six permission prompts (`INFOPLIST_KEY_NS*UsageDescription`, both configurations; words only), Settings (the registration text, "Support Featherguild", the toolkit warning), the tip jar's pitch and privacy text, the Listen blockers, the audio source sheet, the microphone error ("Allow it in Settings > Featherguild.", the display name's Settings entry), the pack row's "Needs a newer version of Featherguild", the rarity explanation ("the birds of the Featherguild packs"), the account deletion texts, the report sheet, the push explanation, the circle invite (subject "Join <circle> on Featherguild") and its notifications line, the lens's friend banner notice and its two status cards (`LensCardRenderer`), and the invite site's four pages (`infra/site/`: only the name, the policies' substance and dates unchanged).
- **Share**: the text reads "Heard with Featherguild · Powered by BirdNET"; the export's title "<species> · Featherguild"; the caption under a glasses snapshot "My glasses snapshot · Featherguild" and the bare caption "Featherguild".
- **The backend**: only the report mail reaches a person, so its subject is "Featherguild report: …" (a mail filter on "BirdJournal report" needs updating). The FastAPI title "BirdJournal API" and the package stay internal. It rides the next deploy.
- **Internal names stay** (CONTEXT.md, "Names"): the bundle ID, targets and schemes, `BirdJournalApp`, `BirdJournalKit`, modules, Keychain items, `BirdJournal.storekit`, the `birdjournal_api` package, the stack and its tags, the `birdjournal://` scheme, the `BirdJournal-` multipart boundary, the pack index URL on the `lightsailvr/BirdJournal` repository, and the `MyBirdJournal-*` icons and preview images. The tabs keep Journal / Field Guide / Circles until #117; Circle becomes Guild in copy with #118.
- **Not in the repository** (the maintainer's): the App Store Connect app name and TestFlight text, the Google OAuth consent screen's app name ("Bird Journal", `infra/scripts/accounts-wizard.sh`), and the Sign in with Apple Services ID description. The wizards' own wording (accounts, push, safety) still says Bird Journal, as it describes those console settings.
- **Tests**: the UI tests that matched the old name (`SupportUITests`, `PushesUITests`), the share text (`SharePolicyTests`), the microphone error (`ListeningCoordinatorTests`, `ListeningSessionTests`), the lens cards (`LensCardRendererTests`), the invite page (`infra/tests/test_invite_site.py`) and the report mail (`backend/tests/test_safety.py`). Screenshots of the launch splash and Settings → Support in `docs/screenshots/115/`.
## Set covers (issue #114) — built 2026-11-02; covers chosen 2026-11-03, Los Angeles v10 published, the other four packs' rebuilds pending (#58)
- **What a cover is**: one watercolour landscape per regional set (design guide pages 3–4 and 14), for the Atlas tiles and the Listen tab's ready state, painted on comfybox by FLUX.3 Image Klein **from text alone** in the set's `edition.theme` (`set.json`): no bird in focus, no animal, person, building, road or sign, no lettering, no map, no location marker. Illustrative, never evidence of a sighting's place. The template is `art/cover-prompts.json` (version 1), **kept apart from `prompts.json`** so a cover change never repaints pack art (every art lock records `prompts.json`'s version); the workflow is `art/workflows/set-cover.json`, `pack-art-text`'s model, encoder, sampler and 16 steps.
- **The canvas**: FLUX.3 paints **1664 × 944** and `covers.py` keeps the middle **1536 × 864** (16:9; no resampling). Its watercolours often end in a ragged paper edge or a white margin a few dozen pixels wide; naming it in the prompt ("no white paper margin, no torn edge") painted more of it on the trial of 2026-11-02, so the crop does it instead. The Atlas tile (about 2.4:1) and the Listen header (about 3:2) crop from the middle; the contact sheet draws both faintly. About 5 s a painting warm.
- **Review, like pack art but a choice**: a round is **four candidates** from consecutive seeds (`crc32("<pack id>/cover/<round>")` and the next three). `cover.lock.json` beside the definition (committed) pins the theme, workflow, prompts version, model, each candidate's seed and SHA-256 (`packbuilder/cache/covers/<sha256>.png`) and the review: `pending`, `approved` (candidate `chosen`, from 1 as on the sheet) or `rejected` (the next run paints the next round; the rejected seeds and the reason stay). A warm lock paints nothing; a changed theme, template, workflow or model paints a new round and withdraws an approval; an approved cover gone from the cache is repainted from its seed and goes back to review if the picture differs. `packbuilder cover <definition>` paints without building; `packbuilder build` runs the same step (`--no-cover`, and `--no-art` skips both art steps); `packbuilder cover-review --approve N | --reject --why`. A failed painting never stops a build: the pack ships without a cover. The sheet is `build/pack-art/<id>/cover.jpg`; the pack art's sheet drawing now deletes only its own `sheet-NN.jpg` and `overview.jpg` there.
- **In the pack**: the approved cover as `cover.avif` (AVIF at the pack art's quality 60) and one row in a new **`cover` table** (`file`, `workflow_version`, `seed`, `model`), **added to schema 4 without a bump**: an older app never opens the table, and a schema-4 pack built before it has none, which `SpeciesPack` reads as no cover (it checks `sqlite_master`). The LICENSE gains "Set cover": an AI illustration, not a photograph, from the theme alone, the model and its license, the workflow and the seed; `report.json` gains `cover` (review, ships, seed). The credits screen already shows the LICENSE; `SetCover.credit` gives the one-line "AI illustration: FLUX.3 Image Klein, set-cover@1 cover-prompts@1, seed N" for #118.
- **In the index** (my choice of how the publisher gets it): approving writes **`cover-thumbnail.jpg`** (480 × 270 JPEG, quality 72, about 30 KB, no metadata; 512 × 288 was 39 KB, and the whole index is 84 KB without covers, fetched on every refresh) beside the lock from the cached painting and records its SHA-256 in the lock; both are committed. `packbuilder index` carries it as `cover` `{thumbnail (base64), width, height, model, workflowVersion, seed}` and **refuses** a thumbnail that is missing or isn't the approved one, as it refuses a stale set. So publishing needs no cover cache and no pack download, the same as the set. The order is approve, rebuild, pin, publish, so the pack and the index show the same cover.
- **Reading** (`Pack`): `SpeciesPack.cover` / `coverURL`, `PackDescriptor.cover` (`PackIndexCover`, decoded leniently: an unreadable cover costs only the cover), and `PackLibrary.cover(setID:)`: the full cover of the pack on the phone, else the index's thumbnail, else nil for #110's designed fallback. No app view changes here: the Atlas and Listen display is #118.
- **Painted 2026-11-02**: four candidates for each of the five sets (round 0), the locks committed pending, the paintings in the main checkout's `packbuilder/cache/covers/` so the approval needs no repaint (elsewhere `packbuilder cover` repaints them from their seeds), the sheets in its `build/pack-art/<id>/cover.jpg` and copied to `docs/screenshots/114/`.
- **Tests**: packbuilder `test_covers.py` (16, stub ComfyUI: a round from text in the theme at the canvas size, a warm lock painting nothing, the sheet surviving the pack art's, approving and its thumbnail, numbers off the sheet refused, a rejected round's new seeds and kept reason, a changed theme withdrawing the thumbnail, an approved cover repainted from its seed, a differing repaint back to review, ComfyUI out of reach, only an approved cover in the pack with its credit, an empty table without the step, the index's thumbnail and credit, no cover without approval, a stale thumbnail refused, the two commands); `PackTests` `PackCoverTests` (the fixture's cover decoding at 1536 × 864 with its LICENSE credit; schemas 2 and 3, a schema-4 pack without the table and an empty table all reading as none; the index's cover, none, an unreadable one, and the cached index keeping it; the library's thumbnail before the download and full cover after). `make_fixture_pack.py` gives the test pack an approved cover; #113 (PR #120) also regenerates `test-pack.zip`, so whichever merges second reruns the script.
- **Chosen 2026-11-03** (the maintainer asked for the best landscape per set, revisable later; PR #123), round 0: Los Angeles 2, San Diego 2, Bay Area 3, Deserts 2, Orlando 2, picked for how each crops to the Atlas band and the Listen header.
- **Shipped** in Los Angeles v10 (below, #113). The other four packs are still at schema 3 and take their covers at their schema-4 rebuild (#58). The index carries a set's thumbnail once its pack is rebuilt with the approved cover.

## Story titles in the packs (issue #113) — built 2026-11-02; the Los Angeles titles reviewed and published in v10, 2026-11-03
- **What**: each species' lore gains a **story title** of 2 to 5 words, the heading of #110's story-first card front ("The Waterside Mason" in the design guide, a proposal there, not data). It is editorial content like the lore: written by `claude-opus-5-5` from the fact sheet, citing the fact ids it rests on, and approved by hand.
- **Prompts v5** (`lore.py`): new lore is written with its title in the same request (the schema gains `title`, `{text, facts}` like the tagline). Lore already locked (all 160 Los Angeles entries, prompts v4, approved) gets its title from a **title-only request**: its own system prompt (`TITLE_SYSTEM`) and schema, the fact sheet, then the locked lore and tagline "which the title heads", so the title matches the story without the story being rewritten. Both prompts share the title rules: an epithet from what the bird does, makes or where it keeps, imaginative rather than taxonomic, nothing its cited facts don't say, never the bird's name, no numbers, varied openings and endings across the set.
- **The checks**, at the build: 2 to 5 words; at least one citation and every cited id on the sheet; no number (the lore's rule: figures or words, "one" left out); **no name**: none of the scientific name's words, the word for its kind (the common name's last word and its last hyphenated part: "scrub-jay" and "jay"), the person it is named for ("Anna's" gives "anna"), or a hyphenated marking from its name ("red-tailed"), each also as a plural or possessive and inside a hyphenated word ("owl-eyed"). A plain word of the name stays allowed ("The Acorn Keeper", "The Black Sentinel"): alone it doesn't name the species, and the bird's story is often in it; the guide's three proposals pass. **Variety**: as `OPENING_SHARE` caps the lore, at most 15 % of a set's titles may begin with the same two words ("the night"), and, my addition, at most 15 % may end on the same word ("hunter"), since "The X Hunter" would be the titles' "Ask why". A request names the crowded openings and endings before they are full. A failing draft goes back with what was wrong, three drafts at most; a title-only failure leaves the lore as it is and no title. A locked title edited by hand that fails the checks stops the build, as for the lore.
- **The lock**: the title is its own object in the species' entry, `{text, facts, model, prompt_version, review}` (plus `why` when rejected), added after the entry's other keys, so the lore, tagline, facts, model, prompt version and review of an approved entry stay byte for byte (tested against the committed Los Angeles lock). Its review is separate: `packbuilder lore-review --approve-title`, `--approve-all-titles` and `--reject-title` (with `--why`) touch only titles, and `--approve`/`--reject` only the lore. A rejected title is written again alone with the reason; a rejected lore is written again with a new title. `--regenerate-title <species>|all` asks for another title; `--no-titles` makes no title-only request. A dataset fact the title cites that changed (#86) sends back the title alone; one the lore cites sends back both, as before. The review file lists every title together at the top (to judge them as a set), then each species' title with its cited facts above the tagline and lore.
- **The pack**: a nullable `title` column at the end of the `lore` table, filled only for an approved title on approved lore. Still **schema 4**: older apps name their columns, so they read the new packs unchanged, and `SpeciesPack` checks the column (`pragma_table_info`) so a pack built before it reads as untitled. `PackLore.title` (nil when absent) is there for #116's card front. A shipped title's cited facts count in `lore.sources`; the LICENSE adds a sentence on the titles when the pack ships any. `report.json` lists shipped lore without an approved title under `unreviewed.titles`; that lore still ships.
- **Fixtures**: `test-pack.zip` was rebuilt with the phoebe's title ("The Waterside Sentinel"); `test-pack-untitled.zip` is the schema-4 pack from before, kept as written then.
- **Tests**: pytest `test_lore.py` against the stub client: the word count, citations, numbers, names (ten named titles refused, the guide's proposals and plain name words allowed), new lore with its title in one request and a bad title sending the draft back, titles for the committed Los Angeles lock leaving it byte for byte with no request on a warm rerun, `--no-titles`, a failed title leaving the lore, a set's openings and endings capped, `lore-review` approving and rejecting a title alone through the CLI (and the rejected one written again with its reason), regeneration on request, a changed cited fact sending back the title alone, a hand-edited title stopping the build, and a rebuild shipping the title only once approved; `PackLoreTests` (the fixture's title, the untitled schema-4 pack, and every Los Angeles species titled from v10).
- **Written 2026-11-02** (`packbuilder lore`, the main checkout's cache): a trial of three (Black Phoebe, House Finch, Great Egret: 3 requests, 3,804 input and 700 output tokens, $0.03), then the rest with `--lore-budget 6`: **166 requests** (157 titles and 9 drafts sent back, all for a sixth word; none for a name, a number, a citation or a crowded opening), 216,657 input and 44,889 output tokens, **$1.76**. In all **169 requests, $1.79**, under the estimate of about 190 and $2 to $5: the outputs ran about 270 tokens, not 500 to 1,500. One request stalled for the SDK's 10-minute timeout and was retried; the stalled attempt's tokens, if billed, aren't counted here. Every one of the 160 species has a cited title, left **pending**; the approved lore, taglines and reviews are unchanged byte for byte. Of the set: 118 titles of five words, 33 of four, 9 of three; the most common opening "Keeper of" (10 of 160, under the cap of 24); one exact repeat ("Keeper of the Hidden Cup", Ruby-crowned Kinglet and Orange-crowned Warbler), which the checks don't refuse, so it's the review's to settle. The tests that copy the committed lock now take its titles out first (`la_lock`), so they still check that writing and reviewing titles leaves the lore byte for byte, and `test_the_committed_los_angeles_titles_pass_the_checks` runs every committed title through the build's checks.
- **The review** (2026-11-03, PR #126): 159 approved. The Orange-crowned Warbler's "Keeper of the Hidden Cup" was rejected as a repeat of the Ruby-crowned Kinglet's and rewritten as "The Trill That Fades Away" (f4, its descending trill; 1 request, 1,265 in / 246 out tokens, $0.01), then approved. The whole set took 170 requests, $1.80. The checks don't refuse repeats yet; the review caught this one.
- **Los Angeles v10** (2026-11-03): rebuilt from the shared cache with the 160 approved titles and the approved cover (#114). `pack art: 0 painted`, `set cover: 0 painted`, `lore: 0 requests`. 160 species, 785 photos, 266 sounds, 160 paintings, the same gaps as v9. 191.7 MB against the 200 MB budget. Released as `pack-us-ca-la-v10`, pinned, and published in the index. PackTests 61 passed. A worktree build needs the gitignored `models/` files cloned in (`cp -c`) for the sounds check.

- **Still to do**: the maintainer's review (`lore-review --approve-all-titles`, `--reject-title ... --why`, then `packbuilder lore` for the rejected ones), then rebuild and publish Los Angeles v10 from the cache with nothing repainted, together with the approved cover (#114), and update the pin.

## Featherguild slice 1: the design tokens and the story-first card (issue #116, of #110) — built 2026-11-02, maintainer review and a device check pending
- **Tokens** (design guide 1.0, page 9; `Design/Theme.swift`, the `Journal*` and `Collectible*` colorsets, each with an Increase Contrast variant): the page `#10292D` dark / `#F4F2E9` light, the raised surface `#193A3D` / `#DFEAE5`, the primary text `#F5F1E7` / `#15363A`, the primary action (`Color.moss`, the name kept) `#BCE3D9` / `#15363A`, the shared-goal lavender `#B9BEDD` / `#E8E6F0` (new: `Color.sharedGoal` and `Color.onSharedGoal`, for #118's goals), as the guide gives them. `AccentColor` follows the primary action. The asset names stay `Journal*` and `Collectible*` (internal identifiers keep their names).
  - **Derived**, since the guide's values are "starting tokens": the secondary ink `#A9BFBA` dark / `#4A6366` light (at least 6.3:1 and 5.2:1 on both surfaces), the text on an action `#10292D` / `#F5F1E7` (11:1), the rules `#2C5054` / `#C5D1CC` (decorative; 3:1 or more under Increase Contrast).
  - **Legendary gold** is the guide's `#D3B475` in the dark, but the light `#886424` measured 4.37:1 on the raised surface, so the light token is `#7F5E20` (4.8:1 on raised, 5.3:1 on the page, 4.7:1+ on the card face). Gold has one job: the Legendary label, keyline and perimeter on the card, and the Legendary reveal's wash. Rarity is said in words and never gets the gold frame.
  - **Increase Contrast** variants darken the light inks to `#0A2124` and lift the dark ones to white, every text pair 6:1 or more.
  - `DesignTokenTests` resolves every colorset in light, dark and each with Increase Contrast and checks every text pairing at 4.5:1 (ink, secondary ink, action and gold on the page and the raised surface; the text on an action, on the gold wash and on the lavender; everything that sits on the card face), the card's edge at 3:1 on the light page, the rules at 3:1 under Increase Contrast, and that the guide's own values are what it set.
- **The card is an ivory object in both appearances.**
  - `CardSurface` draws the face (`#F5F0E3`, the tone of the pack paintings' paper) with a modest radius, a visible outer edge (teal `#2F5F5C`, or the still gold foil on a Legendary card), a fine inner keyline and a quiet botanical sprig in each top corner of a front or tile. The same anatomy goes on every species.
  - It sets its subtree to the light scheme, so the system controls on the back (the Heard/Seen picker, the outlined buttons) draw for ivory, and the page tokens read on it.
  - The old `cardFace`/`cardIvory` names remain as the face and its ink, so the circle feed's fallback thumbnail (#118's screen) follows without an edit.
- **Type** (page 8, `CardFont`): the bird's name in New York semibold at the `title` style (about 28 pt), upright; the scientific name in italics; the story title at `title3` (about 20 pt), upright; the lore excerpt in New York's true italic at `callout` (16 pt) with 5 pt of extra leading; the edition line in serif capitals with open tracking. The footer, the art credit and every control stay in the system sans. All of them are native text styles, so Dynamic Type scales them.
- **The front** (`CardFront`, page 5) runs in this order:
  - the edition (`CITRUS EDITION`, from the set) or `LEGENDARY · YOUR FIRST` in gold, centred
  - the name and the scientific name
  - the pack painting with its compact credit ("AI illustration", "Reference photo", "AI-embellished from your photo", "No art yet") under it; a tap on the painting flips the card
  - the story title, when there is one
  - the lore excerpt
  - a rule and the quiet footer: the place · the date · Heard or Seen, the rarity in words ("Common here in early November", or "Rarity unavailable for this place and season"), then the collector number after a fine rule
  
  **The painting** sits whole in a square slot, feathered at its edges into the face (`PaperFeather`); pack art is 3:4 and painted to its edges (the tail and the feet reach them), so cropping it to the card's proportions would cut the bird. Scene and photo art and reference photos fill the slot.
  
  **The card grows**: nothing is clipped, shrunk or scaled to keep a 5:7 silhouette (readability wins, as page 7 says). At accessibility sizes the names and the excerpt wrap, and the footer stacks.
  
  **Fallbacks**: no story title, and the excerpt leads; no lore, and the tale is left out; no art, and a quiet bird on a faint wash, labelled "No art yet". The place, rarity and Uncharted wording are #52's and #54's.
- **Story titles** (#113, which merged first): `CardFront` and `CardTile` read `PackLore.title` from the species' pack, and an explicit `storyTitle` overrides it (the accessibility test sets one). A pack built before titles, or a title not yet approved, gives none, and then the excerpt leads. The bundled Los Angeles v10 (#127) carries the reviewed titles, so its cards lead with them. The app never invents one.
- **The excerpt** (`LoreExcerpt.front`, sentences by `NLTokenizer`) takes the leading whole sentences of the approved lore while they stay within 40 words. If they come to fewer than 25, the next sentence may stretch the excerpt to 50. The first sentence is always kept whole. On the 160 Los Angeles species this gives 25–50 words each, most within 40, all ending a sentence (checked over the bundled pack in `LoreExcerptTests`).
- **Thumbnails** (`CardTile`, `GalleryTile`) show the label (just "Legendary" when the full line won't fit a tile; VoiceOver still hears "your first"), the name, the painting with its credit, the story title when there is one, and the date · Heard or Seen with the collector number. They never carry the paragraph. The grids keep two columns, one at accessibility sizes.
- **The back** (`CardBack`, "Encounter story", page 15) runs in this order:
  - the encounter: when, where, Heard or Seen, and the caption written for this sighting (#56, moved off the front)
  - the note
  - the lore: the tagline, the full lore, its credit and the Wikipedia revision link
  - the natural history: the type line and the wingspan, weight and diet of #55 (moved off the front), then habitat to lifestyle and the datasets
  - the evidence, with View original photo
  - the rarity, in words and with its explanation
  - "Art and sources": what the art is and what it was painted from
  
  The species, the sighting and the evidence stay in separate sections.
- **One footprint, the front's.** #52 stacked both faces so the card kept the taller face's size. With the stats, type line and caption moved back, the back is far longer than the story-first front, which would leave a tall empty front. So `CollectibleCard` measures the front and gives the back that height, and the back scrolls inside its own surface. The turn still has a stable footprint, and the front stays a card-shaped object.
- **The flip** is otherwise #52's: 480 ms about the vertical axis with both faces mounted, the labelled Flip control above the card (it stays in reach of a card taller than the screen), only the face that is up exposed to VoiceOver, and Reduce Motion switching faces without turning. `StoryCardAccessibilityTests` now walks the real front and back with app accessibility on: the art, names and excerpt with the front up, the encounter, the tagline and the natural history with the back up, and neither face's elements on the other.
- **The share card** renders the same front at 360 points. Its text now ends with the lore's credit and revision link, because the image carries a CC BY-SA excerpt (page 17: lore licenses and revision links travel with shared exports). The name strings in it are #115's.
- **The Legendary reveal** shows the restyled `CardTile` and is otherwise unchanged (#117 has the rest of Listen).
- **No schema change**: only views, colorsets and fonts changed. The collection rules of #51–#55 are untouched (one card per add, Legendary as the earliest surviving card, collector numbers as species positions).
- **Build note**: the app target's "Remove stub onnxruntime.framework" phase races the framework's CodeSign step in incremental builds ("bundle format unrecognized" on an empty `onnxruntime.framework`). A fresh `build/DerivedData/Build` builds every time, and the agents' full runs start fresh. It is not changed here.
- **Tests**: app `LoreExcerptTests` (the 40-word cut, the stretch under 25, a long first sentence kept whole, `!` and `?`, empty lore, every bundled species), `DesignTokenTests`, `StoryCardAccessibilityTests`; UI `CardFlipUITests` gains the story-first front (the Legendary heading, the excerpt ending a sentence, no story title, the footer, the credit, the visible Flip action, the caption on the back), and the missing-place case reads the footer line; `CardStatsUITests` finds the type line and stats on the back.
- **Screenshots**: `docs/screenshots/116/` (see its README paragraph), in dark, light and accessibility extra large (`ax3`): the Legendary and the edition front, the back, the grid, the share sheet and the reveal.
- **Not yet done**: the maintainer's look against page 5, the regional sprig per edition (one motif for now), and a device check of the flip and the feathered art on hardware.

## Featherguild slice 2: Collection, Listen and the app shell (issue #117, of #110) — built 2026-11-02, maintainer review and a device check pending
- **Tabs** (design guide page 10): Listen, **Collection** (`leaf`), **Atlas** (`map`) and **Guilds** (`person.3`), with Guilds still behind `AccountSession.isEnabledInThisBuild`. Only the labels and icons changed: `RootView.Tab`'s cases (`journal`, `guide`, `circles`), the routes, `JournalView` and the `Circle*` models keep their internal names. The rename landed first on the branch so #118 could rebase onto it. The Atlas page's own title and its "Field guide & regional sets" subtitle, and the Guilds pages' titles, are #118's (`Guide/`, `Circles/`); so are the remaining "journal" lines on the Guilds and packs screens.
- **"Journal" becomes Collection** wherever it is a label or a title: the tab and its page, "Add to collection" in a candidate's review, "Added to collection" on a run's row, "Added to your collection" after an add, "Remove from collection" on a sighting (its dialog now says the card goes with it). The journal survives as the Sightings mode.
- **A card's page hides the tab bar** (`.toolbar(.hidden, for: .tabBar)` on `CardDetailView`), so the card and its excerpt get the whole height; back brings the bar back. The card itself is #116's, untouched.
- **Collection comes back as it was left** (page 10): `CollectionState` (`Journal/CollectionState.swift`), owned by the root, holds the mode, the search (the filter), the Cards sort and each mode's scroll offset, so a card opened and closed, another tab and back, or the page rebuilt all return to the same place. Nothing is stored: a new launch starts on Cards, newest first, at the top.
  - The mode picker is pinned under the title (a pinned section header), so it is in reach anywhere down a long collection, and each mode keeps its own scroll: Cards halfway down, Sightings, then Cards again comes back halfway down.
  - The offset is measured with `onScrollGeometryChange` and restored with `ScrollPosition.scrollTo(y:)`. The two can disagree by the collapsing large title, so a restore checks where it landed and corrects once.
  - Offsets reported while the page is covered (a page pushed on the tab's stack, or another tab: `isCovered`, from the root's path and tab) are not saved. At the foot of the grid, hiding the tab bar for a card's page shortens the bottom inset and clamps the offset; once the way back has settled the saved offset is put back.
  - **Sort** (Cards only, a Sort menu in the bar): Newest first (the default and #51's order), Oldest first, Collector number (by set, then the species position of #54; Uncharted last). A new sort starts from the top. It orders cards; it never changes which card is Legendary or what a collector number is.
  - The Cards view shows the set with the most collected species above the grid ("Los Angeles · 3 of 160", its edition, a bar), opening the set's page, as the boards do.
- **Listen, ready** (page 11): the local set's cover (`PackLibrary.cover(setID:)`, #114) runs under the status bar as the page's atmosphere, darkened at the top behind the wordmark and "Wonder is nearby." and fading into the page under it, labelled "AI illustration". Without a cover, a designed edition fallback (a dusk wash, a low horizon and the perched bird, in fixed colours like the paintings). Then the source row with a connection dot (moss when the source can listen now, a hollow ring when not), the microphones line, the blockers and errors as before, one large **Start listening**, and **Your local set**: "Los Angeles · Citrus edition", "3 of 160 species collected" (distinct species, page 12) with a bar, Explore the set, and the newest card of up to two of its species. The local set is the set of the newest card, else the bundled pack's, else the first the phone knows (`LocalSet`). The navigation bar gives way to the cover there; Settings sits on the cover, and the title stays "Listen" for the back button.
- **Listen, active**: no cover or other decoration: the source with its dot, the time, the waveform, "5 species heard · 0 added" and the place, centred, then the candidates, with a calling bird's row raised behind its moss ring, and Stop pinned above the tab bar. The notices (paused glasses, no location) are unchanged and come before the list.
- **Adding stays explicit**: a candidate opens its bird, then **Add to collection**, with "Added as Heard. You can mark it Seen on the card." under it. Heard stays the default; Seen is still the deliberate change on the card's back (#52). Every Add is still one card (`CardsUITests`).
- **The Legendary reveal** (pages 15–16): "Added to your collection" in small serif, "Your first Black Phoebe." in the large serif, the Legendary tile inside a restrained orbit (one fine gold ellipse with two stones, drawn once as it opens and then still; drawn at once under Reduce Motion, and left out at accessibility sizes, where the card takes the width), a light impact haptic as it opens (`sensoryFeedback`, so the system's haptics setting turns it off), "Your earliest card of each species is Legendary.", then **Keep listening** as the primary action ("Done" once the run has ended, for a reveal over the run's review), Share this discovery, See it in my collection (which opens Collection at the top of Cards, search cleared), Undo while the add's window is open, and "Your card is already saved." The background is the page with a soft gold light behind the card: the reveal's wash, still gold's one job. It never replays: it belongs to the add (#51), and reopening a card shows the card. A repeat sighting keeps the quieter acknowledgment, "A new card for your collection" with Undo.
- **The listening accessory** stays on Collection, Atlas and Guilds while a run is on, unchanged; on a card's page it goes with the tab bar.
- **No schema change**, and nothing in listening, identification, the lens or the glasses changed: the views, the root's state and two debug flags (`-autoScreen reveal` over a phone run with `-autoPhoneListening`, and `-autoCardSpecies` for the reveal's species with its real set placement).
- **Tests**: UI `CollectionUITests` (real taps: down to the oldest card, open it with no tab bar, back to the same scroll, Sightings kept across a tab switch, Cards back where it was left, Oldest first kept across the tabs); `CardsUITests` taps Add to collection, sees Keep listening and uses See it in my collection; the Guilds tab in `CirclesUITests`, `PushesUITests`, `ShareCardUITests` and `SettingsUITests`; `ShareCardUITests` steps back from the card's page before tapping Guilds, since the tab bar is hidden there.
- **Screenshots**: `docs/screenshots/117/`, in dark, light and accessibility extra large (`ax3`): Listen ready with the Los Angeles cover, Listen active over the example soundscape, the reveal, Cards, Sightings, Species and a card's page without the tab bar.
- **Not yet done**: the maintainer's look against pages 3, 4, 6 and 15; a device check of the cover, the haptic and the hidden tab bar on hardware; the set covers for the other four packs (#58), which show the edition fallback until then.

## Featherguild slice 3: Guilds and the Atlas (issue #118, of #110) — built 2026-11-02, maintainer review and a device check pending
