- **Guild, in every user-facing string** (design guide page 10): the Guilds list, a Guild's page, the join screen, the new-Guild sheet, the invite (subject "Join <name> on Featherguild", message "Join my Guild <name> on Featherguild"), the store's error messages, the push explanation, the report and block texts, the feed's "First for the Guild", Compare birds' footer, Settings → Account's sharing footer, the account texts, the share sheet ("Your Guilds", "Send to 1 Guild"), and the invite site's four pages (`invite.html`, `index.html`, and in `privacy.html` and `terms.html` the product word only, their substance and dates unchanged). Capitalised as the guide writes it, "a Guild". The tab label is #117's. **Internal names stay Circle**: `Circle*` types, `CircleStore`, `Route.circle`, `/v1/circles`, `circle_first`, the `-fakeCircles` and `-autoScreen circle*` flags, the accessibility identifiers (`circlePageShowing`, `share-circle-…`) and the tests' names. The lens's friend banner never named a circle, so `FriendBanners.swift` is unchanged. **The backend**: only the goal push's fallback for a circle whose name wasn't read reached a person; it says "Your Guild" now (`pushes.py`, copy only; it rides the next deploy). A push otherwise names the Guild itself ("First for Sunday Group").
- **A Guild's look** (`Circles/GuildIdentity.swift`, page 13): an emblem and a habitat cover originally chosen from the circle's ID (FNV-1a). **Issue #181 supersedes the emblem choice:** a creator may choose and later edit its mark and tone, stored and sent with the Guild; no stored emblem keeps the same ID hash. The emblem is one nature mark (leaf, bird, tree, water, hills, sunrise, flower, wind, moon, drop) on a deep disc in aqua or a muted local colour (slate, sage, clay, reed, mist) with a light mark of the same hue, the same in both appearances: never gold (the personal Legendary's) and never lavender (shared goals'). It is identity, not a rank or a purchase: there is nothing to earn or buy. The cover is a designed `LandscapeArt` of a quiet habitat (forest, wetland, coast, meadow, hills, fog), never a place. Member presence is initials on muted discs, "+N" for the rest.
- **The Guilds list**: a card per Guild (cover, emblem, name, "4 members · You started it", the faces, "View Guild"), and this month's goal on a lavender strip ("9 of 14 species this month", a bar and the share), read once per Guild with its members when the list appears; then "Join with an invite" (the app has no scanner: a link, or the Camera on the QR code) and "Guilds are invite-only."
- **A Guild's page** leads with belonging: the cover, the emblem and name, "N members · Invite-only", the faces and **Invite friends**. Then the goals; then **Finds** | Compare birds; then **Your sharing**, the same "Share my finds automatically" setting as Settings → Account, kept in view with what a find carries and that notes and original photos stay on the phone unless a card is shared with them; member admin and settings come last. The navigation title is inline, since the header carries the name.
- **Goals** (page 13: calm progress): the monthly card is lavender (`Color.sharedGoal` / `onSharedGoal`): the month in small capitals, a headline from the target ("Thirty birds, together."), a bar, "23 of 30 species" and "7 still to discover", or "Reached! Every new bird this month is a bonus." **The countdowns are gone**: "28 days left" and "Last day" on the monthly goal, and "7 days left" on a running custom goal, which now reads "3 still to discover"; an upcoming goal still says when it starts. A reached goal no longer turns gold: its bar fills, and a custom goal shows a quiet check. Progress is still the server's count of distinct species (`GoalWordsTests`, `CircleGoalTests`).
- **The feed leads with the artwork**: the member and "Pasadena · 12 minutes ago", the marks, the art large (a pack painting whole on the card's ivory in a square, as on the card; scene or photo art and reference photos filling 4:3), then the bird and Heard or Seen, the shared card's extras, and the reactions. With no art URL yet the row shows the species' pack painting from an installed pack before falling back to its reference photo. **Marks**: Legendary keeps the gold wash; "First for the Guild" is an outlined aqua capsule; a rarity is its word. VoiceOver reads the bird first ("Black Phoebe. Heard by Maya Ortiz. Pasadena · 2 hours ago. First for the Guild."). Reactions and Compare birds are as built.
- **The Atlas** (the Field Guide tab's page; pages 4 and 14): titled "Atlas" with the subtitle "Field guide & regional sets" (`navigationSubtitle`), searching birds and regions together. Each set is a tile: its cover (#114's `PackLibrary.cover(setID:)`: the pack's painting, else the index thumbnail) or **the designed fallback from its edition** (`Guide/Landscape.swift`: Citrus orchards, a Coastal headland, Fog ridges, Desert dunes, Wetland reeds, drawn flat in natural colours, the same in both appearances, no motion); the name; "Citrus edition · 160 species"; **"Ready offline"** for a pack on the phone, apart from **"18 of 160 species collected"**, the birder's progress. Sets on the phone come first, then "Somewhere new" with each one's download. Then Bird packs (downloads, updates and storage), the line "Collect through real discoveries. A download adds field-guide pages, never birds", and the field guide's birds. A set's page leads with its tile.
- **Transfers and their recoveries** (`PackTransferStatus`): Download with the size, a bar with "41.2 MB of 173 MB · 24%" and Cancel, Installing, Ready offline, Update, and each failure with a title and its own action, sorted from `PackLibrary`'s words (`PackFailure`): **no connection** ("You're offline", Try again; packs on the phone keep working), **interrupted** (Try again; the download starts over), **a failed install** (Download again; nothing installed, the collection unchanged), **low storage** ("Not enough space", Check again, and Settings → General → iPhone Storage). The Atlas itself shows "Couldn't check for sets" with Try again when the index can't be fetched, over the last good index. Pack rows and the pack page carry the set's cover, and the page adds "Species collected", "Ready offline" and "Your collection: not changed by downloading or removing a pack". **Removing a pack** says it keeps every card, sighting, note and original photo, and `AtlasPackCollectionTests` proves it: a download adds no sightings or species, and a removal leaves the sightings, the note, the glasses photo and an attached original photo as they were.
- **Profiles stay generous**: the profile gains **The tale** (the story title and the reviewed lore in the italic serif, with its credit) and **At a glance** (the type line and #55's stats), and "Every credit and source" a tap away. The species' credits page adds the stats' datasets, the pack painting (an AI illustration, with what it was painted from) and the set cover's credit.
- **Avoided** (page 19): no ranks, XP, streaks, countdowns, leaderboards, glints or motion; gold only on Legendary.
- **Debug only**: `-autoPackStates 1` to `4` shows every transfer state, a screenful a page, on the packs page (the states pass too quickly to catch), and `-autoScrollTo guild-feed|atlas-new|profile-tale` scrolls those pages once, for the screenshots.
- **Tests**: app `AtlasPackCollectionTests`, `PackFailureTests`, `AtlasIdentityTests`, `GoalWordsTests` (no countdown, the headline), `CircleFeedTests` (the Guild mark); UI `CirclesUITests` (the Guilds list's members and goal; a Guild page's Invite-only header and visible sharing toggle), `GuideUITests` (the Atlas tile's "Ready offline" apart from "0 of 160 species collected"; the profile's tale and credits), and the Guild copy in `CirclesUITests`, `PushesUITests`, `FindsUITests`, `GoalsUITests` and `ShareCardUITests`; infra `test_the_pages_say_guild_never_circle`; backend `test_a_goal_push_without_the_circle_name_says_your_guild`.
- **Screenshots**: `docs/screenshots/118/` (`capture.sh` takes them), in dark, light and `ax3`.
- **Not yet done**: the maintainer's look against pages 3–4, the four other packs' full covers once they are rebuilt (#58; until then their tiles show the index's thumbnail, and a set with neither draws its edition's landscape), and the backend's "Your Guild" fallback rides the next deploy.

## Featherguild slice 4: states, contrast and the screenshot review (issue #119, of #110) — built 2026-11-02, maintainer review and a device check pending
- **Every state of page 16, designed in both appearances** (`docs/screenshots/110/`, `state-*`). Most existed; they now share the tokens, plain words and a next action where there is one:
  - **No cards yet**: the perched bird, "No cards yet", what a card is, and **Start listening** (opens Listen). Shared view: `StateNotice` (`Design/StateViews.swift`), a quiet mark, a serif title, the words and the actions, centred.
  - **No Guilds yet** and **signed out**: #118's, with the titles in the ink token and marked as headers.
  - **Offline**: the Guilds list and a Guild's page now say "Can't reach your Guilds", that the collection, listening and the Atlas keep working, and Try again (`GuildsUnreachable`; it was a bare line). The Atlas's "Couldn't check for sets" and the packs' "You're offline" are #118's. The packs list's fetch failure no longer prints the system's error string.
  - **No location**: the run's "No location" notice with Open Settings, and the card's "Place not recorded" (#52).
  - **Unknown rarity**: "Rarity unavailable for this place and season" on the card (#52).
  - **Art pending and art failed**: both lines now start "Your encounter is saved." ("…The artwork couldn't be painted this time."), since page 16 says a failure never loses the sighting; Retry art stays under the card (#68, #69).
  - **Empty search**: Collection and the Atlas say "No birds match “kiwi”", what the search reads (common or scientific names; regions too in the Atlas), and **Clear search** (`NoSearchMatch`), in place of the system's `ContentUnavailableView.search`. "No field guide yet" (no pack at all) offers See bird packs.
  - **Denied permissions**: the microphone ("Microphone access is off", with the denial's words and Open Settings), location (the run's notice), notifications (a Guild's settings: Open notification settings, and the footer saying finds arrive only in the feed).
  - **Disconnected glasses**: #28's blockers on Listen (Glasses not connected, No glasses linked, Register with Meta AI, each with its way out), unchanged.
- **No diagnostics in a normal page**: `DeveloperView` and its Settings link are DEBUG-only (checked). Two raw error strings left the release screens: the packs list's fetch failure and the credits' missing license (`String(describing: error)`), now plain words. The VoiceOver walk below fails on any label containing "Developer", "DEBUG", "Optional(", "nil", "Error Domain" or "UUID".
- **Contrast, measured** (`TokenPairingTests`, beside #116's `DesignTokenTests`; light, dark and each with Increase Contrast):
  - every text pairing the views draw at 4.5:1: the ink, secondary ink, aqua, accent, gold and alert on the page and the raised surface; the text on an action, on the gold wash (ink, aqua and gold) and on the lavender; the card face's (#116's test);
  - glyphs and marks at 3:1: the connection dot and progress fills, the status ring, the goal bar on the lavender, the error mark;
  - **the materials**, measured on rendered pixels in a window of the test host: the toast's regular material and the bar material (Stop's bar, the review sheet's actions), each over the page, a raised surface and the card face, with the ink and the aqua at 4.5:1 in all four variants;
  - **the system glass** on full-size screenshots (`docs/screenshots/110/measure-glass.py`): the listening accessory over the ivory cards now 12:1 (title) and 7:1 (summary, time) in dark, 11.6 and 5.7 in light, 16.5/11.8 and 15.3/9.1 with Increase Contrast.
- **What the measuring found and fixed**:
  - The **system red** of errors and destructive actions measured 3.2:1 on the light page and 3.6:1 on the dark raised surface. New colorset `JournalAlert` (`Color.alert`): brick `#A3322A` light, salmon `#F4A79B` dark, `#7E1F19` / `#FFC9C0` with Increase Contrast; at least 5.6:1 on both surfaces. `InlineError` shows error lines with a mark, so colour isn't the only signal; in-page destructive buttons (Leave Guild, Sign out, Delete account, Unregister, Remove from collection, Remove pack) take it. Alerts, confirmation dialogs and menus stay the system's.
  - The light **Legendary gold** `#7F5E20` (#116) measured 4.4:1 under the feed's Legendary mark on the gold wash; it is `#7A5A1E` now (4.7:1 there, 5.7:1 on the page, 5.2:1 raised, 5.6:1 on the card face). The dark gold is the guide's.
  - Listen's **paused dot** was the system orange, 2:1 on the light page: now a hollow ring in the ink while starting, paused or stopping, beside the filled aqua dot of listening.
  - The **listening accessory** sits on the system's glass, which turns light over the ivory cards even in the dark appearance: its ivory inks measured 1.5–2.2:1 there. Its contents now sit on the page colour (94 %), where the tokens hold.
  - **Not ours to fix**: the system tab bar's own labels over the ivory cards in the dark appearance measured 2.3–2.9:1 (white on the light glass); with Increase Contrast the glass turns dark and they measure 13.7:1+. Listed for the maintainer.
- **VoiceOver** (`ScreenAccessibilityTests`, the app tests, #52's in-process walk with app accessibility on): Listen, Collection, a card's page, the Atlas and a Guild's page, hosted with the app's shared objects in memory (the seeded collection, the fake account and Guild server), at the default size and at the largest accessibility size. Each checks the screen's elements in reading order (Start listening before the local set; the Guild's name before Invite), Legendary in words, no countdown, only the card's readable face, no unlabelled element (a search field counts by its prompt, as VoiceOver reads it), no diagnostics.
  - **Found**: the face that is up exposed its hidden decorations (the edge, the keyline, the sprigs, the fine rules) as six empty VoiceOver stops. `FlipFace`'s `.accessibilityHidden(false)` on the face that is up undoes `.accessibilityHidden(true)` inside it (checked with a probe: a parent's `false` re-exposes a hidden child; as a `.contain` container it doesn't). `FlipFace` now makes the face a container first.
- **Dynamic Type at AX5** (`ax5-*` screenshots of the five screens): the card's "LEGENDA…" label, the Atlas tile's "Ready offli…" and a Guild's "4 members…" truncated; they wrap now. The Listen cover's "AI illustration" credit sat under the source row (which rises 36 points onto the cover) at every size; it sits above it now. The Atlas's system subtitle still truncates ("Field guide & region…"), listed. The two-column grids fall back to one column, as #116 set.
- **Reduce Motion**: #52's `CardFlipUITests.testReduceMotionSwitchesFacesWithoutTurning` (the `-reduceMotion` flag; a UI test can't turn the system setting on) and `CardFlipTests`; the system value is read-only in SwiftUI's environment, so there is no in-process variant.
- **A decision left open**: an upcoming custom Guild goal shows "starts in 4 days" (#118 kept it while removing the countdowns of running goals). Unchanged; listed in the PR for the maintainer.
- **Debug only** (screenshots): `-autoQuery <text>`, `-autoThenTab <tab>`, `-fakeGlasses unregistered|noneLinked|notConnected` (the Listen page's glasses states; the view substitutes them, `GlassesConnection` is untouched), `-fakeCircles offline`, `-autoScrollTo guild-settings`.
- **No schema, backend, endpoint or pack change.** The layouts the earlier slices settled are untouched; the board differences are listed in the PR.
- **Tests**: app `TokenPairingTests`, `ScreenAccessibilityTests`; UI `SceneArtUITests` reads the new failure line.
- **Screenshots**: `docs/screenshots/110/` (`capture.sh`, index in its README): every reskinned screen and every state in dark, light and `ax3`, the five walked screens at `ax5`, and the glass with Increase Contrast.

## Featherguild: the rename and the reskin (issue #110, slices #115–#119, follow-ups #113 and #114) — built 2026-11-02, maintainer review and device checks pending
- **The name**: the product is **Featherguild** wherever a person reads it (#115): the home screen, the splash, the permission prompts, every screen, shares, pushes, the lens's banner and status cards, and the invite site. The design guide's "Bird Quest" was dropped on 2026-11-02 (two App Store bird apps and a UK trademark application). Internal identifiers keep the BirdJournal names: the bundle ID, targets, `BirdJournalKit`, modules, Keychain items, the backend package, the `project=birdjournal` tags, asset names (`Journal*`, `Collectible*`, `MyBirdJournal-*`), and the `Circle*` models, routes and endpoints.
- **Four places** (design guide page 10, #117): Listen, **Collection** (the journal: Cards, Sightings, Species), **Atlas** (the field guide and the regional sets) and **Guilds** (circles, still behind `AccountSession.isEnabledInThisBuild`), labelled tabs with ordinary back navigation, the listening accessory on the other three while a run is on, and the tab bar hidden on a card's page. Collection comes back as it was left; Guilds use the word Guild everywhere a person reads it (#118).
- **The tokens** (page 9, #116, measured in #119): deep teal and warm ivory, each colorset with an Increase Contrast variant.

  | Token | Dark | Light | Measured |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | Page | `#10292D` | `#F4F2E9` | |
  | Raised surface | `#193A3D` | `#DFEAE5` | |
  | Primary text | `#F5F1E7` | `#15363A` | 10.5–16.7:1 on page and raised |
  | Secondary text (derived) | `#A9BFBA` | `#4A6366` | 5.2–11.9:1 |
  | Primary action | `#BCE3D9` | `#15363A` | 8.8–15.3:1; its label 11–16.7:1 |
  | Shared goals | `#B9BEDD` | `#E8E6F0` | its text 8.3–12.4:1 |
  | Legendary gold | `#D3B475` | `#7A5A1E` (guide `#886424`) | 4.7–10.8:1, the gold wash included |
  | Alert (new, #119) | `#F4A79B` | `#A3322A` | 5.6–11.4:1 |

  The guide's values hold where it set them, except the light gold (4.37:1 raised, then 4.4:1 on the wash). Gold has one job, the personal-first Legendary; shared goals take the lavender; rarity is said in words. Bird art keeps its colours in both appearances, on a fixed ivory card face. `DesignTokenTests` and `TokenPairingTests` keep every pairing measured.
- **The card is story-first** (#116): the edition or Legendary label, the name, the painting whole with its credit, the story title (#113), a lore excerpt of whole sentences in New York's italic, and a quiet footer; the stats, the caption and the full sources on the back; one footprint, the flip with a visible Flip action, only the readable face to VoiceOver, Reduce Motion switching faces.
- **Deferred**: set covers for the four packs besides Los Angeles (#58's rebuilds; their tiles show the index thumbnail or the edition's drawn landscape), a regional sprig per edition (one motif for now), the remaining board differences listed on #119's PR (for the maintainer to take or leave), the system tab bar's contrast over the ivory cards in the dark appearance, and every device check: the flip, the haptic, the hidden tab bar, the cover and the glass on hardware.

## Featherguild 2.0: embroidered patches, one per bird per place (issue #131, slices #132–#138) — decided 2026-11-03, build in progress
- **The direction**: the collectible is an embroidered **patch**, not a card (design guide 2.0, `docs/design/featherguild-2/`). The maintainer reversed guide 1.0's "tactile without skeuomorphism" on 2026-11-03: collected objects (patches, the felt board, the paper card, the share image) are rendered as materials, while chrome, lists and controls stay native and matte. The patch exploration that led here tried five treatments (die-cut, merit badge, souvenir shield, woven, hoop) on comfybox; the maintainer picked the merit-badge style.
- **One patch per species per edition.** The first sighting of a species in an edition sews its patch; later sightings there add to its count; the same species in another edition sews that edition's patch; a sighting outside every set adds to the species' Uncharted patch. **Legendary is retired**, with the gold edge and the gold token: every patch is the one patch, and getting it is the reward. Sightings stay the record (every Add is one sighting); a patch's sewn date is its earliest surviving sighting; deleting the last sighting of a species in an edition unpicks its patch. A patch is derived from the sightings like the card was (`CardCollection`), so no SwiftData migration.
- **Editions look different**: each set's patch has its own shape and cord (Citrus circle, olive; Coastal oval, sea-blue; Fog hexagon, silver-grey; Desert arch, terracotta; Wetland shield, deep teal), twill and stitched motif from its theme. No text is stitched.
- **The app does the lighting**: a patch ships as colour plus a height map (and a stitch order for the stitch-in); the app lights it from the device's tilt. The paper card behind it carries the lore, the biology and the bird's sighting history there.
- **The patch is the only art**: the watercolour pack art, scene art and photo art are retired (the maintainer's decision, 2026-11-03), which also frees the Los Angeles pack's budget (191.7 of 200 MB, the watercolours 18.7 MB) for patch art. Original photos stay as evidence.
- **What the samples showed about the art**: FLUX.3 keeps the pattern of light, well-lit birds in thread, but dark and dim references fail (the black phoebe and the cedar waxwing grew pale patterns on every seed), and a prompt clause keeping black birds black turned the House Finch's blue-grey belly black, so it was dropped. Patch art therefore runs through the same fidelity gate and colour fit as pack art did, on the bird's mask inside the patch, and dark birds are checked by hand. BiRefNet sometimes cuts out the bird rather than the patch, so the patch is cut from its white card (flood fill and a convex hull: every shape is convex), and BiRefNet finds the bird inside it.

## Patches slice 1: the patch model, and Legendary out of the phone and the lens (issue #132, of #131) — built 2026-11-03
- **A patch is derived, like the card was**: `CardCollection` groups the surviving sightings by species and placement (`CardSet.placement`: a set, or Uncharted) into `Patch`es. A patch has its sightings newest first, its count, the sighting that sewed it (the earliest surviving one, ties broken by identifier as before) and its latest. Nothing is stored and there is no migration. Deleting a sighting lowers the count and moves the sewn date on; deleting the last one unpicks the patch; a newly known set pulls older sightings into its patches. The type keeps its `CardCollection` name; `cards` is still every sighting, newest first.
- **The add**: `SightingRecorder.Recorded` and `RunSightings.Addition` say whether the add sewed a patch (`sewsPatch`) and the patch's count after it. The recorder computes this from the species' surviving sightings and the sets the phone knows, before it writes. `ListeningCoordinator` reveals only an add that sewed a patch ("New patch · Citrus edition", "Your first House Finch in Los Angeles", or "Your first House Finch" outside every set). A repeat is acknowledged "Seen again · 8 times in Los Angeles". The reveal (`PatchReveal.swift`, was `LegendaryReveal.swift`) keeps its card tile until #135 brings the stitched patch.
- **The review**: the button reads "Add and sew patch" for a bird new to the place the run is in and "Add sighting" otherwise; a live or reviewed row adds "new here" or "seen 7 times" to its heard line. Both read the whole collection (`@Query`) with the run's location.
- **The lens**: `LensEarnedCard` became `LensEarnedPatch` (`newPatch(collectorNumber:)`, `seenAgain(count:)`), so the saved line reads "New patch · 021/160", "New patch · Uncharted" or "Seen again · 8 times". `LensElement.saved` lost its Legendary flag, and the star with it; every saved line has the check.
- **Collection**: the first mode is **Patches** (its case keeps the name `cards`), one tile per patch, still drawn by `CardTile` with today's art until #134. A tile's footer reads "Seen 8 times · since Sep 14, 2026" and it opens the patch's latest sighting until the patch page (#134). The sort orders patches: the most recently sewn first, oldest first, or by collector number. A search keeps the patches with a matching sighting. The summary reads "7 patches · 21 sightings". The species rows, the gallery and the profile count sightings. Removing a sighting asks "Remove this sighting?" and says when it would unpick the patch.
- **Gold is gone**: the gold edge, foil, label, wash and orbit, the `CollectibleGoldInk` and `CollectibleGoldWash` colorsets and their contrast pairings. The reveal's wash and orbit and the collection intro take the moss. The feed marks a find that sewed a patch "New patch" in a moss capsule. The push explanation says "sews a new patch".
- **Interim, until #136**: the finds' and pushes' `legendary` flag (`FindOutbox`, `FindPolicy`, `CircleClient`, the backend) now carries "this sighting sewed a patch", which is what made a find notable. The art job's `legendary` field does the same until #137 retires scene art. The server's push alert still reads "Legendary · …" until #136 changes `pushes.py`.

## Patches slice 2: patch art in the pack builder (issue #133, of #131) — built 2026-11-03 to 2026-11-04; all 160 Los Angeles patches reviewed and approved, the pack rebuild ships with #134
- **The step**: `packbuilder patches` (and `build`, in place of pack art, for a pack whose edition is in `art/patch-prompts.json` and whose `art.lock.json` names its references) paints one patch per species from the isolated reference bird its pack art used, with the `patch-art` workflow, flat on white card under even light, so the app does the lighting. Prompts and workflow are versioned apart from pack art's, so a patch change never repaints pack art. The other four packs have no art lock yet, so they keep the old step until their references are isolated (#58).
- **Each edition's patch is data**: shape, cord, twill and motif by pack id in `art/patch-prompts.json`, not in `pack.json`, because `sets.py` pins the edition and refuses a change to it.
- **The bird stands where it lives** (the maintainer's review, 2026-11-04): version 1 put every bird on a branch; version 2 takes `art.setting` from `overrides.json`, so ducks swim, shorebirds stand on sand and woodpeckers cling to bark. 94 of the 160 were painted again over the review rounds, for this, a peeled corner or their markings.
- **`art.patch` in `overrides.json`** pins what the maintainer finds on the sheets. `reference` paints from another of the pack's photos, isolated from its original when the cache lacks it: a Great-tailed Grackle and a Heermann's Gull whose pack art references were a brown female and a brown immature, and an American White Pelican whose reference wore a numbered wildlife wing tag, which the thread copied. `markings` states plumage facts in the prompt and never names the species. `box` places a bird the detector can't see (a poorwill on bark mulch).
- **Thread lightens dark birds and invents pale patches**, so a bird whose isolated median L* is under 22 gets the black-thread clause, and black species get "solid black, no white" in their markings. For every bird, the clause turned a House Finch's blue-grey belly black. 35 was too high a threshold: it caught finches and quail.
- **The measure ranks, the review decides**: thread colour moved a good House Finch (ΔE ~20) about as far as a failed Black Phoebe (~23), so there is no gate and no retry on it, and no colour fit, because the bird's mask inside a patch can take in the leaves beside it and a fit would recolour them. The maintainer reviews every patch on the contact sheets. The one automatic retry is geometric: FLUX.3 sometimes paints a lifted corner of the card, and a patch with more than 1.5% bare card in its outer band is painted again with the next seed, three times at most.
- **New prompts don't repaint reviewed patches**, unlike pack art: the review is the costly part. A rejected patch takes the current templates.
- **Cutting**: from the white card by flood fill and a convex hull, since every edition's shape is convex. BiRefNet takes the bird as often as the patch; given the patch's interior with everything outside the cord filled with twill, it finds the bird (sometimes with the leaves beside it), which is what the measure uses.
- **In the pack, schema 5**: a `patch` table and `patch/`, the colour on transparency at 720 px on its longest side (AVIF, about 83 KB) and half-size layers (AVIF 4:4:4, about 39 KB) with height in red, the cord in green and the stitch order in blue. The cord's depth is 25 px at 720 px on every edition. Full-size layers would have put Los Angeles at about 215 MB; with these it is about 194 MB of its 200, once a build with patches stops writing pack art. `pack_art` stays as an empty table until #137.
- **Interim**: the bundled pin stayed at Los Angeles v10 (schema 4, with its watercolours) until #134 could draw patches and published v11, since a pack with patches has no pack art for the cards before it. `BirdJournalKit`'s `Pack` already reads schema 5 (`PackPatch`, `patchURL`, `patchLayersURL`).
- **Running it**: about 20–30 s a patch on the box itself and about 25 s to a minute from the Mac, depending on the tailnet. A pinned reference's isolation needs the detector and the originals, so run those from the Mac.

## Patches slice 3: the lit patch, the felt board and the paper card (issue #134, of #131) — built 2026-11-03 to 2026-11-04
- **Decoding**: AVIF decodes block their thread, so they run two at a time on their own queue, never Swift's cooperative pool: a board's dozen at once held every pool thread and hung the test host, with the AV1 decoder waiting underneath. A tile scrolled away before its turn is skipped. Each decode is redrawn into a plain bitmap: an ImageIO thumbnail can stay a lazy provider, and it decoded again whenever the renderer asked for its pixels, which a moving light does every frame (about 120 AV1 decodes a second, none with the light still). After the redraw, a sweep logs no decodes and a flat 16.7 ms on the simulator, whose display is capped at 60 Hz. **120 Hz on a phone isn't measured yet.**
- **The light**: `PatchLightModel` is pure and takes gravity in the device's frame, not the attitude's Euler angles, which are unstable when the phone is held upright, and a lamp overhead shouldn't move when you turn on the spot. The first reading is "as held", with the light resting upper-left. Tilt moves it as that lamp would, low-passed over about 100 ms and clamped at 35°. Past the clamp "as held" is dragged along, so the first degree back is a degree of light. Below 0.12° nothing is published: sensor noise never moves the light and it stops when the phone does. `PatchLight.shared` runs Core Motion while a lit surface holds it, and it is the one light every patch reads. Reduce Motion draws at the resting place.
- **The shader**: `patchLit` is one SwiftUI `layerEffect`. The layer is the patch's colour; `.image` (SwiftUI allows one per shader) is the pack's layers, height in red and cord in green. Diffuse and sheen come from the height's Sobel normal, the sheen strongest on the cord. The two-part shadow is tapped from the layer's own alpha inside the shader, opposite the light, so a tilt never re-blurs a SwiftUI shadow. Only `LitPatch`, the leaf, reads the light. Thumbnails decode at half size (360 and 180 px). `-patchLightSweep YES` circles the light and logs frame times; `CADisableMinimumFrameDurationOnPhone` lets a display link ask for 120 Hz. Motion stays at 60 Hz.
- **Patterns are drawn, not painted**: the guide's prototype painted them; issue #134 chose app-drawn. `pattern` in the same shader keeps the painted cord, lays the edition's even twill with a diagonal rib, and draws where the painting's colour changes in transfer pen, over a ring of taps wide enough that the thread's grain doesn't speckle it. The height layer's edges were tried first: thresholds either scattered scratches or noise, and no bird. The twill is the edition's token, not the pack's `ground`: `ground` is measured beside the cord and swings with what was painted there, and a board of patterns should read as one set. `ground` is the fallback for a set the app doesn't know.
- **`trim_rim` (packbuilder)**: `cut_from_card` can leave a pale crescent of card under its soft shadow along one side (2–7% of a Los Angeles patch). It never showed on the white review sheets; on felt it read as a paper disc under the patch, and it shifted the cord mask, which is measured from the alpha edge. The writer now trims it when it writes the pack: it grows in from the edge through what is smooth and unsaturated, so stitched thread stops it however pale, then takes the convex hull. It only changes the alpha, so `patch.lock.json`'s reviews and hashes stand.
- **One footprint**: a painting is drawn to fill its edition's aspect, so a circle whose painting came out 3% oval is drawn round (the largest correction on Los Angeles is about 6.5%, after the trim). The guide asks that an edition's patches share a shape and size.
- **The board**: Collection's first mode opens on the place of the newest sighting (the guide's "the set you're in"), a set or Uncharted. The Board menu in the toolbar chooses another; `CollectionState` keeps it. Your patches come first in collector-number order, then the patterns in collector-number order, as guide page 6 shows them: a strict merge left a birder's few patches behind rows of patterns. That order is the board's, so **the Patches sort is gone** (#117's newest, oldest, collector number; `CardSort`). A search reads every board, so a bird sewn elsewhere is still found; patterns match too. Three columns, two from xxLarge text, one at accessibility sizes: the mock's three, and the guide's "drops to two columns, then one". A pattern opens the bird's profile in the Atlas.
- **The patch page and the card**: `Route.patch` is keyed by species and placement, since a patch isn't a sighting. The flip keeps `CollectibleCard` and `FlipFace` at 600 ms; Reduce Motion fades the outgoing face fully away before the incoming face appears. The caption and turn-button label use the same split opacity curve in stable `CardFlipText` slots, so quick reversals cannot overlap old and new words (issue #176). The footprint is 330 × 470 pt. The card scrolls inside it, with its footer pinned; at accessibility sizes it grows and the page scrolls. The lore excerpt comes first and only what's left of the lore follows the history, so nothing is read twice.
- **Where the card's back went**: the card holds the species and the patch's history. What was about one sighting moved to `SightingDetailView`, which each history row opens: Heard or Seen, the caption, the evidence and original photo, the rarity in words. Rarity is a sighting's (its place and season), so it's on the sighting rather than the card; guide page 12 allows it on the card, page 10's anatomy doesn't list it. Scene art and photo art controls moved there too (`SightingArtSection`) rather than being lost, until #137 retires them. The type line and the fuller natural history (habitat, migration, lifestyle) stay on the Atlas profile: guide page 10's biology is wingspan, weight, diet, nest, clutch and size.
- **The species view replaces the gallery**: `SpeciesPatchesView` shows the bird's patches with counts and the editions still to find as patterns, or blanks where that set's pack isn't on the phone. `SpeciesGallery`, its place filter, its sort and the card stepper are removed: their point was telling many cards of one bird apart, and there's one patch per place now. "All N sightings" lists a patch's or a species' sightings, each opening its record.
- **What keeps the old card, until its slice**: `CardFront`, `CardSurface` and `CardArtView` remain only for the share image (#136) and the sighting's art (#137). The reveal draws the lit patch now but keeps its orbit until #135. Uncharted patches and birds in a set whose pack has no patches yet show the blank with a paper tag on their page; #138 owns those states' review.
- **Textures**: felt, linen and paper are the guide prototype's own, painted from text alone (`Assets.xcassets/Material*`), about 750 KB together, their edges blended so they tile. Building needs Xcode's Metal Toolchain (`xcodebuild -downloadComponent MetalToolchain`).
- **Publishing**: Los Angeles v11 (schema 5) is pinned and published. A phone on an older build reads schema 4 at most, so its index lists v11 as needing a newer app and it keeps the pack it has. `scripts/publish-pack-thumbnails.sh` wasn't run: it uploads pack art thumbnails, and v11 has no pack art, so friends' feeds keep v10's thumbnails until #136 sends patches.

## Patches slice 3, after the maintainer's phone (issue #134) — 2026-11-04
These overrule design guide 2.0 and the #134 entry above where they differ.
- **The light has to show**: on a phone a tilt looked like no motion. The light followed the tilt, but a 10° tilt changed the patch by about 1 of 255 per channel, because diffuse was divided by the light's own angle, so flat thread never changed, and the sheen and shadow barely moved. Now the whole patch brightens and dims with the tilt, a broad neutral sheen travels across it, the shadow lengthens as well as turns, and the full 35° moves the light further. A 10° tilt now changes it by 3.5 to 4.9. `PatchLightPixelTests` goes from gravity to rendered pixels and fails under 3 of 255 at 10°: testing the light's numbers is what missed this. How strong it should feel is the maintainer's to judge on a phone.
- **No patch until you've found the bird**: the maintainer's call, reversing guide pages 4, 6, 7 and 15. Boards show only sewn patches, and the species view has no "still to find". A patch sewn in a set whose pack has no painting still shows its blank, since you did find that bird. The shader's pattern stays for the stitch-in (#135), which starts from it.
- **The material covers the whole sheet**: Patches mode and the species view, under the bars, with the mode picker on it when pinned. Sightings, Species and every list stay matte, per guide page 13. The keyline panel remains only on Listen's local set.
- **Swiping**: a patch page swipes through every patch (`CardCollection.browsingOrder`: each set in the order known, collector-number order, then Uncharted), in a paged `TabView`. One turned-over patch is held above the pages, so a swipe from a card always lands on the next patch's face. The title reads "3 of 12".
- **Sideways**: the patch on the left and its card on the right, scrolling, with nothing to turn. The project already allowed landscape on every screen; this page is the first designed for it. Gravity is read in the screen's axes, so the light stays upper-left, and a turn of the screen starts it again from there.
- **Search in the Atlas**: `.searchable` had been hiding until a pull down the page, so the Atlas's field is now always in view and each set's page has its own. Collection's search still hides until pulled, as it was.

## Patches slice 4: the sewing ceremony (issue #135, of #131) — built 2026-11-04, with the maintainer's decisions of the same day
**The maintainer's decisions, 2026-11-04.** These overrule issue #135 and the guide where they differ:
- **"Repeat adds ticks up the number and records the sighting."** A repeat add from the phone saves the sighting and says "Seen again · 8 times in Los Angeles" in the toast, with no page and nothing to tap. The toast starts at the old count and ticks over (7 → 8) with a haptic after 0.6 s, so the review sheet has finished closing; under Reduce Motion or VoiceOver it's the new count at once, and VoiceOver only ever reads the new one. Its Undo stays for its six seconds. This supersedes the issue's "the patch lifts, the latest sightings show", so `SeenAgainReveal` and `-autoScreen seen-again` are gone.
- **"The glasses are about hearing and capturing. The celebration of the patch occurs on the phone once the run is over."** The lens stays text only ("New patch · sewn", "Seen again · 8 times"), with no patch image (guide page 20 isn't built), and nothing plays on the phone mid-run. When the run is over, however it ended, the phone plays the stitch-in and reveal for each patch that run sewed from the glasses, in the order sewn. Repeat adds from the glasses play nothing.

How the second is built:
- **The queue** is `ListeningCoordinator.pendingCeremonies`: sighting identifiers only, in `UserDefaults`, with no schema change. The rest is rebuilt from the album when the ceremony plays (`RunSightings.sewnAddition`, so the count and placement are as they are then), and a sighting deleted meanwhile is dropped. `GlassesListeningSession.save` is the only thing that adds to it, so a phone add during a glasses run keeps its ceremony there and then, and its Keep listening.
- **When it plays**: `RootView` starts the next one when the app is active, the launch splash has gone and `hasCeremonyWaiting` holds: no run active or stopping, no reveal up, no candidate's sheet up. So a run that ends in a pocket waits for the foreground, and a run killed mid-way plays at the next launch. After Stop it plays over the review, through the sheet's own `patchReveal`; after Stop on the listening strip on another tab, over that tab, since the review sheet belongs to the Listen tab. A system sheet that happens to be up (Share, say) isn't known to it: the stitch-in would go unseen under it and leave the finished page.
- **It plays once**: an identifier leaves the queue when the ceremony finishes (`finishCeremony`: the stitch-in ended or was tapped through, or Reduce Motion, VoiceOver or no painting went straight to the finished page). Killed part way through a stitch-in, it plays again at the next launch, since it never finished.
- **The wording was this agent's choice, for the maintainer to check**: the run is over, so "Keep listening" would be untrue. With more waiting the primary button reads "Next patch" and the secondary "Skip the rest · 2 more", in place of "See it in my collection", which comes back on the last page. The last page's button reads "Done", as a reveal over the review already did. These pages offer no Undo: it can be long after the add, and a relaunch would have no record to undo. The sighting's own page can still delete it.
- **Tested through a real mock run**: `-autoScreen glasses-run` pairs Mock Device Kit and starts a glasses run with the clip standing in for the stream, and `-autoLensAdd` walks the lens with mock inputs. `-albumFile <name>` keeps the album on disk so a test can relaunch over it. `PacedAudioSource.stop()` now ends at once: it used to play out what the file had already buffered, so a stop on the simulator took as long as the rest of the clip.

The rest of the slice:
- **The stitch-in is the same shader**: `patchLit` keeps its one `.image`, and its `pattern` flag became `sewnTo`, how far along the stitch order (the layers' blue) the thread is laid. Past it there's the twill and the transfer pen, out to the edge, since the cord comes last. At 1 or more all of that is skipped, so a finished patch costs what it did and there's no seam when the reveal takes over. `PatchView.Face` is now `.sewn` or `.stitching(sewnTo:)`; no surface was using the static pattern after #134's "no patch until you've found the bird".
- **The clock**: `StitchTimeline` is pure and date-based (a `TimelineView(.animation)` asks it for a frame), so coming back from the background finds it finished. 0.4 s on the bare pattern, also long enough for a review sheet to finish closing; every thread the same time however much it fills, so the fine details are seen; the cord twice a thread's, since it has the whole way round to go; 0.25 s on the finished patch. Only the patch reads the clock. The caption and the haptic change with the thread.
- **Thread names and swatches are read from the patch**: the pack orders threads and doesn't name them, so `StitchThread.threads` takes each band's commonest colour (its mean was mud, a band's thread is grainy) and `ThreadName` words it from plain colour buckets: "Thread 3 of 10 · Ochre", "Cord · Olive". The guide's board says "Thread 5 of 10" and its text says the thread is named; this does both. The words are approximate by nature: a band of the order isn't one catalogued thread, and a dark bird reads "Black" several times over. A band under 0.1% of the patch is left out, so no tick goes by with nothing happening.
- **Measured, as the brief asked**: `-stitchInSweep YES` loops the stitch-in and logs through `PatchFrameLog`, the frame timing pulled out of the light's sweep. On the iOS 27.2 simulator, with the Mac at a load average of about 250: three windows of 240 frames, median, p95 and worst all 16.7 ms, none over 1.5× budget, and no decode lines in 14 s of the process's log. That is the main thread's pace on a display capped at 60 Hz. **120 Hz and the GPU's cost on a phone aren't measured**, the same gap #134 left; the same flag measures them on a phone.
- **The order on screen is tested in pixels**: The stitch-in's tests in `PatchLightPixelTests` (one serialized suite, since every patch reads the one shared light) compare the share of the patch the shader sews at a given `sewnTo` with the share of pixels at or below it in the decoded file (52.1% in the file against 50.3% on screen at order 85, 82.3% against 82.4% at 170), so a colour conversion on the way to the shader would fail them. They also check that all-sewn equals the sewn patch to under 0.1 of 255, and that the cord is absent before its turn and goes round clockwise from the top.
- **It plays once**: `Reveal.hasPlayed` is on the coordinator, not the view. A run ending under the page moves it from over the tabs to over the review sheet, so `stop()` finishes the ceremony first; the review sheet can't be dragged away from under a page. Whether to stitch is decided once on opening, and once the patch is prepared the settings no longer matter. While a review sheet is up the root's overlay waits (`patchReveal(isCovered:)`): two pages would each have run the clock and the haptics.
- **Undo's six seconds start again when the ceremony ends**: they started at the add, the page only offers Undo once it's finished, and the stitch-in would have used 4.2 of them.
- **VoiceOver skips the stitching too**: the guide only says so for Reduce Motion. Nothing in it can be spoken, so it would be four seconds on a page that says nothing new; the finished page reads the patch in one sentence and VoiceOver's focus starts at its first line. If VoiceOver does land on a stitch-in it's one button, "Sewing your House Finch patch", with the thread as its value, and activating it finishes it. `-voiceOverPath YES` stands in for VoiceOver as `-reduceMotion YES` does for Reduce Motion. #138 owns the wider review.
- **The reveal follows the board**: felt instead of the paper wash, no orbit ("no confetti, rays or orbit sparkles"), the name as the title with "No. 019 of 160 · Los Angeles" under it, "Your first …" under the patch, Share reads "Share this patch". The Close button went: Keep listening is the way back, as mocked. "See it in my collection", Undo and "Your sighting is already saved." stayed.
- **The lens** reads "New patch · sewn" and "Seen again · 8 times"; `LensEarnedPatch.newPatch` lost its collector number. Page 5's "· 8 times in Los Angeles" isn't built, since the brief gave the shorter line.
- **`-autoLocation griffith`** answers with a fix in Griffith Park with no prompt, so a UI test's add lands in the Los Angeles set and sews a patch with a painting; `-autoLocation denied` makes everything Uncharted, which has no painting to stitch. `-stitchInSeconds <n>` slows it down for a test or a screenshot.

## Patches slice 5: finds, pushes and sharing without Legendary (issue #136, of #131) — built 2026-11-04, the backend deploy and the two-device check pending
- **The rename**: a find says `new_patch` and carries its `edition`, the set's pack id (null outside every set), in `FindPolicy`, `FindOutbox`, `CircleClient`, `FriendFind`, `FakeCircleClient`, `finds.py`, `finds_api.py` and `pushes.py`. The edition is on every find, repeats too, since a hand-shared repeat still draws that edition's patch. It isn't frozen in the outbox: it's read from the sighting's placement when the put is sent, as the place is. The flag stays frozen at the add.
- **What the deployed server does (checked 2026-11-04 against its live `/openapi.json` and the code at #71)**: `FindBody` is `extra="forbid"` with no `new_patch` or `edition`. A find carrying either gets a 422, even alongside `legendary`, and the outbox treats a 422 as final and drops the find, so the naive rename would have silently lost every find until the deploy. It sends `legendary` in the feed and in a push's `find`, and nothing else.
- **What a phone from before #136 does with the new server**: it sends `legendary` and no `new_patch`, and its `FeedItem` and `FriendFind` can't decode an item or a push without `legendary`.
- **The seams, so a find stays notable in both directions.** The phone's is one file, `Share/LegacyFindWire.swift`; delete it and its callers after the deploy (`grep -rn LegacyFindWire BirdJournal`):
  - The phone offers the new form first; a 422 (which the old server gives before writing anything) is tried once more with `legendary` in place of `new_patch` and no `edition` (`HTTPCircleClient.putNewFormElseOld`, for finds and shared cards). So the day the server is deployed the app speaks `new_patch` with no release and no flag to flip. It costs one refused request per put until then. A find refused for another reason (a coordinate in the place) is refused twice, then dropped as before.
  - It reads `new_patch` or `legendary`, in the feed and in pushes. With no `edition` from the server it takes the pack from a patch's art key (`packs/us-ca-la/patch/…`), so the feed leads with the right patch before the deploy. Only a patch's key: pack art can come from any pack with the bird, even for an Uncharted find. A painted find's key names no pack, so until the deploy its row leads with the blank (or a pack's painting), with its painting below.
  - A `FindOutbox.json` with `isLegendary` is read and rewritten at once as `newPatch`, so a later build need not read the old name.
  - The server's are separate, and outlast the deploy. `FindBody.legendary`, and `legendary` in `FindFields` and `find_summary`, stay **until no beta phone from before #136 is left**, whenever that is. `_content`'s read of a stored `legendary` stays **until the table's old items are rewritten**: a deploy doesn't touch them (a put moves one), and there's no backfill.
- **Until the deploy, a push's words still read "Legendary · Pasadena"**: the deployed server writes the alert and iOS shows it. The phone can't change that.
- **The feed follows guide page 8's mock**: a new patch is a row led by the patch at 76 pt (stacked at accessibility sizes), "Maya sewed a new patch", the bird, "Heard · Pasadena · 2 hours ago" and its labels. A repeat is one line, "Maya heard a House Finch again · Pasadena · 2 hours ago", keeping "First for the Guild" or Rare and its reactions, since those still matter. The mock's "8 times now" is left out: the count isn't on the wire, and adding it would be a new field the old server refuses. A repeat shared by hand keeps the patch-led row, "Maya shared a sighting", or its note and photo would have nowhere to go. An automatic repeat showing a painting stays a line with the painting under it, until #137. "New patch" is no longer a capsule, since the headline says it; VoiceOver still reads "New patch".
- **The patch in the feed**: the viewer's own pack first, lit like any other, then the CDN thumbnail, then an installed pack's painting while packs without patches remain, then the edition's blank. Never another edition's patch. The find's art is a `pack`-kind key, `packs/<id>/patch/<species>.png`, which the deployed server takes. `packbuilder thumbnails` now writes these 320 px cut-out PNGs and `publish-pack-thumbnails.sh` uploads them, **but they aren't uploaded yet**: that's the maintainer's, and until then a viewer without that pack sees the blank. Los Angeles is bundled, so nobody is affected today.
- **The share image** (`PatchShareImage`, rendered by `CardExport`): the patch at 1080 px on its longest side, on undyed linen with its paper card tucked beneath, 1620 px wide. The pack's paintings are 720 px and its layers 360, so the guide's 1080 is an upscale, which looked sharp enough on the rendered image; a larger source would cost pack size (slice 2). The shader runs in `ImageRenderer`, from a light fixed at `PatchLightModel.rest` (`LitPatch.fixedLight`). `ShareCardTests` checks that in rendered pixels: the light shows, the shadow falls lower-right, and a tilt changes nothing. The card reads the name, story title, "Citrus edition · Pasadena · Nov 3, 2026", "AI illustration · Featherguild" and the credits. Its place is only what the share may carry: the city, "a secret spot", or none with the city off. A pack photo is no longer on the image, so the image doesn't use `SharePolicy`'s license rule (it stays, with its tests, for #137 to judge); the patch's reference-photo credit and the lore credit travel in the text and on the card. `CardFront` and what only it used are removed.
- **Threatened species and the city left off: decided by the maintainer, 2026-11-04.** Showing the region is fine; what stays hidden is the exact location on the map. So the edition and the patch's shape may name the set, a metro-sized region, while the place stays "a secret spot" or none. No behaviour changed. `test_a_threatened_or_city_off_find_names_its_region_and_nothing_finer` (backend: the feed and everything stored) and `FindPolicyTests.regionAndNothingFiner` (the phone's payload) check that neither carries a coordinate or a precise place; a find has no field for a coordinate and the server refuses one in the label.

## Patches slice 6: retire scene art, photo art and the watercolour pack art (issue #137, of #131) — built 2026-11-04, the backend deploy and comfybox's clean-up pending
The patch is the only painting (decided 2026-11-03). This closes out #68, #69 and #70: scene art, photo art, the painting allowance and painted art in finds are retired, and their "not yet verified" lists will never be checked. **Nobody ever had a painting on the server** (the maintainer's words, checked on the live stack 2026-11-04: no `ARTJOB#` item in the table's 9 items and nothing under `art/` in the CDN bucket), so nothing for stored paintings stays on the server.
- **Gone from the app**: `Art/` but for the face blur, `SightingArtSection`, `CardArtView`, `CardText.Art` and its lines, photo art's request and comparison sheets, "Did you see it?", the debug flags (`-autoArt`, `-fakeArt`, `-fakeArtDaily`, `-autoPhotoArt`), and the Album's job transitions. Art's only callers of the upload, the polling and the find hooks went with them.
- **What had to stay in the app, and why**:
  - **`SharedPhoto`** is `ArtPhoto` moved to `Share/`: a shared card's original photo leaves through the same face blur and metadata strip. `maxSide` went, since only photo art's library upload used it.
  - **`PackImage`** stays without its `painting` mode: the field guide, the set page, the credits, the journal rows, the listening rows and the pack page show reference photos through it.
  - **`Sighting.artState`, `CardArt`, `ArtImage`, `ArtJob`** stay as plain Codable, with no schema bump (it is JSON). A stored sighting opens, shows none of it and offers nothing. `filePaths` still lists the paintings, so they go with the sighting; undo keeps them. A stored job stays "painting" forever, unread.
- **The birder's own photo stays, outside painting (the maintainer asked, 2026-11-04).** A sighting heard on the phone gets its patch, the first time, and the pack's reference photos; a birder can also attach a photo from the library. `SightingPhotos`, in the Evidence section of the sighting's page: "Attach a photo" opens the system `PhotosPicker`, which runs out of process and needs no library permission. `JournalEdits.attachPhoto` keeps the file as it came (often HEIC) in `attachedPhotoPaths`, with no painting and no server call, so it goes with the sighting and comes back on undo. It shows on the page; "View original photo" and the share's original photo already read it first (`CardText.originalPhotoPath`), through `SharedPhoto`.
  - **"Did you see it?" stays gone.** Nothing needs it: attaching leaves Heard or Seen as it was, and the picker above it on the page still changes it.
  - **One at a time, with a way to take it off (`JournalEdits.removeAttachedPhoto`).** The old flow had no removal: it appended, and only the first photo was ever shown or shared, so a second did nothing. One at a time makes it plain which photo a share sends, and removal is then the only way to correct a wrong pick short of removing the sighting. A card already shared keeps the prepared copy it sent. A stored sighting with several shows them all.
  - **`-fakeLibraryPhoto YES`** makes the button hand over a generated picture, since XCUITest can't reach inside the system picker.
- **A bird with no patch yet**: `library.patchArt` is nil for any pack not rebuilt with patches (the other four, until #58). The tile, the patch's page, the share image and the feed already draw the edition's blank (`PatchBlank`) for that; none of them read pack art. So a downloaded non-Los Angeles pack's bird shows the blank, and doesn't crash. #138 designs the art-missing state. Its find now carries no art rather than a watercolour's thumbnail.
- **The feed** draws a CDN thumbnail only when its key is a patch's (`FeedItem.Art.patchThumbnailURL`), so a stored find naming a watercolour's thumbnail leads with the viewer's own patch, else the blank. `FindArt`, the painting under a row, is gone.
- **The pack format is untouched.** Removing `pack_art` is a schema change and a rebuild. `Pack` still reads the table and keeps `PackLibrary.packArt`, `SpeciesPack.artURL` and `sourcePhotos(of:)`, with `PackArtTests`, so a schema 3 pack still opens. They go with a later pack schema, after #58. The credits' "Painting" section is gone, since the app doesn't show what it credits.
- **The backend**: the art routes (`/v1/art/*`, `/internal/art/*`), `art.py`, `art_api.py`, the allowance, `ART_AUTO_PAINT`, `ART_DAILY_ALLOWANCE`, the queue and worker token settings, moderation of art uploads and paintings, `painted.py`, `ArtKind.PAINTED`, the art's `fallback` and the `ARTJOB#` walk at account deletion are all gone. A leftover item under `ACCOUNT#<sub>` still goes with the rest of that partition.
- **What a phone from before this sends still passes.** A find from `dev` before #136 carries `legendary` and art of kind `pack` under `packs/`, with no `fallback` (the encoder omits it, and only a painting ever had one), or `art: null`. `test_a_phone_from_before_136_is_taken_as_it_sends_a_find` puts exactly that. Only a phone holding a painting would send `kind: painted`, which now gets a 422 (which its outbox treats as final), and nobody has one. A `kind` of `painted` stored in the table would fail to read, but there is none: on 2026-11-04 no item in the live table had an `art_kind` or `art_key` at all.
- **What a phone on an old build sees after the deploy.** Only builds with `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` (Debug; it is off in Release) ever called the art routes. `POST /v1/art/jobs`, `GET /v1/art/jobs`, a job, `submit`, `received` and `GET /v1/art/allowance` all answer 404. The old client's refresh logs it and carries on; nothing crashes and no find is lost. An old phone's new glasses add (or a job it had pending) stays "Your encounter is saved. Artwork is on its way." for good, since a 404 on create leaves a job pending. Tolerated: answering 409 `not_eligible` instead would clear it, but that keeps art routes alive for a handful of Debug installs.
- **The stack**: `ArtJobs` is gone; `infra/birdjournal_infra/art.py`'s `ArtCdn` stays, because finds' patch thumbnails and shared photos live there, and it stays Retain. The API has no access to `art/*` any more: Rekognition reads only `photos/*`, and shared photos have their own grant there. `artAutoPaint` and `artDailyAllowance` leave `cdk.json`. A deploy deletes the upload bucket, both queues with any message in them, the worker's IAM user and its policy, and the worker token. **Two things can stop a delete, so clear them first (the PR's steps)**: the worker's access key was made outside the stack, and CloudFormation can't delete a user that has one; and the bucket has `auto_delete_objects` off, so it can't delete a bucket with an upload still in it (they expire within a day of the last one).
- **`artworker/` and `art-wizard.sh` are deleted.** Neither `packbuilder` nor `art/` imports from `artworker/`; the dependency ran the other way. `art/` keeps `scene-art.json`, `photo-art.json` and their templates, and `packbuilder art-references` stays, with nothing using them now.
- **`SharePolicy`'s pack-photo license rule, which #136 left for this slice to judge: it stays.** `Picture`, `mayShare` and `shareableLicenses` are live only in tests, since `ShareCardView` passes no reference photo. But the rule is about pack photos, not paintings, so retiring art doesn't need it gone, and `SharePolicy.text` around it is live. Removing it is a separate tidy-up.
- **ADRs**: ADR-0003, ADR-0002, ADR-0004 and ADR-0007 each carry a status line saying what ended. ADR-0004's rule has no exception: nothing painted is left on the server.
- **Copy**: the tip jar says a tip "helps keep the server running", without painting. The privacy page and the landing page drop art jobs and paintings. Account deletion's confirmation no longer lists painted art. Both pages go live with the deploy. The tips' descriptions in App Store Connect are the maintainer's to change: the app shows whatever they say, and the local StoreKit file's two that mentioned painting are changed here.
- **Tests**: Album `StoredArtTests` (every field, the earliest form, unreadable JSON, `filePaths`, a store reopened); app `RetiredArtTests` (a sighting with stored art opens with nothing to show or ask for, a stored painting stays off the find, only a patch's thumbnail is drawn), `SharedPhotoTests` (was `ArtPhotoTests`), `FindPolicyTests`, `JournalEditsTests` (attach, refuse what isn't a picture, remove), `CardFlipUITests` and `ShareCardUITests` (attach, see it, open it, remove it, send it on a card, with the picker stood in for); backend `test_retired_art.py` (the routes gone), `test_finds.py` (`painted` refused, an old phone's body taken), `test_account_deletion.py`, `test_moderation.py`; infra `test_art.py` (the CDN stays; no queue, user, token, upload bucket or setting; nothing on `art/*`).
- **Not yet verified**: the deploy, comfybox's clean-up, and the system picker's own sheet with a real photo on a device (the UI tests stop at the button; `loadTransferable` and a real HEIC were never exercised).

## Patches slice 7: states, accessibility and the screenshot review (issue #138, of #131) — built 2026-11-04, the maintainer's VoiceOver walk pending
- **Not yet found has no pattern, though #138 and guide page 15 asked for one.** "No patch until you've found the bird" (the 2026-11-04 entry above) reverses page 15 and says the shader's pattern stays for the stitch-in, so a bird you haven't found shows no pattern and no blank on the Atlas or anywhere else. It stays as it was: the set page's `SpeciesSilhouette` row with its number and name, and the bird's no-sightings page with its number in each set that lists it, both leading to the profile. **The maintainer confirmed this on 2026-11-04**: "keep the silhouette. The patches are separate from the atlas, which is more like a compendium of birds." The row now reads "Not yet found here": `collected(in:)` is per set, and you may have found that bird elsewhere.
- **The empty collection is the felt, "Your first patch is a bird away.", Start listening and "See the birds to find"** (the Atlas's tab, `openAtlas`). No patterns and no blank, for the same reason; page 15's "local set's first patterns" is what's left out. #119's perched-bird line drawing stays, as it's nobody's patch.
- **`PatchArtState` says what stands in for a found bird's painting**: `.painted`, `.beingSewn` (no painting, and its edition's pack is downloading or installing) or `.tagged` (nothing is coming). A painting that won't decode shows the same tagged blank, not a blank left loading. `PatchView(state:)` takes it; the blank fills in when the pack arrives because `PackLibrary` is observable. Being sewn is tested on the real path: a set known only from the index, with its download held open by a `URLProtocol` that never answers.
- **The tag is on every blank, the board's too**: page 15 asks for it and it's what tells a board's blanks apart. It hangs by a short string from a slot, and it stops growing with Dynamic Type (at `large` on a tile, `xxLarge` on a page), since the same words are in type beside the patch and those grow. The blank also has a cloth now: a twill's rib, or Uncharted's weave.
- **Uncharted has no bird, though page 15 says "and the bird".** Packs paint per edition, so there is no painting to show, and a patch is never drawn with another edition's (guide page 7). It's the canvas round with the brown cord and the name on its tag. The card reads "Uncharted · outside every set" as page 15 writes it.
- **VoiceOver**: the guide's sentence, "House Finch, Citrus edition patch, seen 8 times, sewn September 14", with the year only when it isn't this year's. A patch being sewn ends ", art on its way": "sewn September 14, being sewn" contradicts itself. A number reads "Number 1 of 160 in Los Angeles".
- **Words on a material are in the full ink**, measured on rendered pixels against each texture's worst pixel. The secondary ink got 3.2:1 on the linen and just under 4.5:1 on the felt, so no text on the board uses it (`SetProgressRow.detailInk`, `StateNotice.messageInk`, `NoSearchMatch.messageInk` set it where they sit on the material). The card stock's secondary ink got 4.2:1 on the fibre, so `CollectibleInkSecondary` went from `#4F6464` to `#485C5C` on the Light token. A silhouette's mark got 1.9:1 on the raised surface, so `markOpacity` went from 0.45 to 0.75. Cord against board and transfer pen against twill aren't held to a bar: neither carries meaning alone, since the name is in type beside the patch and the pen only shows during a stitch-in. The PR lists the measures.
- **Increase Contrast**: `.boardLabel()` puts every word on a material on the page colour (tiles, the board's rows, the patch page, the species page, the reveal, the stitch caption, the empty state and a search's no-match), and the felt darkens and the linen quiets. `isOnBoard` keeps the modes that sit on paper from shifting.
- **Reduce Motion is proven across launches**: `testReduceMotionFixesTheLight` measures the patch's pixels with `-patchLight` moved, with a control that shows the light moving without it. A process-wide flag in the app's unit tests would reach other suites running beside them, since Swift Testing runs suites in parallel; for the same reason the walks of `PatchDetailView` use `startsTurned` rather than the `autoCardBack` flag.
- **Seen again isn't a designed state here**: the maintainer decided on 2026-11-04 that a repeat add ticks the number up and records the sighting, with no page and no tap. #135 removed that page, so it isn't reviewed or captured here, and neither is the ceremony after a glasses run, which came with the same follow-up (`docs/screenshots/135/`).
- **What's left on the boards** is listed against the guide in `docs/screenshots/138/README.md`. `-fakePatchArt missing|sewing` and `-autoBoard uncharted` are the debug flags behind its shots; `-autoCardSpecies` now also picks the species page.

## UX cohesion slice 1: the fixes (issue #150) — built 2026-11-05, the maintainer's screenshot review and a device check pending
The UX audit of 2026-11-05, picked over by the maintainer, is #150; this slice is its fixes, the ones that were wrong whichever direction the rest takes.

- **Every list themes its rows.** `PaperList` and `PaperForm` replace `List` and `.paperList()`, so a screen can't forget the raised colour. Ten lists had: Settings, Account, Support, Credits, Compare and others fell back to the system's rows, near-black on the petrol page and white on the ivory one. A row or section that sets its own background keeps it. Measured from the screen in `SettingsUITests`, since the colour is what a birder sees and the view tree can't say.
- **Tap targets.** Reactions keep their small pill and get a 44 pt hit area (they were about 26 pt). Outlined buttons were 40 pt under a comment saying 44; they're 44 now, so rows with one grow by 4 pt.
- **A find's menu is in plain sight.** Report and Block sit behind an ellipsis on a friend's find as well as touch and hold.
- **Words that contain themselves.** Outlined buttons and find marks stay capsules while their words sit on one line and become rounded rectangles at accessibility sizes, where a capsule's ends cut the wrapped words off.
- **Native bars.** Stop, Add and Share use `safeAreaBar` with a hard scroll edge in place of `.background(.bar)`, which cut a flat band above the floating tab bar. `ContrastTests` drops its `.bar` surface, since nothing uses it. Stop takes the outlined style: the birds are the main thing on that screen.
- **Listen's gear is a real toolbar item**, on Liquid Glass over the painting; the hand-drawn dim circle outgrew itself at the largest text. The navigation bar stays visible with its title removed, so the cover's words are placed from the window's status-bar inset, which leaves them where they were. **The wordmark, "Wonder is nearby." and the painting are the maintainer's and don't change.** Measured against a build of `dev` at the same text size, in both appearances, they don't, to the pixel, and the status bar keeps the system's soft scroll edge behind the clock (`docs/screenshots/150/README.md`). The bar has to stay inline, since leaving the mode off changes the pixels over the tagline, where a large title would sit; a pushed page inherits it, so Settings and a set's page ask for their large titles themselves.
- **The source is named once**, in its row. "Using the iPhone microphone" and the tip under Start are gone; the tips live on the Audio source sheet.
- **Heard or Seen is on the review sheet**, as guide board 03 draws it, Heard by default, and an add takes the method through `ListeningCoordinator.add`, `RunSightings.add` and `SightingRecorder.record`. It replaces a sentence telling the birder to mark it Seen "on the card", which moved to the sighting's page in #134. The sighting's page keeps the picker for changing it later.
- **Explanations** on the Audio source sheet, a Guild's sharing and Settings are one line and a `HowThisWorks` disclosure, word for word where the words still hold. The two sharing footers said slightly different things; `SharingWords` is now the one wording.
- **Words.** "Card" goes wherever it meant the old collectible, about 20 strings ("Share card image…" is "Share image", "Your collection and cards" is "Your collection"), while "paper card" stays. "Open in Field Guide" is "Open in Atlas". "A story to share" is "Share this patch". "Your notes, one bird at a time." is gone. The stats credits read "Bird stats".
- **Grammar.** "All 1 sighting" is "1 sighting". A tile says nothing about the count until the second sighting: "No. 003", or "Uncharted" outside every set. A patch's page is titled "Los Angeles, 2 of 5", the position still counting every patch in the swipe.
- **The invite sheet stops printing its link**, which showed the dropped name: it offers the QR code, Share link and Copy link. The QR code's accessibility value still reads the URL, and the UI test reads it, until the host changes in #150's domain slice.

## UX cohesion slice 9, the pack half: true silhouettes and hero photos (issue #150) — built 2026-11-05, the maintainer's review of both and the pack rebuild pending
The pipeline half of The Atlas (A5, F12). The app half reads what this writes. Nothing here is approved, pinned for heroes, published or in `packs/manifest.json`.

- **A silhouette is derived, not painted**, from the isolated reference bird the patch was painted from (`packbuilder.silhouettes`). The first threshold passes closed the gap between a gull's legs into a block, so "fill holes" fills only what the hole's surroundings say is plumage: a ring of the bird around it more than half pale (a Bald Eagle's head blown to pure white) or a pinhole. On Los Angeles the two kinds separate cleanly, at 56% and up against 16% and down, but for one small gap by a Red-winged Blackbird's leg, which closes.
- **Within schema 5, no bump**: a `silhouette` table and a `silhouette/` folder, as `cover` came within 4. A reader that doesn't know them never opens them; a pack without the table reads as none, so the other four packs keep the glyph. PNG in luminance and alpha at 180 px, 16 alpha steps, about 1.8 KB, which the app tints.
- **A rejected silhouette stays out until its reference changes.** The recipe is deterministic, so unlike a painting there is no next seed. `art.silhouette.reference` pins another pack photo with a cached isolation.
- **Seven pins came with the first pass**, where the patch's bird makes a poor shape and a cached one doesn't (Rock Dove, American Wigeon, Northern Flicker, Bufflehead, Hooded Merganser, Downy Woodpecker, Black Oystercatcher). They're choices for the review to overrule: every silhouette is pending. A pin must be in the pack, because the LICENSE credits that photo; one whose photo left the pack stays out and `report.json` says so.
- **F12's cause is the slot as much as the photo.** The profile fills a 3:2 slot from the middle of the phone crop, and a portrait crop loses its top and bottom: Black Phoebe's 1200 px crop has its head, around 250 to 440 px down, and the slot starts at 354. The review therefore shows every first photo as the profile shows it, and the detector's flags compare its box with the slot. Replacing photos is the issue's decision and stays; the slot itself is the app's, and is left to its half.
- **A proposal isn't a pin.** `"hero": {"proposed": ...}` in `overrides.json` is ignored by the build; `packbuilder hero-review --accept` by the sheets' numbers puts the photo first in `include`, with the species' patch and silhouette photos after it, since a pack holds five photos and those two are credited. The first pass proposes 45 of the 160. Whole means head, body, tail and feet, except Brandt's Cormorant, where no cached photo has one whole bird alone. The detector was wrong on 7 of its 44 flags (a bird it couldn't find, a wingtip) and blind to 8 birds behind branches, so the list is by eye.
- **A hero is also the lens photo.** The first photo's lens crop is what the glasses and the Atlas rows show, and the lens score that used to pick it prefers a dark background for the additive display. Proposals are chosen for the profile: some on bright sand or sky will read less well on the glasses, a trade the review should know about.
- **The budget holds.** Measured by trial builds in scratch against v11's 194.59 MB of files (192.82 MB zip): all 160 silhouettes approved, 194.94 MB (193.13 MB zip), so they add 0.36 MB with their rows and credits; every hero proposal accepted as well, 196.18 MB (194.37 MB zip), since sharper photos weigh more. The budget is 200 MB.

## The mascot's art: patches from the app icons (issue #150, slice 11) — painted 2026-11-05, picked by the maintainer
The art half. "The mascot in the app", below, is what became of the picks.

- **Painted from the icons, cut out on white**, with the patch language's words and the glasses and binoculars spelled out. There's a seventh subject, `perched-finch`: the issue's finch for the splash, sign-in and empty states takes over from `PerchedBirdDrawing`, a whole bird on a branch, and it needed a pick of its own beside the icon's portrait.
- **A mascot patch is a circle, on tan or on peach.** Tan and olive are the Los Angeles edition's, so the mascot looks like the real patches; peach and terracotta are the icon's own ground. Both are on the sheets rather than decided here.
- **No measure against the icon.** The patches' ΔE and IoU only ever ranked, and a 3D cartoon rendered in thread is meant to move from its reference, so the review is the only gate.
- **1024 px, not the patches' 720**, from a 1152 px canvas, because it has to hold up as an app icon.
- **`art/mascot/approved/` is the only way out.** Candidates stay in the gitignored cache and can be painted again from their seeds; approval writes the one file that ships and records its hash, as a set's cover thumbnail does.
- **Thin rims are trimmed harder than on a pack's patches** (`RIM_MINIMUM` 0.2% against 1.5%), because a sliver of card that a 720 px patch can keep shows on an icon, and the sheets draw the trimmed patch, so what's reviewed is what ships. A thin warm sliver still survives on some whole-bird paintings on peach (10, 12, 30, 42), where the shadow picks up the twill's colour.
- **Toes are spelled out.** With "feet with three toes" thread wrapped every foot round the branch like knotted cord; the prompt now describes a short leg and three clawed toes and rules the knot out. The busts have no feet and repainted bit-identical from their seeds (24 of 24), which is also the proof that a lost candidate comes back unchanged.

## The mascot in the app (issue #150, slice 11, 2026-11-06)

The maintainer's picks ship as they are: finch on peach, the others on tan, the warbler a whole bird. No shader and no lit layers: they're plain pictures.

- **The icons keep their names**, `MyBirdJournal-*`, because iOS remembers an alternate icon by name, so someone who chose a bird keeps that bird. The hawk stays the primary. The pictures they replace moved to `art/mascot/icons/`, unchanged: the lock pins each patch to its source's hash, and a changed hash starts a new round and takes the approval back.
- **The ground is the app's felt in petrol** (#0E343C, about the felt on the mock's Guild page), the same for all six. Every patch stands out on it, the peach finch most of all, and it's what a patch sits on in the app. Moss looked muddy under the olive cords, and oatmeal lost the tan patches' edge at 60 pt. The patch takes 88% of the icon's width: larger crowds its shadow, and smaller loses the glasses on the home screen.
- **Flattened, with no glass.** One opaque RGB picture with the shadow in it, over a solid fill, `specular` and group shadow off, so the system adds no rim to cloth. A layered icon went black behind the patch in Dark, so the ground wouldn't be the ground. Tinted and Clear are the system's monochrome of the whole square.
- **Size.** iOS keeps three lossless 1024 px renderings of each icon, so an icon's cost follows its entropy, not its picture's size.
