Fine felt fibre cost about 1.3 MB an icon and a 1024 px picture about another 1.3 MB, neither visible by 180 px, so the felt is softened (3 px blur at 1024) and the alternates are 512 px; only the primary, which the App Store shows, is 1024. `Assets.car` for an iPhone went from 42.61 MB to 42.38 MB (0.23 MB smaller), measured from Debug simulator builds: the icons 0.85 MB smaller, the finch 0.53 MB larger than the drawing's picture, the thumbnails 0.05 MB larger.
- **One finch picture**, `PerchedFinch`, 240 pt square with the 200 pt patch and its shadow baked in, because a launch screen can't draw a shadow. The launch screen, the splash and the states all draw it, so nothing shifts between launch and splash. The title keeps its offset from the bottom of the picture, which is taller than the drawing's, so it sits 48 pt lower; that keeps the splash's change to the art, for slice 3. It's the same picture in both appearances, so its shadow is stronger than the lit patches' 38% and 28%: at their strength it vanished on the dark felt.
- **The launch screen can show only its colour after an install over a build with the old picture.** It's the system's cache, not the app: `com.apple.splashboard` keeps the old asset catalog, where `PerchedFinch` doesn't exist. On the simulator, uninstalling first and bumping the version both stayed blank, and once launched that way the blank snapshot survived a restart; restarting the daemon and then installing again cleared it, while a reinstall without the restart stayed blank. A restart between the install and the first launch shows the finch, which is how the evidence is taken. The worst case is the paper colour, and on first launch the splash then brings the finch in. Whether a phone does this after an update is an on-phone check.
- **What ships stops at the cord** (`trimmed_to_cord`, at build time). The cut patches kept pale card and the shadow painted on it outside the cord, up to about 11 px of 1024 and on every bird: plain on the 1024 px primary icon and on the dark launch screen, faint at 60 pt. The outline is the convex hull of the thread near the edge, and the patch is cropped to it so it stays centred. Only the alpha changes, in the app's copies: the approved files and their hashes are as the maintainer chose them. Thread is told from card by the share of its colour, not the amount: (largest channel less smallest) of at least 38% of the largest. An amount (60) took the cord's shaded outer side for the painted shadow and cut up to 32 px into it, thinning the cord by half on the hawk, the egret, the warbler and the jay. Before and after: `docs/screenshots/150/mascot/icon-edges-before-after.jpg`.
- **In a state**, `StateNotice`'s `.drawing`, the patch is 110 pt at the default text size, growing to about 167 pt at the largest, with a cap so the words stay near. The glasses read at 110 pt.
- **Where it went.** The empty collection, where the drawing was, and Settings → Account signed out, the one sign-in page without an emblem. The Guilds sign-in and join pages keep the emblem: they never had the drawing, and the issue's "Sign-in and join" says both show the emblem, with slices 7 and 8 adding the samples. Other states keep the mark they had: an emblem or a symbol says more than a bird would on "No Guilds yet" or "No field guide yet".
- **The mascot isn't a found bird.** The guide's "a patch means someone found this bird" is about a place's birds. The finch is the app's own character and names no sighting, so it may sit on an empty collection, as the issue's story asks. It's a picture at the head of the Account page too, though Settings is otherwise plain iOS, because the issue asks for it on sign-in and that page has no other mark.
- **The old drawing is gone.** This slice kept the line drawing for the Listen hero's fallback, a set without a cover. Slice 6's bundled covers gave every set a painting and took that use away, which left the drawing with nothing outside its own file, so the epic's clean-up deleted it.

## UX cohesion slice 4: speed (issue #150) — built 2026-11-05, the numbers on a phone pending

Before and after on a simulator with 1,000, 5,000 and 20,000 sightings are in `docs/screenshots/150/speed/`, with what's left for a phone. `-seedLarge <n>` and `-speedTour YES` (DEBUG) take them, on a phone too.

- **One store owns the collection.** `CollectionStore`, in the environment, holds the `CardCollection`, and screens read it. It builds again only when the sightings or the sets differ from what it was built from (`CardCollectionCache`, whose tests hold that it answers as a fresh collection does). `PackLibrary.collection(of:)` is gone, so a view can't go back to building its own. Screens that read only the collection lose their `@Query` of the whole album too, Listen among them, so a launch fetches the album once, for the store.
- **It listens for saves, not `@Query`.** `ModelContext.didSave` for its own album reaches it before `save()` returns on the main actor, so a screen drawn after an add already reads the new patch, without a store updated from a view body or a frame with the two disagreeing. Every write in the app saves explicitly, so nothing it reads goes unnoticed. A save that changes nothing it reads (a note, a caption, rarity) reads the album and compares, and no screen draws again. The sets it follows by observation.
- **Two builds stay outside it**: `SightingRecorder.record` and `RunSightings`, which aren't screens, build from the sightings of one species at an add, and have to answer about the sighting just written.
- **A patch's page takes its sightings from one table of the album** rather than filtering the album once per patch. Not in the audit: profiling found it, at 8 s of CPU opening a patch over 5,000 sightings.
- **Lazy rows.** The Atlas's tiles and birds and a set's rows are `LazyVStack`s inside the page's plain `VStack`, which keeps the layout and `-autoScrollTo` as they were. `testASetsPageMakesItsRowsAsTheyScrollIn` counts what was made at open.
- **The engine warms when Listen appears**: 300 ms after the page appears (past its first frame), at utility priority, in `WarmLoader`, the one load every run shares. Start during the warm-up waits for the same load and raises its priority. It doesn't warm during a run, which holds its own engine. A memory warning drops the loader's reference: a run under way keeps its own and the memory goes when it ends, and nothing reloads until Listen appears again or Start is tapped. A load under way carries on, since forgetting it would start a second beside it, and the 8 MB geomodel stays, since the rarity reader uses it as well. The root sets `warmEngine` in the environment, so screens hosted in tests don't load the models.
- **The Guild cache holds more than the issue names.** The issue says the list and each feed's first page; it also holds each Guild's detail and goals, since a Guild's page can't draw without its detail and a card's faces and goal come from the two. It's one small JSON file in Caches (the server's own shape through the same decoders), so it's out of backups and iOS may purge it. Each read marks what's fresh, so what came from the cache is still read once per launch, in place.
- **It goes with the account.** The root's `clear()` runs on any sign-out, including a revoked token and account deletion, and removes the file before returning. An answer on its way at that moment is dropped rather than stored, and a cache found at launch while signed out goes unread. Blocking takes a friend's finds out of the feeds and the file at once, not after the reload, which could fail, and a block made on another device does the same when the list of blocks is read.
- **Compare keeps the last read in memory only**, by friend, in `CircleStore.friendBirds`, and a block, here or read from another device, or the server answering not found, drops it, so a blocked friend's birds don't stay on screen. Slice 8's friends' species can build on it.
- **The placeholder** is `GuildPlaceholder`: rows of redacted text with a soft disc, on the list's raised surface. VoiceOver hears "Loading" once per placeholder. It replaces the centred spinner on the Guilds list, a Guild's page, its feed and goals, Compare and the join screen; spinners inside a button (More finds, Join) and "Making an invitation…", which waits on an action rather than content, stay.
- **`-fakeCircles offline` now fails every read**, not only the list, as no connection does. `-fakeCircleLatency <seconds>` slows the fake's reads and `-circleCacheFile <name>` gives it a cache in tmp; the fakes have none without it.

## UX cohesion: foundations (#150, slice 2, 2026-11-05)

The rule, the words and the shared components the other slices build on. The rule's text and bans are in the design guide 2.0's README, and the words in `CONTEXT.md`, "Words a birder reads".

- **One heading.** `SectionTitle("157 still out there")` is New York at title 3 in the ink, with no rule, from the mock's `.h` (14.5 px at 1.34 is 19.4 pt). `size: .lead` is the larger first line of a page, title 2 ("Maya has these. Go find them."). `top:` is the space above, and `onBoard:` gives it `boardLabel`'s solid surface under Increase Contrast. `AtlasHeading` and the ad hoc headings are gone. A plain-iOS list keeps system section headers. A goal's "Fourteen birds, together." and a page's own title aren't section headings and stay as they are.
- **One avatar.** `MemberFace(member:size:)` or `MemberFace(id:initials:size:)` is a disc in the seeded tone with dark initials, and `MemberFaces` overlaps them. `MemberRow`'s moss disc is gone. The initials scale with the disc rather than with Dynamic Type, and VoiceOver skips the disc, so a screen says who it is in words.
- **One state.** `StateNotice` replaces every `ContentUnavailableView`, with an optional message, an emblem mark for a Guild state, and `.statePage()` for a whole page.
- **Short facts.** `ShortFacts(a, b)` is two short facts on one line at a small size with a middle dot, and VoiceOver reads a comma. Elsewhere words join with a comma, as the mocks write them ("Seen in Pasadena, 3 days ago", "Citrus edition, 160 birds"), or a sentence. Three more keep their dot on screen with different words for VoiceOver: a tile's "No. 003 · seen 8×", the stitch-in's "Thread 5 of 10 · Ochre", and the paper card's "Uncharted · outside every set". `ScreenAccessibilityTests.expectClean` now fails on a dot in anything VoiceOver reads on the walked screens.
- **No dots in what's shared (the maintainer's decision, 2026-11-06).** The share image, the share text and the share sheet's title join with commas or take a second line: "Citrus edition, Pasadena, Nov 3, 2026", "AI illustration, Featherguild", and the text's bird, then "Sep 21, 2026, Los Angeles, CA" on its own line. The lens keeps its dots: its lines are hints on a small display, and its design doesn't change.
- **Capitals** stay on the wordmark and the paper card's edition line. The reveal, the patch page and the goal card are in sentence case, so `CardFont.edition` and `editionTracking` are gone.
- **Words on the lens (the maintainer's decision, 2026-11-06).** The lens's design doesn't change, but "card", "pack" and "region" leave its copy with the rest. The maintainer chose these words: "Birds appear as they call.", "No recordings on your phone", "No photo on your phone", "Not in your field guide", and "Species are not filtered by place."
- **The downloads page is titled "Downloads".** "Field-guide downloads" truncated as a large title from xxxLarge on, which #119 treats as a bug. The links to it keep the full words, since they wrap.
- **Set pages.** "157 still out there" heads the rows once, or "All 160 sewn" when none are left, and silhouette rows drop their line. VoiceOver reads the state on each row, since it can't see the silhouette. A sewn row says "Sewn". Slice 9 redraws the rows.
- **The tab icon** is a custom symbol, `stitched.ring`: twelve running stitches round a solid centre, drawn as filled paths from the mock's `#i-coll`. `scripts/make-stitched-ring.py` writes the SF Symbols template with all 27 masters, nine weights at three scales. With only Regular-M, `.imageScale(.small)` and `.large` drew nothing and the ring ignored Bold Text. The other three tabs keep their system symbols, whose fill the tab bar applies.
- **The light page** is `#EEF1EC`, the issue's starting value, chosen against linen and card stock on the screenshots. Under Increase Contrast it's `#F3F5F1`. The raised surface is re-derived as `#E0E8E2` (`#D5E0D9` under Increase Contrast): `#DEE7E1` looked right too, but left the alert text at 5.46:1 against the 5.5 the token promises. The Increase Contrast rule darkened to `#7C908B`, from 2.998:1.
- **Paper on linen.** In the light appearance the fibre's multiply drops from 50% to 15% and a 1 pt edge in `cardEdge` is added, so the stock measures lighter than the cloth (it was #E1D9C3 on #E8DBCD). On felt it's unchanged. The Increase Contrast linen had been lighter than the stock at any easing, so its veil drops from 72% to 55%: words on the board already sit on a solid surface under that setting, so nothing reads worse. `CardStockSurface` reads the page's appearance from outside the light scheme it sets for its type.
- **Thread comes in slice 6.** `ThreadProgress` will replace the three bars left here: the tinted `ProgressView`s (`SetProgressRow`, `LocalSetSection`, `DownloadProgress`), `SpeciesCollected`'s capsule, and `GoalBar`.
- **Not here.** The site's privacy page and the server's pushes still say "cards", "packs" and a dotted "New patch · Pasadena". Each needs a deploy, so they wait for the maintainer's go-ahead. The Review button on a run's rows belongs to slice 5, and the set's menu in place of the toolbar's Set button to slice 3.

## UX cohesion: after the run (#150, slice 10, 2026-11-06) — the on-phone checks pending

The run's ending, the Lock Screen and the Home Screen (audit L5, L6).

- **The recap is the sheet's first thing, and the screen is the image.** `RunRecapCard` is printed on `CardStockSurface` without its slot (a page has none; the slot says a patch's backer), with the place as the title in New York and the date beside it, or under it when they don't fit, as the plate's caption has it. The system share gets the same card on linen, at a fixed type size and in the light appearance, as a fresh PNG through `CardExport`. So what the birder reads is what goes.
- **The place is a rule, `SharePolicy.recapPlace`.** The city, through `FindPolicy.cityLabel`. A City switch leaves it off, as on a patch's share sheet. A threatened or unrated bird among the patches pictured makes it "a secret spot", on screen too, because a patch beside a city says where that bird is. Birds heard but not added are only a count and don't move it. The issue says only "the rules for place"; this is what those rules come to for a page with several birds on it.
- **Patches are on it, flat.** The issue's list is five facts, and story 31 says "the patches I sewed", so the card pictures them as well as counting them, three across up to three, five across for a big morning. They're rendered once (`FlatPatch`, the share image's fixed light) so a printed page has no shader on it. The image carries "AI illustration" and the reference photos' credits, as a patch's share image does, with commas rather than dots, per the maintainer's decision in the foundations entry.
- **The time listened stops at Stop.** `ListeningCoordinator.endedAt`: the old line counted to whenever the sheet was drawn, and Stop on the Lock Screen can bring it up long afterwards.
- **"New patches waiting" is slice 5's `RunHunt`**, the same count as the run's summary line: birds heard whose add would sew a patch at the run's place, so an add takes one off and Undo puts it back. The sheet keeps that line, under "What you heard", in place of "Started 7:12, listened for…", which the recap says.
- **Files in both targets live in `Glance/`,** a synchronized folder both targets list, not a package module. A live activity intent has to be compiled into the app and the extension for the system to find it, and it needs no `AppIntentsPackage` this way. The extension links none of the packages, whose resources are hundreds of megabytes. Deriving the values from `ListeningCoordinator` and `CardCollection` stays in the app, under `Listen/RunActivity.swift` and `Journal/LatestPatchPublisher.swift`.
- **Neither surface waits for a view.** `RunActivityController` and `LatestPatchPublisher` follow by observation from the app's init, since a run goes on behind a locked phone where SwiftUI draws nothing.
- **A killed app can't end its activity,** so the next launch ends any it finds, and every update carries a stale date 15 minutes off, kept fresh by a 5-minute heartbeat. A stale activity says to open the app. Its Stop still works: it launches the app, which clears it.
- **The widget doesn't wait for the app to be in front.** Lighting a patch needs the GPU, which a backgrounded app doesn't get, so a patch sewn from the lens behind a locked phone goes up as its plain painting, cut to shape with no light or shadow, marked unlit, and is lit the next time the app is in front. Whether it's in front is asked after the decode, which can outlast the moment. The recap's image still waits for the front.
- **A picture that can't be drawn writes nothing,** so the words never name a bird the old picture isn't of, and the rule tries again. A painting that wouldn't decode goes as its blank, marked unpainted, and is tried again too.
- **A Live Activity refused is asked for again** at the run's next change, for a run begun with the app out of the front.
- **The widget's empty state has no ring or blank**, only Listen's mark and "Your first patch is a bird away.": the issue rules out patterns and blanks for birds not found.
- **WidgetKit refuses a big image and says so only in the system's log:** the app's 1056 px felt left a real widget on its placeholder for good. The limit follows the phone: 1,069,415 px of area on a 17 Pro's small widget, 375,161 on an SE's, the lowest iOS 27 runs on. So the extension's felt and linen are 400 px and the patch 451 px, which together stay under the lower one, and `picturesFitAWidget` holds it.
- **Time listened leaves out time paused** (glasses off, out of range, the touchpad, a session being re-opened). `ListeningClock` takes every change of the run's state, and the recap, its VoiceOver sentence, its image, the run's timer, the strip and the Live Activity all read it, so none of them disagrees. Time listened therefore isn't "from start to end", but a birder with their glasses in a pocket wasn't listening.
- **Any end of a run lands on the recap.** The coordinator follows its own state, so Back on the lens or a source giving out goes the way Stop does. A failure's reason is said over the card.
- **The review is presented from the root**, not from the Listen tab's view, which could only present while its tab was showing: on another tab SwiftUI gave up on the sheet, and the birds heard went with it. This is what Stop on the Lock Screen or the Island exposed. It waits for an open candidate sheet, since only one can be up.
- **A bird added from a recap has the run's end as its date,** on the sighting and in the find, at the run's place: from a recap days old it was still met on that run. Each add there is a millisecond after the last, so a board and the widget keep them in the order sewn. Past a day, its row says the date heard rather than "72 h 1 min ago".
- **A recap waits for any sheet already up,** asking UIKit, since no one place knows them all: over New Guild it would throw away what's typed. It follows when that sheet closes.
- **A review waiting to be seen outlives the app.** Stop on the Lock Screen leaves a suspended app holding a large model, which iOS may kill long before the birder is back. On each end, add and Undo during the review, the run is written to the defaults (`EndedRun`: its birds, place, times and the adds' identifiers), and the next launch opens on the recap, with adds and Undo still working. Done forgets it. It's about a kilobyte. A run killed while still listening has nothing to review and isn't protected: birds heard and not added go with it, and sightings added were saved at the add, as ever. Debug albums in memory skip the archive, and `-albumFile` has its own.
- **The widget shows the most recently sewn patch, with that patch's set's progress,** so the two agree. An Uncharted patch, or none yet, shows the local set's (`LocalSet`). A repeat sighting rewrites nothing.
- **A link waits for an open run review.** Its skipped birds are forgotten when it closes, so a widget tap doesn't close it: the patch opens after Done. A patch unpicked since the widget drew opens the Collection tab alone.
- **Release needs its own entitlements file.** Release signed with none until now, and `BirdJournal.entitlements` holds Sign in with Apple, pushes and the invite domain, which stay Debug-only until #71. `BirdJournal-Release.entitlements` holds only the App Group. Without the group, `WidgetStore.shared` is nil and the widget stays on its empty state; nothing else notices.
- **Slice 6 in the extension.** `GlanceInk.tint(setID:)` is `PatchEdition.tint`'s colours, which the extension's catalog doesn't have, and a test holds them equal; under Increase Contrast it's the ink. `GlanceProgress` draws the same `Stitches` as `ThreadProgress`, at rest, since a widget has one frame. The Live Activity's tint reads the run's own fix through `CardSet.containing`, not `CurrentPlace`: it's where the run is, not where Listen last looked.
- **Text on the Lock Screen and the widget stops growing at xxxLarge.** Both are fixed-size surfaces, and the words are in the app at any size.
- **`-autoScreen glance` draws the surfaces' own views in the app**, for looking at every family and appearance side by side. `GlanceCaptureUITests` drives the real ones on a simulator when given a folder: the Island, the Lock Screen and Stop on it, and a widget added through the gallery and tapped. Neither replaces the on-phone check.
## UX cohesion: the place (#150, slice 6, 2026-11-06)

The painting that follows you, the edition's accent, and the one progress bar. Evidence is in `docs/screenshots/150/place/`.

- **One object knows the place.** `CurrentPlace`, in the environment, holds the set shown and its decoded cover, and the painting, "Your local set" and the root's tint all read it, so they can't disagree. `LocalSet.choose` is the pure order: the set containing the coordinate (`CardSet.containing`, the smallest part, as a card is placed), then the newest sighting's set, then the bundled set. An Uncharted newest sighting has no set, so it falls to the next sighting that has one rather than to the bundled set: that's still "your last set".
- **One read, which never prompts.** `LocationProvider.lastKnownCoordinate()` is `CLLocationManager.location`, behind a check that when-in-use is already granted. It starts no updates, so there's no blue pill. On the simulator the read does show iOS's location arrow in the status bar, gone by 30 s; it needs checking on a phone. The permission prompt's words now say what this read is for. The site's privacy page doesn't mention it yet, which is the maintainer's to change. It runs when Listen appears and when the app comes back to the front on Listen, which a birder also sees as Listen appearing, and again if the phone learns a set or the newest sighting's set changes, since either can change the answer. The coordinate goes no further than the choice; `UserDefaults` holds the set's ID.
- **Shown at once.** `CurrentPlace.init` decodes the remembered cover synchronously, so the first frame has it. Later covers decode off the main actor first, and the set, painting and tint change together. Reduce Motion switches without the 0.6 s fade.
- **The five set definitions ship with the covers**, 89 KB, so a brand-new phone that has never been online knows which set it's in. `PackLibrary.sets` adds them last, for ids no pack or index describes. It also means a sighting on such a phone is placed in its set at once, rather than Uncharted until the first index read, and placement is computed, so nothing has to migrate. The set's Atlas tile and page work with no Download to offer, under the offline notice.
- **The covers are JPEG at the pack cover's full 1536 × 864, quality 90, 2.4 MB together**, not AVIF: an AV1 decode holds its thread, and this one runs at launch. Listen's hero is 362 pt tall, so it shows 1086 px of height at 3x, and a narrower "phone-width" file would look softer than the pack's cover does today. `packbuilder bundle-covers` writes them into the `Pack` target from the approved locks, they're committed, and `test_covers` fails if one isn't the cover its lock approved.
- **The drawn landscape is gone for sets.** `EditionFallback` and `Landscape(edition:)` went; `LandscapeArt` stays for `GuildCover` until slice 7. A set with no cover at all, which none is today, shows the raised surface in the Atlas and a fixed petrol under Listen's white words.
- **The accent is `.tint`, not a token.** Everything that drew `Color.moss` reads the `tint` shape style, so `RootView.tint(...)` reaches it, and a set's page, a pack's page and a patch's page override it. `Color.moss` remains as the aqua of a set the app doesn't know. A pack's page takes its set's edition as well, since it's the same set's page. The maps stay aqua, markers, circles and small pins alike, since slice 9 redraws them. New code should use `.tint`, or `AnyShapeStyle(TintShapeStyle.tint)` where a ternary needs one type.
- **The issue's tints were starting values, and measured on rendered pixels they moved.** On linen's worst pixel (`#C4B5A7`) the light values measured 3.1 to 3.7:1, so they're darker: Citrus `#454B18`, Coastal `#184C6B`, Fog `#3D494F`, Desert `#703622`, Wetland `#0B5048`, Uncharted `#594428`. On felt's lightest pixel (`#374F55`) Desert and Uncharted's dark values measured 4.28 and 4.35:1, so they're `#E9B099` and `#D1BA95`; the other dark values are the issue's. `EditionTintTests`, in `ContrastTests.swift`, holds them in all four variants.
- **Lavender thread has its own colorset**, `ThreadTogether`: `#B9BEDD` in dark as now, and `#41446A` in light, because the light `sharedGoal` is a pale fill that wouldn't show on the page.
- **`ThreadProgress`.** 8 on, 3 off, 3 tall in the mock, so 10.7, 4 and 4 pt, about 24 stitches across a phone. Stitches are whole, and stretch a little to end flush with the margin. The last stitch waits for the whole, so 159 of 160 doesn't look finished. Counted birds get a stitch each for the first two, which is what the mocks draw for "3 of 160", so a new birder sees the first birds sew; past those it's the share, rounded. Falling (an Undo) doesn't animate. Birds are added during a run, when Listen's section isn't there, and on other tabs, so a thread keeps what each set or goal last showed on screen this launch and sews the difference when it next comes up, past a 350 ms settle: a rise sews once, where the birder next sees it. A row reading the same count beside it hides the thread from VoiceOver at the call; on Listen the thread reads the sentence and the count beside it is hidden.
- **Listen's section follows the mock**: the set's name as the heading, "3 of 160 sewn", the thread and three patches with no words, on the page rather than a felt panel. Two things differ from the mock on purpose. The name needs a way to the set's page, since "Explore the set" went with the heading, so it's a link with a small chevron. And the words "Your local set" are no longer on screen, as the mock draws it; VoiceOver still says them on the link. At accessibility sizes it's two patches, larger.
- **Not here.** Haptics on a stitch are slice 3's. The Guild goal line under the patches is slice 8's. Set tiles aren't plates until slice 9.

### The launch trap (found in review of this slice's runs, 2026-11-06)

- **What happened.** About one launch in six trapped (`_dispatch_assert_queue_fail`, from `swift_task_isCurrentExecutor`, in `closure #1 in BirdJournalApp.body`, on `com.apple.SwiftUI.AsyncRenderer`), 2 to 9 ms after the splash ended.
- **The cause, two halves.** The `WindowGroup`'s closure read `showingSplash`, so the splash's end made it stale. SwiftUI can draw an animation's frame on its async render thread, and a frame arriving before the main thread's update ran the closure there, and Swift 6's isolation check trapped. The base build had that first half but nothing running on that thread at the moment. This slice gave it something: `ThreadProgress`'s animated path (12 of 12 launches trapped with a Guild's goal sewing as the splash ended, 0 of 12 with the sew's animation off, and 0 of 12 at 123e5da under the same flags), and the painting's cross-fade at launch (1 of 14).
- **Not the cause.** Load changes the rate, not the fact; the count stayed 5 of 30 against 0 of 30 at similar load. Writing the remembered set on every read wasn't it either, though it now writes only on a change. The coordinate read, the cover decode and the root tint aren't on the trapped path.
- **The fix.** What changes (`showingSplash`, the appearance's `@AppStorage`) moved from the `App` into `AppSurface`, a view, whose body SwiftUI keeps on the main thread. Nothing the closure reads changes after launch, so any animation, including the next slice's, can run through the splash's end. `LaunchUITests` launches four times with a goal sewing across the end: red before, green after.



## UX cohesion: feel (#150, slice 3, 2026-11-06)

How the app answers. Lift, press, haptics, the patch's pop-in, the set's menu, modes, the splash and the reveal.

- **One list of haptics.** `Haptic` (`Design/Feel.swift`) names `lift` (soft), `turn` (light), `stitchTick` (selection) and `stitchDone` (success), `startStop` (medium), `reaction` (light) and `error`; `.haptic(_:trigger:when:)` is the only way to play one, and it goes through `sensoryFeedback`, which follows the system's setting. A tick is the system's selection tick, chosen from its description and not yet felt on a phone; the light impact it replaces is the other candidate.
- **Haptics and the microphone.** The issue has them play during a phone-mic run unless a stitch's ticks raise false detections, and `AVAudioSession` silences haptics while recording by default, so `PhoneMicAudioSource` sets `setAllowHapticsAndSystemSoundsDuringRecording`. That's untested on a phone, which is the first check. If ticks do raise false detections, `Haptic.ticksDuringPhoneRun` goes false: only ticks stop, gated on `phoneRunHoldsSession`, and Start, Stop, the success and a reaction still play. Removing the session's line would have silenced all of them.
- **System sounds come with it.** The session has one switch for both, so allowing haptics allows system sounds during a phone-mic run. The app plays none itself, but keyboard clicks, for a birder with them on who types during a run (a search, a note), may play through the speaker, as the session routes to it, and reach the microphone. Kept on, since the issue wants the haptics and the alternative is none of them. Not measured; it's on the phone check.
- **A thread's haptics.** `ThreadProgress` ticks a stitch and succeeds on the last for what it counts in birds, a set's or a goal's. A download's doesn't: nobody did anything. Its ticks go through the same switch as a stitch-in's.
- **Where each is played.** Start and Stop on the root, following the run (`isRunGoing`), so the Listen tab's Stop, the listening strip's and a run the glasses end say the same; a run that failed plays only the error. Lift when a patch's page first opens, not on touch: touching a tile also starts a scroll, and a buzz for every scroll that starts on a patch would mean nothing. The press answers the touch. A run's failure and a failed add or undo on the root; a failed reaction, goal, report, unblock or new Guild through `InlineError(_, afterAction: true)`. Errors reading a page aren't felt: nobody just asked for them. A Guild's page and the join screen show their load and action failures through one `error`, so they don't opt in yet. A repeat add's count rising is one tick and no success: the success is for a new patch, as the audit has it.
- **The press.** `.pressResponse(isPressed:scale:)` is what the styles apply: `journalPrimary` and `journalOutlined`, and `.journalPress` (no look of its own, sinks) and `.patchLift` (a patch, rises 4%). Under Reduce Motion it dims and doesn't move. Beyond the styles, the listening strip's Stop and the audio source row take `.journalPress`; other `.plain` rows keep the system's dim, and the issue names only the shared styles.
- **Stop** is the mock's compact centred pill in `journalOutlined`, with full width only at accessibility sizes, where its words need the room. There its whole shape grows with its words, and a toast stops growing at the first accessibility size: it took most of the page at the largest, broke "House" across two lines and squeezed Stop to one truncated line.
- **Lift.** The namespace is per navigation stack (`.patchStack(push:)`, on the tabs' stacks and the sheets' that can reach a tile), since a tile for the same patch can be on two tabs, and the tile's id carries its surface (`PatchLiftID`, through `Route.patch(_, from:)`), since a species' page can sit on top of a board with the same patch. The source is the patch's picture, not its words. The route fixes the id, so a swipe to another patch still goes back to the tile opened. Under the system's Reduce Motion the zoom is itself a cross-fade with nothing moving, seen frame by frame, so there's no branch. `.navigationTransition(.crossFade)`, new in this SDK and documented for sheets, draws a slide on a push. So `-reduceMotion` doesn't reach the Lift; `testUnderReduceMotionAPatchPageCrossFadesWithNothingMoving` sets the simulator's own setting. In a sheet's stack it's a plain push on purpose: a zoom there cut to the page, with the page behind the sheet showing for about three frames. A patch opened any other way (a debug route, slice 10's widget) has no tile and is a plain push.
- **The peek.** Turn over opens the patch's page on its paper card (`Route.patch(_, turned: true)`), and All sightings its sightings, through an environment action (`pushRoute`), rather than a `NavigationLink` inside a menu item. "All sightings" reads "1 sighting" or "All 3 sightings" as the paper card's footer does (`PatchText.allSightings`). Share shares the newest sighting, as the patch's page does, fetched when asked for so a board of tiles doesn't each hold the album.
- **Pop-in.** Cached at first draw, a patch simply shows, and a page's patch shows the tile's picture until its own arrives, so it doesn't turn blank growing out of its tile. A wait shows the blank, which fades into the lit patch in 0.18 s. A board decodes ahead through `PatchTextures.decodeAhead`, one low-priority, utility-QoS operation at a time on the existing two-at-a-time queue, so a patch on screen always has a slot, and an operation that finds its patch already decoded does nothing. It stops with the board's task. The first 48 only: at about 650 KB each, a full 160 would be 100 MB, and the rest decode as they scroll in, as they do today.
- **The set's menu.** The name heads the Patches mode and is the Menu when there's more than one set; with one, it's just the name, and the total shows nowhere, since the count under it says the same patches and Sightings has the rest. Under it the thread and "3 of 160 sewn, Citrus edition", still opening the set's page. The total ("5 patches, 6 sightings") is the menu's first line, above the sets: as a section title it doesn't show over a picker. The menu is in the bar with the modes, so another set is in reach from anywhere on a board; the thread and count scroll with the board. There, the name stops growing at the first accessibility size. The bar takes the touches on its empty parts. Slice 6's `ThreadProgress` sits under the name, with `.id(setID)`, so another set's thread doesn't sew from this one's count.
- **The picker stays still.** It's in a `safeAreaBar` above the scroll view, under a title that doesn't collapse (`.inlineLarge`), with the set's menu. Pinned inside the scrolling content, it rode that content: from a scrolled board to Sightings it fell to about y=675 and climbed back over 0.3 s, as the new mode's height collapsed inside the fade, and it rested 52 pt apart between scrolled and not. So the bar stays one height, and the title keeps its row, swapping from large to small within it as a board scrolls. Search is a magnifier in that row, and opening it swaps the row for a field and a close button. The navigation bar steps aside for it, and the field sits in the bar with the picker, 54 pt tall like the row it stands in for, since a toolbar item wouldn't hold one that wide. It's our own: as a drawer the system's hid on scroll and took the picker with it by 52 pt, always showing it cost 52 pt of patches, and `.searchable(placement: .toolbar)` with `.searchToolbarBehavior(.minimize)` looked right but had a close button that did nothing with the keyboard up, with or without `isPresented:`, and left the words filtering the board with it down. Without `.minimize` its close button works from every state, but that brings back the always-showing field, and the picker moves about 52 pt on open and close. Close ends the search from any state: the field, the keyboard and the words. The field takes the keyboard and VoiceOver's focus once, on opening, not each time it comes back on screen over the results; closing puts VoiceOver's focus back on the magnifier. Closing holds the room under the board for 600 ms, while the keyboard goes down: at the foot, that pulled the offset back just after the restore had saved it, and every later close landed in the wrong place. Each search's results get a new grid, since the old one's range came from the old offset and left them blank at the largest text. Closing goes back to about where the board was: at the largest text, far down a board, up to 92 pt off, since a one-column board's rows are estimated until they're seen, and the tile stays in view. A search starts at the top of its results, since at the old offset a short list's patches sat above the bar until scrolled, and clearing it goes back to where each mode was. At the largest text the field stays in its row, where as a drawer it took 124 pt. `testThePickerStaysStillThroughAModeChange` samples it through the change.
- **Modes.** The surface cross-fades (0.25 s, 0.12 s under Reduce Motion), as the issue says, in one scroll view. What's on it doesn't cross-fade with it, because that scroll view moves to the new mode's place about 60 ms in, and the old mode would be seen jumping as it faded: the old mode is gone in 0.05 s, and the new one comes up from 0.08 s. One scroll view per mode would have kept each one's offset for free, but would have put a second picker on screen and handed the navigation bar a new scroll view at each change. A quick run of changes cancels the restore under way, so a mode's saved place isn't overwritten part way.
- **The foot of a board.** A real bug, not load: opening a patch and coming back left the foot of a board 49 pt lower every time (537 to 586, the tab bar's height). The page hides the tab bar, the scroll view underneath gets taller, and the offset was clamped. Correcting it afterwards still let the zoom land low, so now it isn't clamped: there's room below the content from the moment the page opens until the bar is back. A finger on the board takes the room away at once, so a flick can't reach empty felt and then be pulled back. `keepsItsFootUnderAPage` is the one place, on the board and on Listen's page, whose foot did the same at the largest text. `testTheFootOfABoardComesBackWhereItWas` failed before and holds it.
- **The splash** is decided in `AppSurface`, not the `App`, whose closure Swift 6 traps off the main actor. It plays until `launchSplashShown` is set, which is at its start, so a launch killed part way has still shown it. An upgrade shows it once more. The launch screen stays. Slice 11's art goes in `LaunchSplash.art`.
- **One finished page.** The reveal's finished page is one view however it's reached, so going straight there and being marked as played doesn't make a second page with a second success.
- **The reveal.** Primary, Share, Undo while its window is open, and the patch as a button to the collection, with a hint. With more ceremonies waiting, the patch is only a patch and "Skip the rest" stays, since going to the collection waits for the last. It opens the collection on the patch's own board (`openCollection(_:)`), since a ceremony from the glasses can be about a set other than the newest sighting's. The patch is 300 pt wide on both pages, up from 280, in the room the note left.
- **"Shared with".** The issue's three conditions are `FindPolicy.sharedLine`, and the page also asks `FindOutbox.isPublished`, since a patch from the glasses can be celebrated days after its add, under settings that have changed since. One or two Guilds by name, more by count: "Shared with your 3 Guilds". If the Guilds haven't been read this launch, the page reads them.

## UX cohesion slice 5: patch means yours, and the hunt (issue #150, V2, L1, L2, A2) — built 2026-11-06, the maintainer's phone checks pending

A run reads as a hunt, and a patch on any row now means you found this bird. Evidence in `docs/screenshots/150/hunt/`, with its decode counts.

- **Three looks, from the patch an add would join** (`CandidateLook`). No patch where an add would go is new here: the reference photo in a dashed `TransferPen` ring, "new here" in the tint, Sew it. A patch there shows it with its count, and Add. Added on this run shows the patch and a check. A bird added on this run reads as new again if the run has walked out of that patch's set, or its sighting has been deleted, because that's what an add would then do.
- **`TransferPen` is a colorset**, `#9DB4EE` dark and `#3F5FA8` light as the issue has it, `#BCCEF8` and `#2C478A` under Increase Contrast, and all four clear the tint table's 4.5:1 on the page and the raised surface (`TokenPairingTests`), though today it only marks a ring. Xcode generates `Color.transferPen`. `PatchMaterial.transferPen`, the shader's fixed pen on the cloth, stays: it's drawn on twill, not on the page.
- **The tint is read, never named.** "new here", "Calling now" and Sew it's fill use `.tint`, so they take the edition when slice 6 sets it at the root. Sew it's words use `mossText`; slice 6's tints need that pairing measured.
- **Add and Stop are in the ink, not the accent**, as the mock draws them: the birds are the loud thing. `OutlinedButtonStyle` gained a `colour`, and `.journalQuiet` is that in the ink. Stop hugs its words, centred, in the same `safeAreaBar`, so "Possible matches" sits above it, where the mock has it below. The issue's bar wins over the mock's order.
- **Calling now is unchanged where the mock differs:** the raised row, the level glyph and the words stay, and they win over "Added" on the line. They now say what else is true of the bird ("Calling now, new here"), as the mock does. The ring round the thumbnail follows the patch's own outline, in the tint. A new bird that's calling has only its dashed ring: the issue gives that look the one ring, and two wouldn't read.
- **A repeat's acknowledgment names the bird** ("House Finch seen again, 3 times in Los Angeles"), from a row and from the review alike. It has no reveal, and on a real list a thumb can land on another bird's Add: over the clip the order changed in 11 of 13 five-second samples.
- **The row's type is `rowTitle`**, as Species rows have it, and what's tinted on the line below is semibold, as the mock's is.
- **The row's words drop "Heard"**, as the mock does ("3 min ago, seen 7 times"). VoiceOver keeps it: "House Finch, heard 3 min ago, you have this patch, seen 7 times", and "Acorn Woodpecker, calling now, new patch waiting". The row is one button opening the review, and its Sew it or Add is another.
- **The summary counts by the patch, not by the pack.** A bird no pack describes still counts as waiting, since its review can sew it. With nothing waiting it says "3 birds you already have", and with nothing heard the page leaves the line out for its own "Listening for birds…". The Listening strip on other tabs says only the first thing, for room, and VoiceOver reads it all. "N added" is gone: the rows show it.
- **The place line moved up under the source**, as the mock has it, so the summary leads straight into the rows.
- **Sewn from a row, a bird is Heard** (`addFromRow`, which calls the review's `add`). A bird no pack describes goes to its review and nothing is written, as a second guard behind the row offering only Review. A row's add is held while it's under way (a glasses run takes its photo first), as the sheet's is.
- **Rows' pictures** (`RowPictureSource`, `RowPicture`): your patch at the row's 67 pt, or the 84 pt a Sightings row already gave its photo, growing with the text to 120 pt, with no paper tag, since the name is in type beside it. Species rows take the newest sighting's patch. Sightings rows show your original photo first, attached then glasses (`CardText.originalPhotoPath`), then the patch. A patch needs no caption, and "Reference photo" stays for the moment before the collection has the sighting, so a reference photo never looks like your own. Set rows show the patch in that set's edition. Atlas rows show your patch in that pack's set, else the reference photo: found by scientific name (`CardCollection.patch(scientificName:setID:)`), because a pack's own `birdnetLabel` is that bare name and a sighting's label isn't. An attached photo isn't cropped, as `AttachedPhoto` doesn't crop it; a glasses frame takes the album's crop. The pack page's strip of photos stays: it's for identifying. The review stays a reference photo.
- **Patches on rows stay plain bitmaps.** `PatchView` already draws one; the waveform moved to its own view, so its dozen updates a second no longer rebuild the rows, which now redraw once a second. Species rows and a patch's sightings became lazy, since each row would ask for a decode. The decoding queue reads and fills the cache itself, since a page of one bird's sightings asks for one patch per row: at most the two decoding at once can miss each other. `-patchDecodeLog YES` logs each decode; the counts are in the evidence.
- **The light stops with the scene** (`holdsPatchLight`), for every lit surface. No surface was on screen with the phone locked until the run's rows were, and "You can lock your phone and look up" would have run the motion updates for a whole walk.
- **The profile** leads with your patch on felt or linen, in your newest sighting's edition, every other beside it, each opening its page; then the name, the sightings link, and the story on card stock, then the photos. It's in one place only: the story section further down is left out. With a `match` from a review it's as before, with the photo first. A bird with no sighting is as before too.
- **Rows keep their identity across a re-sort**, so they slide (#42) and an add under way stays under way. Nothing on a row may put its position in an `.id`; `aResortKeepsEachRow` fails if one does.
- **`RowPillStyle` is in `Theme.swift`** beside the other shared styles, as `.journalRowPill(isFilled:)`, so slice 3 can give it the press response in one line.
- **`-seedBoard` and `-seedLarge` seed the label an add writes** (`DebugSeeds.sightingLabel`), not the pack's bare `birdnetLabel`, which left their patches apart from a run's rows.
- **`-fakeRunBirds`** holds a run's list still, for UI tests and screenshots. A real run re-sorts as birds call, so a tap can land on a row that has just moved. That was harmless when a tap opened a sheet, and from a row it adds the wrong bird. Undo and the reveal's name make that cheap, which is what the issue accepted; it's a thing to watch on a phone.

## UX cohesion slice 8: Guild data, the backend half (issue #150) — built 2026-11-05, deployed 2026-11-06
Two additive read fields, so the Guild can show patches. Both read finds the server already holds: nothing new is stored, ADR-0004 stands, the write paths and the table are untouched, and the stack needs no change. Old clients ignore the fields (the app's models name what they decode), and new clients tolerate their absence, so the backend went first: **deployed on 2026-11-06 from `7c0e0b7`, at the maintainer's go-ahead.** Fixes after it ship with the next deploy: an edition, an art key, a photo key and a device token are now matched whole (`fullmatch`), since `$` lets a trailing newline through. An edition or art key with one could be stored; a photo key's still had to name an uploaded photo; a token's was already trimmed, so that change only keeps the four alike.

- **Compare birds** (`GET /v1/friends/{sub}/compare`): each entry in `species` gains `editions`, so `{species_id, scientific_name, common_name, method, editions: ["us-ny-nyc", "us-ca-la"]}`. It holds the pack ids the friend found that species in, each once, earliest found first so the first is the patch to show ("the edition they first found it"), from the same finds that give the method: their finds whose circles include one the two share now. Someone blocked is still 404, for the blocker alone.
- **Goals** (`GET` and `POST /v1/circles/{id}/goals`): each goal gains `species`, `[{species_id, scientific_name, common_name, edition}]`, one per species, earliest found first. It comes from the read that computes progress (`FindStore.species_between`), so it is always `progress` long. It names no finder. `edition` is that of the species' earliest find in the period that names a set, or null when none does: a later find in a set shows a patch where an earlier one outside every set couldn't.
- **Outside every set is the absence of an edition** (my reading): `editions: []` in Compare, `edition: null` in a goal, as a feed item's `edition` is null. So a friend with a Los Angeles Phoebe and an Uncharted one reads `["us-ca-la"]`, even if the Uncharted one came first: a board shows one patch per species, and the set's says more. A species with none shows the blank with its tag, as the issue says.
- **A find stored before #136 has no `edition`**, and the server deployed until then stored none. `stored_edition` reads one from a patch's art key (`packs/us-ca-la/patch/…`) with the rule the app's `LegacyFindWire.edition(ofArtKey:)` uses, for the same reason: pack art can come from any pack with the bird. What isn't a pack id isn't read as one, since an art key is looser. Without it, every older find would come back with no edition. It stays until the table's old items are rewritten, like `legendary`'s read. The feed is unchanged: it still sends such a find's `edition` as null, and the app applies the same rule.
- **A goal shows a blocked member's species, with no owner attached: the maintainer's decision, 2026-11-06.** #71 kept their finds in a goal's progress because a circle's goal is the circle's, and #71 hides a blocked account's finds from the blocker, so a species only they found could have gone either way. The maintainer chose to show it, so the row always matches the count: `species` is always `progress` long, for a blocker too. It shows the bird, not who found it.
- **No art key in either** (my reading): the issue asks for the edition, and the phone draws the patch from its pack, or the edition's blank without one. Only a feed item carries the CDN thumbnail.
- **Tests**: backend `test_reactions.py` (the editions per species, earliest found first and once each; none outside every set; only finds in circles shared now, including a circle one has left; a block hiding them from the blocker alone and back on unblocking; a deleted find's edition gone; the old art keys) and `test_goals.py` (the species with an edition each, earliest first, deduplicated, inside the period; on the monthly goal and a new one; the circle's own finds only; a delete taking a species off and passing its edition on; a blocked member's species; the old art keys), through the in-process harness. The app half is the rest of this slice.

## UX cohesion slice 12: the domain (issue #150) — built 2026-11-06, the deploy and DNS pending the maintainer's run of the wizard

The maintainer chose **`featherguild.matthewcelia.com`**. `infra/scripts/domain-wizard.sh` does the move; nothing in AWS or DNS has changed yet. The mentions of the old host above are history and stay.

- **The old host stays on the distribution for good** (`inviteFormerDomains` in `cdk.json`), because old invite links, printed QR codes and installed apps name it. The CloudFront viewer function serves its `/.well-known/apple-app-site-association` directly, since Apple doesn't follow redirects for that file, and sends everything else to the same path and query on the new host with a 301. It decides by the `Host` header, before the cache, so no cache key changes. CloudFront's own name keeps serving the pages.
- **One certificate names both hosts**, requested outside the stack as before. `check-invite-certificate.sh` runs before any `deploy.sh --diff` or deploy and stops one that isn't issued or doesn't cover every host in `cdk.json`, because CloudFormation would otherwise learn that from CloudFront partway through. The wizard saves `INVITE_CERTIFICATE_ARN` only once the new one is issued, so the file never holds an ARN a deploy can't use, and finds a pending one again from ACM by its hosts and the `project=birdjournal` tag. A certificate without the tag is left alone. The old certificate stays in ACM, unused.
- **Records before the deploys, and two deploys.** The circles wizard deployed first, because the distribution didn't exist yet. It does now and keeps its address, so the new host's record goes in first. `check-invite-dns.sh` asks the zone's own name servers that both hosts point at the distribution before any deploy, the wizard's or a bare `deploy.sh`, because the zone's SOA minimum is a day and a redirect to a name that doesn't resolve can stay cached long after the record is added. It tries each name server in turn, and a timeout or SERVFAIL from all of them reads as a lookup failure, never as a missing record. Then the first deploy (`deploy.sh --new-host-first`, the `inviteNewHostFirst` context) adds the alias and the certificate while the old host keeps serving its pages and the API keeps its links there. The viewer function auto-publishes as soon as it updates, ahead of the distribution, so one deploy doing everything could redirect the old host to a new host CloudFront doesn't serve yet, for about 15 minutes. The second (`--move-to-new-host`) turns on the redirects and moves `invite_base_url`, once `https://<new host>/` answers 200 over TLS from this Mac.
- **A check must never block the fix for what it detects.** The stack's `InviteBaseUrl` says which side of the move it's on. Before the move, a plain deploy refuses, even if the new host answers, because finishing the move has to be a choice and not a side effect of adding pushes: it names `--move-to-new-host` and `--new-host-first`, which leaves things where they are. After the move, a plain deploy skips the 200 check, since a new host answering 500 is what it may be fixing, and `--new-host-first` is refused because it would turn off the redirects and move links back. **`--emergency` skips the certificate, DNS, live-backend and move checks**, with a loud warning saying so, and every refusal it can help with names it. It can't help with every one: a certificate that's unissued or misses a host stays a dead end for this checkout, because both templates name both hosts and CloudFront should refuse it (inferred, not tried). That refusal says how to ship an urgent fix instead: wizard stages 2 to 5 then `--new-host-first`, or a checkout with slice 8's backend but not this change. **Before the move, `--emergency` with no side named uses `--new-host-first`'s template and says so**, because the plain one would redirect the old host to a host that may not resolve, the lock-out this slice exists to prevent. If it can't read the stack, it asks for a side. Moving the site takes `--move-to-new-host` by name. The template tests and the bootstrap check still run, since the second guards the shared account. Tests over the fakes cover each refusal and what the flag then does.
- **The app accepts both hosts and makes new links on the new one.** A link in the app comes from the API (`CircleInvite.url`), so what the phone shows follows the backend. Associated domains name both, still in the one Debug-only entitlements file: Release has none until #88's switch.
- **Which old apps exist.** Release builds have never had `ACCOUNTS_ENABLED` or the entitlements, so a TestFlight build has no Guilds and claims no links. Its only use of the host is Settings' privacy and terms links, and those redirect. Only a Debug build from before this change knows the old host alone: a new-host link opens in the browser for it, on the invite page. That page explains Guilds but has no way to get the app: "Find out how to get it" goes to the home page, which says the app is on TestFlight without a link, and the repo holds no public TestFlight or App Store address. So the backend does not keep a second link host for those builds; they get reinstalled.
- **Deploy first, then release the app.** A build with the new host opens its Settings links there and reads the association file at install, so the host has to be live first. After the wizard, deploy only from branches holding this change: an older one takes the new host back off the distribution.
- **`deploy.sh` refuses a checkout without the live backend**, since slice 8's backend went live from its own branch before merging and a deploy without it would take its fields off the API. `check-live-backend.sh` parses the models with `ast`, so it needs no backend environment, a comment doesn't count and reformatting doesn't matter; it can go once #150's epic has merged to `dev`. `--without-live-backend` is the way past a false refusal or a deliberate removal. The wizard offers no override, and it asks before deploying uncommitted changes in `infra/` or `backend/`. `deploy.sh --diff` uses `cdk diff --method=template`, since the default makes a temporary change set with the deploy role and uploads the assets. A declined deploy ends the wizard saying so, not with "Setup complete", and the push, safety and accounts wizards say where to resume when `deploy.sh` refuses.
- **`circles-wizard.sh` is gone.** It would request a certificate for one host, and the domain wizard does everything it did.
- **Nothing else is host-specific.** No new app, no new Xcode Cloud workflow and no App ID change: the bundle ID stays, `BirdJournal/ci_scripts/ci_post_clone.sh` only fetches models and the pack, and the Associated Domains capability isn't per host. SES's production access names `https://lightsailvr.com`, read from the account. The `gh-pages` "has moved" notices link to the old host and the redirect carries them, so they stay.
- **Words on the site and in pushes** (the maintainer's addition): words only, with no change to what the policy says or promises. "Card" becomes "patch" and "pack" becomes "field-guide download", or "set" where it meant a place's collection ("the set's patch of the species"). A caption belongs to a sighting in `CONTEXT.md`, so "A card's one-line caption" is "A sighting's". "Your journal" stays: the issue's word table doesn't retire it.
- **Effective dates stay: the maintainer's decision** (privacy November 5, terms November 2). The privacy page's own line says "We will post any changes here and update the effective date", which read strictly covers these word changes too. Word-only changes have never moved the dates (#115, #118), and the maintainer chose to keep them for these as well.
- **Pushes join with commas.** "New patch · Pasadena" is "New patch, Pasadena", as dots became commas in the app. Reactions have one exception, since a comma after "Jealous!" reads wrongly: "Lovely find, Jealous! Let's go together".

## UX cohesion slice 9, the app half: the Atlas as a book (issue #150) — built 2026-11-06

A1, A3, A5, A6 and F12, against `output/ux-audit/reference/4-set-page.png`. Evidence in `docs/screenshots/150/atlas/`.

- **A plate is the mock's paper in both appearances**: `CardStockSurface(.plate)`, without the backer's slot and inner keyline. The texture multiplies at 90%, at the mock's scale of one texture pixel to 1.34 pt, from the plate's corner (`PlatePaper`), over stock #F1EBDD, which lands on the mock's (222,209,181), measured on the rendered page. It isn't eased for linen, since a plate sits on the page, so the light appearance has the same paper and the same grain; the paper card is unchanged. Only the ink is used on it: the secondary ink misses 4.5:1 against the fibre's worst pixel, so the credit, "3 of 160 sewn" and "Ready offline" are in the full ink. The keyline is the mock's: opaque #AFAF99, one CSS pixel, outside the picture, so it holds over dark trees.
- **The credit is printed under the picture, right-aligned, as a plate's caption**, which costs about 14 pt the mock doesn't have, since the mock leaves the credit out and the issue asks for it. VoiceOver hears it once, with the picture: "Painted plate, AI illustration" on a set's page, after the set's name; on the Atlas's tile, one sentence that leads with the name and ends with the plate.
- **The download control sits under a plate, on the page.** It's something you operate, so it's plain iOS, and on the stock it would have needed its own ink. A set's page drops "Ready offline", as the mock does; the Atlas's tile keeps it, with the birder's progress apart from it (#118). The percentage beside the count goes: the mock has none. The downloads list's small covers are plates too, with their credit.
- **A set's page has no large title and no search bar until you pull down**, as the mock. The plate names the set, and the bar does once the plate has scrolled away. Search stays, pulled down as iOS does it. Rows drop the scientific name and the chevron, as the mock does; search still finds a scientific name.
- **"Yours, seen once"** on a sewn row, as the mock and as VoiceOver already says "seen 9 times" of a patch: in the app's count, a sighting is "seen" whether it was Heard or Seen. Issue story 47 says nothing about being seen once on a *patch's* line, which stays as it was.
- **The silhouette's colour is the secondary ink itself**, which is what the mock's measures (#A9BFBA). The glyph keeps its 0.75. Under Increase Contrast the token's own variant applies. Row pictures are 63 pt here against 67 elsewhere: the mock's tile is 138 of 900.
- **`ThreadProgress(onPaper:)` prints the mock's thread.** Inside the card stock the scheme is light, but the light tints were deepened for words on linen (Citrus's is #454B18). A stitch on paper needs 3:1, not 4.5, so `PatchEdition.paperThread` keeps the issue's values, which for Citrus is the mock's #5C6420, and `plateStock` measures each of them on the fibre's worst pixel. Increase Contrast takes the deeper tint.
- **The map moved to `MKMapView`.** SwiftUI's `Map` has no clustering in the iOS 27 SDK, and the issue asks for MapKit's. A marker is a plain bitmap drawn once per painting, edition and size from `PatchTextures`' bitmap, through the two-at-a-time queue, with no shader.
- **A marker's picture is a sublayer of a view of a fixed size, never `MKAnnotationView.image`.** Setting `image`, or nilling it on reuse, reports new metrics, and MapKit clusters again, which makes more views and sets more pictures. At 5,000 sightings, a cluster's tap and a pan kept the main thread busy for over three and a half minutes (found in review). `testTheMapAnswersAtFiveThousandSightings` is the guard, and thousands of views waiting on one painting wait on one decode.
- **Past 500 sightings, VoiceOver's elements on the map are the patches and clusters on screen.** MapKit makes a view for every annotation in the visible area, and its own `accessibilityElements` compares each with all the others on every question VoiceOver or a UI test asks: at 5,000, one answer ran tens of seconds at 100%. That list keeps the map's own controls, the locate button, the compass and Legal, and leaves out its street and place labels; the sighting's place is in words under the map.
- **A map opens at once and fills in twenty goes, the opening sighting first.** Sampled at 20,000 sightings across 30 km, nearly all the CPU is MapKit's `addAnnotationView`, about a millisecond for each annotation in the visible area, clustered or not. Building markers off the main thread wouldn't touch it, since our part is small, and no cheap change removes it: it's the price of MapKit's clustering, which the issue asks for. Batching keeps the screen answering: one go froze it for about 18 s, and twenty take about 26 s of CPU in all, with a second or so between chances to answer. Smaller batches cost more, since each one clusters again (46 s in hundreds).
- **The sighting the map opened on stays out of every cluster**, at `required` priority with nothing colliding with it, so the map always shows the bird its callout names and a cluster at that spot stays. Its identifier is its own, since a view's clustering doesn't change with its annotation. It carries a "+8" when others share its spot, and a tap on it lists them. A patch you chose that a zoom then gathers into a cluster puts its callout away.
- **Which sighting is chosen is ours rather than MapKit's selection**, and the callout stays under the map. A run's birds share one fix, so no zoom would part them: a tap on a cluster tighter than 50 m lists its sightings under the map, each with Open, and a wider one zooms. `SightingPin` keeps its name out of the annotation's `title`, because with a title MapKit drew a small callout over the patch even with `canShowCallout` off.
- **An empty answer from the geocoder is no name** (`PlaceNames.usableName`), so a place keeps its coordinate rather than reading "Near".
- **A record's lead**: the attached photo, else the glasses frame with its caption, else the patch on felt, which opens the patch's page. With an attached photo at the head, "Your photos" holds its words and Remove rather than showing it twice, and the glasses snapshot moves there, so nothing goes from view. "Evidence" and its "Photo attached" line moved into Identification. The reference photo joins "About this bird", so the page has one section fewer. Its tint is the patch's edition, as a patch's page is.
- **F12's other half is the slot.** `AttributedImage` now takes the photo's own shape, read from the file's header once and cached so the page lays out right before the picture decodes; a slot stops at 3:4 and at 2.4:1. All 160 first photos fit uncropped (`everyFirstPhotoFits`). The `aspectRatio` parameter is gone: nothing passed it.
- **The set page's sentence with no descriptor** says it can download once the list of sets comes through, since no Download is offered yet.

## The run's list holds still around a touch (issue #150, the maintainer's decision, after slice 5) — built 2026-11-06, a VoiceOver check on a phone pending

A live list re-sorts as birds call, and since a row's Sew it and Add act at once, a row moving under a thumb adds the wrong bird. Over the clip the order changed in 11 of 13 five-second samples.

- **The rule** is `HeldListOrder`, a pure value: the order freezes when a finger comes down on the rows, while the page scrolls or decelerates, or while VoiceOver's focus is on a row, and moves about 1.5 s after the last of those ends, with the usual slide. A second touch inside the wait holds the same order and starts the wait again. Reduce Motion gets the same order with no new motion, since the existing animation is already off for it.
- **A bird first heard during a hold goes at the bottom.** Held back, a new patch would stay hidden for as long as a finger or VoiceOver's focus stayed, and seeing it at once is the point of a hunt. At the bottom it moves no row, only the "Possible matches" line. It keeps that place until the hold ends, then sorts in like any other.
- **Only the order holds.** "Calling now", counts and pills come from the coordinator on each draw. The raised row and the outline follow the bird wherever its row is held.
- **What else would move a row is held too.** The summary line and the place line's age are frozen during a hold, since either can wrap. Each row keeps room for its "Calling now" line, so a bird that starts or stops calling doesn't change its row's height.
- **Not held:** a notice that the glasses have paused or the run has an error, and the place line first appearing or changing its name. Those say the run itself has changed, and could push the rows down a touch from under a finger. They're rare, and hiding them for a touch's length was the worse trade.
- **The finger** is a `UIGestureRecognizer` (`FingerDown`) that prevents nothing and delays nothing, so buttons and the scroll view keep every touch. Scrolling is `onScrollPhaseChange`, while `.interacting` or `.decelerating`: re-tapping the tab scrolls to the top, and with the list already there that never reported its end. VoiceOver turned off with its focus on a row is checked at each tick, since nothing else says that focus has gone.
- **Leaving lets go.** A flick still decelerating when another tab opens never reports idle, and the list stayed frozen until the next scroll. Leaving the screen, the phone going to the background and a sheet or the reveal covering the list now let go of every holder. `HeldListOrder` also has a last resort for a finger or scroll that never reports its end, 20 s after its last report at most every 5 s. A finger that truly stays still for 20 s without a tremor would see the list move: long enough that it's only ever the net. VoiceOver's focus isn't subject to it.
- **A long toast at the largest text** stacks its words over a whole Undo, in a rounded rectangle, since slice 5's named toast no longer fit beside it.
- **A launch argument can't hold an apostrophe**: a `-fakeRunBirds` with "Anna's Hummingbird" in it came up with none of its flags.


## Guilds: finds open, the page on felt, the tab across Guilds (issue #150, slice 7) — built 2026-11-06

The issue's "Guilds" section, with what it left open:

- **A merged row never says more of where the bird was than the copy that says least.** A find's copies can differ between Guilds: a card shared by hand to one with its place left out, and the automatic find in another. The merged entry drops the place if any copy dropped it, and says a secret spot if any copy does. It keeps a card, a mark and a new patch from any copy.
- **The merged feed stops at a horizon.** Merging by time alone would let a page read later put a find above what's been read. So it shows only what's newer than the newest last-read find of any Guild with unread pages, and "More finds" reads from that Guild, whose next item can't be older than anyone's. Nothing lands in the middle. A Guild whose read pages hold only blocked accounts' finds has nothing to compare, so it's read first.
- **Reading a first page replaces what was read.** `.task` runs again on the way back from a find, which didn't matter when a row opened nothing, and would now throw away the pages under the row. `CircleStore.isFeedStale` reads again only what hasn't been read this launch, or after a minute if the birder hasn't gone past any Guild's first page, since a re-read can also move the horizon. Pull to refresh always reads.
- **What arrives by itself never takes rows away.** A push, or the birder's own share or block, lays the newest finds over what's been read (`CircleStore.refreshFeed`): what's older than they reach stays, with the cursor to read on from, so the horizon doesn't jump. It reads on, up to five pages, if the first holds nothing already read. If those still don't come down as far as what's been read, there may be finds between, a marked one among them, so they replace the rows and read on from their cursor: no gap, and "More finds" reaches everything. Pages with nothing to show (the server leaves a blocked account's finds out after it pages) are read on through, up to twenty, and a read that finds nothing it can show leaves the rows; "No finds yet" shows only when there's nothing further to read. A pull, and an unblock (which brings rows back on pages already read), start again from the first page.
- **A row holds still when finds land above it.** `.scrollPosition` on the page and the tab holds the reader's row, by `.scrollTargetLayout` on the finds. Further down, the system already holds the rows; without this, the feed's first row slid down by a new row's height, 191 pt, under a finger. At the very top of the page nothing is held, whatever came before (`holdsTheRowBeingRead`): a find lands in sight, and the header's controls stay put.
- **Finds come back from Compare where they were.** Compare takes at least a screenful, since the picker is on screen to be tapped, so a short section doesn't pull the page back. A Guild's page and the tab keep their foot under a find's page as the board does (`keepsItsFootUnderAPage`).
- **An open find's page keeps its find** through a read that leaves it out (`holdOpen`), and goes only on a block or when its Guilds have gone, so "This find isn't here any more" is never said of a find that's only off the pages. Opened from a Guild, it shows that Guild's copy, so its marks match the row it came from. From the tab, it's the merged entry the tab's row showed.
- **A line of repeats needs two.** One repeat stays its own quiet row, with its reactions. "Within a day" is counted from the line's newest find, and anything led or marked ends the line. It reads "3 more sightings from Lee Birder", since a line can mix Heard and Seen, and hold one bird twice.
- **"First for the Guild" on a merged row may be true of one of its Guilds.** It keeps the mark's kept words, rather than naming the Guild, and a marked find never collapses.
- **Blocked members leave a page's faces and Compare too**, as their finds do. The Info sheet still lists them, marked, because that's where they're unblocked.
- **A thread's memory now names its Guild** (a small change to slice 6's `ThreadProgress`). A monthly goal's id is `monthly-<yyyy>-<mm>`, the same in every Guild, so a page opened after another Guild's sewed from that Guild's count, with its haptics. `FeelUITests`' sewing test had been passing on this: it now sees a real rise, with `-fakeGoalRises`.
- **A row's headline uses the first name, as the reference does** ("Maya sewed a new patch"). VoiceOver and the find's page keep the full name.
- **A CDN thumbnail is used only if its key names the find's own edition.** Both are the finder's phone's words. The page shows the same picture as the row, so a zoom doesn't change patch on the way.
- **The friend's paper card has no history.** `FriendPaperCard` shares the story and biology with `PaperCardView` and leaves out the count, the sightings and "All sightings", since those belong to the card's owner.
- **The emblem's disc matches the patches.** Its ring is the page's colour and vanishes on felt, so it's drawn at 112 with -5 padding and the disc reads at 102, as the mock has them. `GuildEmblem` is unchanged.
- **A reaction's failure is said under its own pills**, not at the end of the column, which is usually off screen.
- **A member of one Guild keeps "Join with an invite"**, at the foot of the page the tab opens on. Signed out, the join screen shows Featherguild's emblem until the invitation says which Guild, so nothing changes at sign-in.
- **The Invite pill and the sizes follow the reference at 1.34 where the tokens allow:** the name is `JournalFont.title` (largeTitle measured 18 % wide of the mock), the goal `JournalFont.heading`, a bird's name `JournalFont.section`. Reaction labels are `.subheadline`, as the mock's. The pills are 44 pt tall against the mock's 39, as the issue requires. "Let's go together" wraps at default text, since the issue's full names win over the mock's "Let's go".
- **The reference's order isn't kept in the fixture.** Its Maya, "3 days ago", sits above Jo, "yesterday", which a feed sorted by time can't do. `-fakeCircles reference` makes Jo's 4 days ago.
- **Compare keeps its screen behind the picker**, with its friends on a raised panel rather than felt, since they're rows to operate (slice 8 replaces it). The goal's patches are slice 8's too: `CompactGoal` shows thread alone, with the place for them marked.
- **`Landscape.swift` is gone.** Nothing else drew a landscape, so the enum and palette went with `LandscapeArt`. `SetCoverImage` and `SeededRandom`, which were in that file, are in `SetCoverImage.swift`.
- **By hand on a phone:** the reaction's pop and haptic, the zoom from a row's patch, and the row's patches at 120 Hz.

## Patches only on the patches: rows and the Atlas use photos (issue #150, the maintainer, 2026-11-06)

The maintainer: "patches should only be on the patches"; "the sightings should use user photos by default, and the atlas photo otherwise. species should always use the atlas photo. in the atlas, i noticed that patches show up there too, and that is incorrect because it removes the scientific nature of understanding what birds look like and the fact that the atlas is like a guidebook".

This overrides issue #150's V2, A2 and stories 44 to 46, 54 and 57, and the patch row in `4-set-page.png`, and slice 5's "Rows' pictures" and "The profile" above.

- **Sightings rows** have your own photo, attached then glasses, else the Atlas's reference photo. "Reference photo" is the caption that was there already, so it can't read as yours.
- **Species rows** always have the reference photo.
- **The Atlas has no patches in it.** A set's row for a bird you've found has the reference photo, and keeps "Yours, seen once". An unfound bird keeps its silhouette or the glyph. Search rows have the reference photo.
- **`BirdProfileView`**: the names run straight into the reference photo, in the same place for every bird, found or not. Under its credit come your sightings and, if you have it, your patch as a link in words, "Your patch, Los Angeles", opening its page; with several it's your newest sighting's, and the others are a tap on from the sightings. Then the story on card stock. With a `match` it's as before. Above the photo, the links pushed it about 60% down the screen at the largest text.
- **`RowPictureSource` has no patch case**, so nothing using `RowPicture` can show one. `RowPatch` stays for the places that keep their patches: a run's rows and a Guild goal's month of patches.
- **What keeps its patches, confirmed by the maintainer:** a run's rows, Listen's latest patches, a sighting's own page, map pins, Guilds, Compare and goals, "because it's the mark of the bird you found. the issue was wanting users to understand what the bird actually looks like."
- **The reasoning:** a patch marks a bird you found, and a photo teaches what the bird looks like, so the places you read to learn a bird show the photo.



## Guild data, the app half: Compare as boards, a goal's patches, the goal on Listen, friends around the app (issue #150, slice 8) — built 2026-11-06

The issue's "Compare", "Goal", "Friends around the app" and "The goal on Listen", with what they left open:
