- **What a tile shows.** Where the friend has the bird (their board and "You both have"), their patch in `editions[0]`. Under "Only you", your own, the earliest sewn, for symmetry. No edition, or a word the server passed on that names no set, is the plain blank with the bird's name on its tag, never another edition's patch.
- **Where a tap goes.** A patch you hold only yourself opens your patch's page, growing out of its tile. Any other patch in Compare, and any patch on a goal, opens the bird in the Atlas: for a friend's it's how to go and find it, and a goal's patches name no finder, so there's no find to open. "+N" opens a new page, every patch of that goal as a board, since nothing else could reach them.
- **Heard or Seen is under every patch, unlike the mock.** The mock captions only the first board, but the issue and story 87 keep it, in a line short enough to stay on one at the small boards' size: "Seen by both", "You heard, Maya saw", "Seen by you". VoiceOver has the longer sentence.
- **The tally is one line at the page's foot**, in words rather than the old three columns ("You have 5, Maya has 5, and you both have 2."). The mock doesn't have it and opens on Maya's board, and the issue keeps it, so it goes where the mock's page ends.
- **Names never come from the server, on Compare or a goal.** The packs and sets, then BirdNET's own list, read once and only when a pack misses, and on Compare the words on your own sightings. Every bird a real phone can find is in BirdNET's list, so what's left is a hand-built client or a newer model's bird: "A bird this iPhone can't name", which goes last and opens nothing, since a profile would print the sender's words as its heading with nobody beside them to report. A find's way to the Atlas follows the same order, and has no link without a name. A feed row and a find's page keep the finder's words for now, because each names the finder and can be reported. The lens's friend banner also shows a push's words: it names the friend, but can't be reported from the glasses, and that's left open.
- **A goal's row runs newest first.** The server counts earliest first, and with "+N" a new bird would never reach the row. "+N" makes the row add up to the larger of the list and the count, so it still adds up if the two disagree. With seven places and twelve birds that's six patches and "+6", where the mock draws seven and leaves the count to the tally: the issue's "+N past what fits" wins.
- **No tag on the small patches.** At 45 pt a paper tag can't be read, as on a row (`RowPatch`). VoiceOver names the bird, and the page it opens does.
- **The goal on Listen is the Guild fewest birds from a monthly goal that's running**, the first listed of a tie. A goal already reached isn't being chased, so it says nothing. Goals can come from the cache, so "running" is judged by the dates. It's read when Listen appears: the list once a launch, then each Guild's goals if they're over five minutes old. Nothing for someone signed out. The line opens the Guild on the Guilds tab, where a Guild lives, rather than pushing it onto Listen.
- **The friend cache is one read per friend, per session, one at a time.** A set's page or a paper card asks once per screen, never per row, and `friendsBySpecies` makes a row's lookup one dictionary read. Nothing reads it at launch or for Listen. A failure at any step stops the sweep and leaves the rest for the next screen, so no connection costs one call, not one per Guild and friend; not found counts as read. Compare's own screen still reads its friend for itself, once as it opens and on a pull. An unblock can be read again. A friend's birds count the Guilds the two share, so when one of those goes (left here, gone from the list, or a member lost from its detail), what was read of its members goes at once and the next screen reads them again, even if another Guild keeps them friends. An answer already on its way then is asked for again rather than kept.
- **A set row's "you don't" is by species, as Compare is**: a bird you found in another edition gets no face, though its row here is a silhouette. Story 56 says "birds they've found that I haven't".
- **A paper card reads the friends when it's turned over**, or shows beside its patch on a phone on its side, not when its page opens, though both faces are built from the start: browsing patches reads nobody's birds. The names sit above the history, so they're in view: a full card cuts the history off, which says there's more.
- **The sweep is the store's, not a screen's.** A screen pushing the next one would cancel a read that one is waiting on, so it runs on its own task, and a screen asking during it waits. Listen's read of the goals is on its own task for the same reason: a cancelled list reads as a failed one.
- **By hand on a phone:** the board's press and the grow from a tile, and the goal row's small targets, at 45 pt, one-handed.

## The epic's clean-up (issue #150, from the whole-epic review) — built 2026-11-07

No behaviour or look changes, except the two the review asked for: the review sheet's City switch takes the page's tint, and a species' patch tile reads "Los Angeles, seen 8×" with a comma (the short-facts rule above).

- **One Reduce Motion reader.** Every view that reads `accessibilityReduceMotion` reads it through `PatchMotion.reduces(system:)`, so `-reduceMotion` reaches Listen, the run's rows, waveform and accessory, the splash and the Journal too. The name stays: renaming every reader for a word wasn't worth the churn.
- **One stitch tick**, `.stitchTickHaptic(trigger:when:)` in `Feel.swift`, reads the run itself and asks `Haptic.ticks`; the toast, `ThreadProgress` and the stitch-in use it.
- **Shared helpers:** `CircleMember.initials(of:)` and `firstName(of:)` for a feed find's owner and Compare's friend; `String.capitalizedFirst` in `Design/` for the run's recap title too; `PackLibrary.set(id:)` for a set by id; `OutlinedButtonStyle(colour:size: .small)` for a Guild's Invite, which had its own style.
- **`RowPatch` keeps its name** for a run's rows and a Guild goal's patches: both are a row of small patches.
- **Notes for later, left alone:** splitting `CircleStore`, one cap for the small patches' sizes and one way to load them, and the copy namespaces' names.

## Species page: the patch as hero, sightings inline (issue #179, 2026-11-07)

The species page centres its names and patches. One patch is 286 pt, the patch page's picture size; several keep that size in a snapping horizontal carousel, or a vertical reading column at accessibility text sizes. The full-width filled **Open in Atlas** action follows them. All sightings follow on matte paper, newest first, each opening its own record. They use the same scientific-name scope as the page, including older stored labels with a different common name. The separate species **All sightings** page is gone; a patch's paper card and peek keep their patch-specific list. A bird not yet found keeps its photo, set numbers and listening link, with Atlas as its primary action.

## Guild names and icons (issue #181) — 2026-11-07

- A creator can pick a nature mark and muted tone while creating a Guild, then change both its name and icon through Info → Edit Guild. The existing ten marks and six tones remain the only choices. With no choice on creation, no emblem is sent and the server-assigned ID gives the Guild its stable automatic look.
- `emblem: {mark, tone}` is optional on the Guild META item, API responses and app cache. Old Guilds retain their hashed look until edited. All Guild surfaces, including invitation previews and Listen's goal, use the stored icon when present. An unknown future mark or tone safely uses the hash.
- `PATCH /v1/circles/{id}` accepts a name and/or emblem and returns the updated detail. Only the current creator can edit (403 for other members; 404 for non-members). The DynamoDB write itself conditions on the creator, so a concurrent handoff cannot let the former creator edit. Name-only edits preserve the emblem.
- Deploy the backend before using the phone's new create/edit controls. This change needs no stack migration and does not deploy infrastructure.
## A launch shows its real loading work (issue #175, 2026-11-07)

This overrides the earlier first-install-only splash and synchronous cover decode decisions. `AppSurface` first draws the launch screen's paper and finch, and holds that surface only until the stores are ready. Work taking more than 300 ms gains an accessible "Opening your journal…" line and a system progress indicator; there is no fixed minimum hold or decorative animation, including with Reduce Motion. A failed album read keeps the gate with Try again, rather than showing an empty collection.

- The app creates stable store identities at initialization. The bundled and downloaded SQLite packs, cached index and bundled set JSON are read in a worker; the initial album fetch uses a worker-owned SwiftData context and returns only Sendable entries and a finished collection. A save during that read invalidates its snapshot. Later user edits retain the existing immediate collection update semantics.
- The remembered place's cover is decoded in a worker before Listen appears. An index read whose effective collection sets did not change no longer fetches the album a second time.
- Cold-launch URLs are received by the always-mounted loading surface, so a widget link or Guild invite is retained while the root is absent.
- `Shader.compile(as: .layerEffect)` precompiles the patch's stitchable shader asynchronously alongside store loading. The widget follows after readiness, coalesces changes for two seconds at utility priority and encodes PNGs in a worker. SwiftUI `ImageRenderer` itself still requires the main actor; that render has not been moved off it.
- The SwiftData container/migration, wearable configuration and small Guild/outbox file reads remain in initialization. Launch signposts include those intervals as well as store work, surface appearance and the root becoming interactive, so phone traces can identify the next measured bottleneck. Downloaded packs are still opened eagerly together, on the worker.
- Device before/after timings are pending an unlocked phone; see `docs/screenshots/175/README.md`. Simulator results must not be described as phone results.
